  • No unwrap in the TCB on untrusted input. Deserialization of Message/BrokerRequest returns Result<_, SbxError>; malformed bytes from a renderer must never panic the browser (Phase 13 fuzz gate).

11.1 sbx-core — OS-free shared types (TCB-safe, no syscalls)

/// Stable per-process identity. Allocated by the browser; u32 monotonic, never reused
/// within a session (avoids ABA on crash+restart). Copy: pure data, no authority.
pub struct ProcessId(pub u32);

/// Demux key WITHIN one MessagePipe: many logical frames over one socket (Phase 10).
/// RouteId is namespaced PER pipe — same numeric value on two pipes is unrelated.
pub struct RouteId(pub u32);

/// Kind of child. Drives the default CapabilitySet profile + sandbox Policy (Phase 8).
pub enum ProcessType { Browser, Renderer, Gpu, Network, Utility, Storage }

/// Coarse trust class. Browser = the only Privileged process (the TCB); all children
/// are Sandboxed. Used as an assertion gate, NOT the fine-grained authority model.
pub enum ProcessRole { Privileged, Sandboxed }

/// Security principal for isolation: scheme + eTLD+1 (Phase 9). String form e.g.
/// "https://example.com". Equality of SiteId == same isolation principal.
pub struct SiteId(pub String);

/// A group of SiteInstances that may script each other (same browsing context group).
pub struct BrowsingInstanceId(pub u32);

/// Local name for a Handle in a Message's handle table (index discipline, Phase 2).
pub struct HandleId(pub u32);

/// One least-privilege grant. The ATOM of authority. Each variant is a narrow,
/// self-describing right the broker can validate a BrokerOp against.
pub enum Capability {
    OpenFile { path_glob: String, mode: AccessMode }, // glob, NOT arbitrary path
    ConnectSocket { host: String, port: u16 },        // exact endpoint
    SharedMemory { max_bytes: u64 },                  // cap on memfd size
    ReadClipboard,
    GpuChannel,
    // ... least-privilege grants; closed enum — adding authority is a code change
}

/// Access mode inside Capability::OpenFile. ReadOnly is the renderer default.
pub enum AccessMode { ReadOnly, ReadWrite }

(sbx-core types (continued))

/// The total authority of ONE process. Set semantics (no duplicates). Immutable after
/// process launch in the steady state; grant/revoke (Phase 8) mutate the browser-side
/// copy under the event loop, never the child's. The child cannot grow its own set.
pub struct CapabilitySet(/* HashSet<Capability>, per-ProcessId, browser-owned */);

impl CapabilitySet {
    pub fn allows(&self, op: &BrokerOp) -> bool;  // the central authority predicate
}

/// Flat error taxonomy crossing every crate. Maps to CLI exit codes + metrics labels.
pub enum SbxError {
    Ipc,           // framing/socket/EOF on a MessagePipe
    Sandbox,       // seccomp/namespace/pivot_root setup failed
    PolicyDenied,  // broker refused: op not in caller CapabilitySet
    SpawnFailed,   // clone/exec/zygote-fork failed
    ChildCrashed,  // observed via SIGCHLD / IPC disconnect
    BadHandle,     // missing/duplicate/oversized handle table entry
}

Security semantics: Capability/CapabilitySet are descriptions of authority, evaluated only inside the broker; possessing a CapabilitySet value in a child grants nothing (the child's copy is advisory). SiteId equality is the site-isolation principal — a bug equating two distinct sites collapses isolation, so derivation (Phase 9) is the trust-sensitive path.

11.2 sbx-ipc — core wire types (Handle, MessageHeader, Message)

/// Owned OS file descriptor. SINGLE-OWNER, close-on-drop (RAII). Moved (never copied)
/// in Rust; the underlying fd is DUPLICATED into the peer via SCM_RIGHTS on send.
/// THE capability-passing primitive: a Handle IS authority to use whatever it refers to.
pub struct Handle { /* RawFd; impl Drop { close } */ }
impl Handle {
    pub fn from_raw(fd: RawFd) -> Self;     // takes ownership
    pub fn into_raw(self) -> RawFd;         // relinquishes (no close)
    pub fn try_clone(&self) -> Result<Handle, SbxError>; // dup(2)
}

/// Message kind discriminant on the wire.
pub enum MsgKind { Request, Response, Event, Control }

/// Fixed-size, little-endian wire prefix. Parsed FIRST; bounds payload + handle count.
pub struct MessageHeader {
    pub route: RouteId,     // demux target
    pub kind: MsgKind,
    pub req_id: u64,        // correlates Response to Request
    pub num_handles: u16,   // count of fds expected in the SCM_RIGHTS aux data
}

/// One IPC unit. payload = opaque serialized body; handles = passed fds (ancillary data).
pub struct Message {
    pub header: MessageHeader,
    pub payload: Vec<u8>,
    pub handles: Vec<Handle>,  // len MUST == header.num_handles after recv
}

Wire format (LOCKED):

[ MessageHeader  fixed 24 bytes, little-endian ]
    route:u32@0 | kind:u16@4 | _pad:u16@6 | req_id:u64@8 | payload_len:u32@16 | num_handles:u16@20 | _pad:u16@22
[ payload       payload_len bytes (length-prefixed; bounded by MAX_PAYLOAD) ]
handles travel OUT-OF-BAND in SCM_RIGHTS ancillary data, NOT in payload bytes.
  • payload_len checked vs MAX_PAYLOAD (default 1 MiB) before alloc → no OOM amplification from a malicious renderer. Larger transfers use sbx-shm, not payload.
  • On recv, the count of received fds is reconciled with num_handles; mismatch ⇒ SbxError::BadHandle, message dropped. Extra/unexpected fds are closed immediately (no fd leak / smuggling).

11.2b sbx-ipc — typed pipe, endpoint, and concurrency contract

/// Typed request/response contract bound to an Endpoint. Codegen-style: each Interface
/// defines msg ids + (de)serialization, giving a Mojo-like statically-typed pipe.
pub trait Interface {
    const NAME: &'static str;
    fn dispatch(&mut self, msg: Message) -> Result<Option<Message>, SbxError>;
}

/// A typed end of a message pipe speaking Interface T. !Sync: owned by one task/thread.
pub struct Endpoint<T: Interface> { /* MessagePipe + PhantomData<T> */ }
impl<T: Interface> Endpoint<T> {
    pub fn send_request(&mut self, m: Message) -> Result<u64, SbxError>; // returns req_id
}

/// Bidirectional UDS-backed channel (one socketpair end). !Sync, Send. Blocking + async
/// modes; carries Messages incl. handles. The browser holds one MessagePipe per child.
pub struct MessagePipe { /* OwnedFd of a SOCK_SEQPACKET unix socket */ }
impl MessagePipe {
    pub fn send(&mut self, m: Message) -> Result<(), SbxError>;
    pub fn recv(&mut self) -> Result<Message, SbxError>;
}

/// Connected pair (socketpair(2)): one end stays in the browser, the other is passed to
/// the child as fd 3 at launch (Phase 3). Both ends Send-movable across fork.
pub fn pair() -> (MessagePipe, MessagePipe);

Concurrency: MessagePipe/Endpoint<T> are !Sync; the browser event loop owns each pipe single-threaded (poll/epoll readiness loop). Cross-thread sharing requires a Mutex<MessagePipe> — never two threads writing one socket (interleaved frames). SOCK_SEQPACKET gives message-boundary preservation so a partial read never splices two Messages.

11.3 sbx-shm — shared memory (large / zero-copy transfers)

pub enum Prot { ReadOnly, ReadWrite }

/// A memfd-backed shared buffer. handle is the passable fd (SCM_RIGHTS) to the memfd;
/// len is the sealed size. Ownership: the Handle owns the fd (close-on-drop); the
/// backing pages are reclaimed when ALL holders unmap + close.
pub struct SharedBuffer {
    pub handle: Handle,
    pub len: usize,
}
impl SharedBuffer {
    pub fn create(len: usize) -> Result<Self, SbxError>;       // memfd_create + ftruncate
    pub fn seal(&self, flags: SealFlags) -> Result<(), SbxError>; // F_SEAL_SHRINK|GROW|WRITE
    pub fn map(&self, prot: Prot) -> Result<Mapping, SbxError>;
}

/// Seal flags applied via fcntl(F_ADD_SEALS). Producer seals WRITE+GROW+SHRINK before
/// passing a ReadOnly view → receiver cannot be tricked by a buffer that resizes mid-read.
pub struct SealFlags(/* bitset: Write|Grow|Shrink|FutureWrite */);

/// An mmap'd region. Deref<Target=[u8]> (ro) / DerefMut (rw). munmap on Drop. !Sync.
pub struct Mapping { /* ptr + len + prot */ }

/// Single-producer single-consumer ring over a SharedBuffer. Lock-free via atomic
/// head/tail in the buffer header; for bulk/streaming transfers (e.g. frame data).
pub struct ShmRing { /* SharedBuffer + header offsets */ }
impl ShmRing {
    pub fn push(&mut self, bytes: &[u8]) -> Result<(), SbxError>; // producer side
    pub fn pop(&mut self, out: &mut Vec<u8>) -> Result<bool, SbxError>; // consumer side
}

Security semantics: shared memory is the one place an untrusted child writes bytes the browser reads. Mitigations: (1) sender SEALS the buffer ReadOnly before passing the read view → no TOCTOU resize; (2) the consumer treats ring head/tail as untrusted, bounds-checks every offset against len (a malicious producer cannot drive an OOB read/write); (3) SharedMemory { max_bytes } capability caps total mapped size per child (memory-pressure DoS bound, Phase 12). Mappings are !Sync; concurrent access within a process needs external sync — the ShmRing SPSC contract assumes exactly one producer + one consumer.

11.4 sbx-sandbox — OS confinement (Linux primary)

/// Per-syscall outcome in a seccomp filter. KillProcess for any syscall the policy
/// never expects (fail-closed); Errno to spoof a benign failure; Trap → SIGSYS for
/// broker-redirectable calls; Log for dev-mode auditing.
pub enum Disposition { Allow, Errno(i32), Trap, KillProcess, Log }

/// One rule: syscall nr + outcome + optional argument constraint (e.g. only O_RDONLY
/// open flags, only AF_UNIX socket family). arg_filter compiles to extra BPF comparisons.
pub struct SyscallRule {
    pub nr: i64,
    pub disposition: Disposition,
    pub arg_filter: Option<ArgFilter>,
}

/// Constraint over seccomp_data.args[0..6] (index, op, value). Limited to scalar compares
/// (BPF cannot deref pointers → cannot inspect strings; path checks belong to the broker).
pub struct ArgFilter { pub index: u8, pub op: CmpOp, pub value: u64 }
pub enum CmpOp { Eq, Ne, Ge, Le, MaskedEq }

/// Compiles SyscallRule[] → a classic-BPF program over `struct seccomp_data
/// { nr, arch, instruction_pointer, args[6] }`. FIRST instruction MUST validate `arch`
/// (reject anything != AUDIT_ARCH_X86_64) to block the x32/ia32 ABI bypass.
pub struct SeccompFilter { /* Vec<sock_filter> */ }
impl SeccompFilter {
    pub fn from_rules(rules: &[SyscallRule]) -> Result<Self, SbxError>;
    pub fn install(&self) -> Result<(), SbxError>; // requires no_new_privs already set
}

/// clone(2) namespace flag set. user ns FIRST (unprivileged → fake root) enables the rest.
pub struct Namespaces { /* CLONE_NEWUSER|NEWPID|NEWNET|NEWNS|NEWIPC|NEWUTS bitset */ }

/// Resource ceilings (setrlimit) — DoS bound + escape-surface reduction.
pub struct Rlimits { pub as_bytes: u64, pub nofile: u64, pub nproc: u64, pub cpu_secs: u64 }

/// The complete confinement spec for one child. Built per ProcessType (Phase 8).
pub struct Policy {
    pub namespaces: Namespaces,
    pub seccomp: SeccompFilter,
    pub rlimits: Rlimits,
    pub root: PathBuf,        // pivot_root target: a minimal, mostly-read-only rootfs
    pub drop_caps: bool,      // drop ALL capabilities (capset to empty)
    pub no_new_privs: bool,   // PR_SET_NO_NEW_PRIVS — MUST be true before seccomp install
}

/// Backend-agnostic confinement entrypoint. apply() runs in the CHILD after fork/clone,
/// before exec'ing/running untrusted code. Ordering is load-bearing (see Phase 4/5).
pub trait Sandbox { fn apply(&self, policy: &Policy) -> Result<(), SbxError>; }

/// The PRIMARY backend. macOS (Seatbelt) + Windows (job/restricted-token/AppContainer)
/// are PhaseDeferred stubs returning SbxError::Sandbox.
pub struct LinuxSandbox;

Apply ordering (invariant, enforced by LinuxSandbox::apply): unshare/clone namespaces → pivot_root + remount ro → set Rlimits → drop all caps → PR_SET_NO_NEW_PRIVS → install SeccompFilter. Seccomp LAST and AFTER no_new_privs so an unprivileged process may install it and so setup syscalls aren't filtered. arch check is BPF instruction #0 — a filter that omits it is rejected at from_rules.

Security semantics: Policy is the OS layer of defense in depth layer 1 (seccomp + namespaces); the broker (11.6) is layer 2. A single layer's bug must not yield escape — e.g. even if a forbidden syscall slips past seccomp, no network namespace means no socket to reach.

11.5 sbx-zygote — pre-forked template for fast spawn

/// A pre-forked, pre-sandboxed parent holding warm COW pages (runtime + common libs).
/// Owns a fork-server socket; lives for the session. Spawning a renderer = fork from here
/// (cheap, COW-shared) rather than a fresh exec.
pub struct Zygote { /* fork-server MessagePipe + base Policy */ }

/// Request to mint a child. process_type selects the FINAL (tightened) policy; channel is
/// the IPC pipe end (Handle) the new child will own; policy is the to-be-sealed confinement.
pub struct ForkRequest {
    pub process_type: ProcessType,
    pub policy: Policy,
    pub channel: Handle,
}

/// Runs inside the zygote; services ForkRequests by fork() then SEALING (tightening) the
/// seccomp policy in the child before it touches untrusted work.
pub struct ForkServer;
impl ForkServer {
    pub fn serve(req: ForkRequest) -> Result<ProcessId, SbxError>; // forks; child seals + runs
}

Security/concurrency semantics: the zygote holds a loose base sandbox (enough to fork); each forked child must TIGHTEN (seal) seccomp before running renderer code — an unsealed child is a hole. The zygote is single-threaded around fork() (forking a multithreaded process risks lock-state corruption). COW means the child shares read-only pages but any write triggers a private copy → no cross-child leakage through shared pages.

11.6 sbx-broker — privileged resource broker (defense layer 2)

/// What a child is asking the broker to do on its behalf. Validated against the caller's
/// CapabilitySet before ANY privileged action. Closed enum = closed authority surface.
pub enum BrokerOp {
    OpenFile { path: String, mode: AccessMode },
    Connect  { host: String, port: u16 },
    MapShm   { bytes: u64 },
    // ...
}

/// A request as received over IPC. `from` is the AUTHENTICATED ProcessId (the browser
/// stamps it from the receiving pipe — NOT taken from renderer-supplied bytes; a renderer
/// cannot impersonate another ProcessId).
pub struct BrokerRequest { pub from: ProcessId, pub op: BrokerOp }

/// Result. Handle = the brokered fd (passed back via SCM_RIGHTS). Denied carries a reason
/// string for audit (never echoes secret state). Deny-by-default: unmatched op → Denied.
pub enum BrokerResponse {
    Handle(Handle),
    Denied(String),
    Error(SbxError),
}

/// Maps ProcessId → CapabilitySet; the single chokepoint that decides allow/deny. Owned
/// by the browser, mutated only under the event loop. Every BrokerOp passes through here.
pub struct PolicyEngine { /* HashMap<ProcessId, CapabilitySet> */ }
impl PolicyEngine {
    pub fn validate(&self, req: &BrokerRequest) -> Result<(), SbxError>; // Err(PolicyDenied)
}

pub trait Broker { fn service(&mut self, req: BrokerRequest) -> BrokerResponse; }
// Implementors: FileBroker, SocketBroker, ResourceBroker.

Serialization: BrokerRequest is the payload of a Message on the broker RouteId; BrokerResponse::Handle returns its fd in the Message.handles table (out-of-band), the rest in payload. Security: from is server-authenticated; path/host strings are validated by the PolicyEngine against Capability globs/endpoints AFTER canonicalization (defeat ../symlink TOCTOU by opening with O_NOFOLLOW/openat + realpath then re-check). The broker opens the resource and passes only the FD — the renderer never receives a usable path. This is why the renderer can have NET namespace (no sockets) yet still reach an allowed endpoint: via a brokered, pre-validated FD.

11.7 sbx-launcher — spawning + lifecycle

pub struct LaunchOptions {
    pub process_type: ProcessType,
    pub policy: Policy,
    pub use_zygote: bool,   // true → ask ForkServer; false → direct clone/exec
}

/// A live child: its authenticated pid + the browser-side MessagePipe end. Dropping it
/// does NOT kill the child (lifecycle is managed via CrashHandler/ProcessHost).
pub struct ChildProcess { pub pid: ProcessId, pub channel: MessagePipe }

pub struct ProcessLauncher { /* zygote handle OR direct clone/exec path */ }
impl ProcessLauncher {
    /// Builds child cmd+env, sets up the IPC channel (pair()), applies sandbox (in child),
    /// performs the hello handshake, returns the connected ChildProcess.
    pub fn launch(&self, opts: LaunchOptions) -> Result<ChildProcess, SbxError>;
}

Handshake invariant: after fork/exec, child and browser exchange a MsgKind::Control hello on RouteId(0); the child confirms sandbox sealed BEFORE the browser routes any work to it. Missing/late hello within timeout ⇒ SbxError::SpawnFailed and the child is reaped.

11.8 sbx-site — site isolation policy

/// A (site, browsing-instance) pair = the process-assignment principal. id is browser-local.
pub struct SiteInstance {
    pub id: u32,
    pub site: SiteId,                       // scheme + eTLD+1
    pub browsing_instance: BrowsingInstanceId,
}

/// SiteInstances that may script each other (window.open w/o noopener) → may co-locate.
pub struct BrowsingInstance {
    pub id: BrowsingInstanceId,
    pub instances: Vec<SiteInstance>,
}

/// Outcome of asking "what process for this SiteInstance?"
pub enum ProcessReusePolicy {
    ProcessPerSiteInstance,   // the default: 1 SiteInstance → 1 renderer
    ReuseExistingForSite,     // same site already hosted → reuse (under limit)
    LimitReached,             // process-count cap hit → must consolidate
}

/// Derives the SiteId and maps site → process. eTLD+1 via a bundled public-suffix list.
pub struct SiteIsolationPolicy { /* PSL + HashMap<SiteId, ProcessId> */ }
impl SiteIsolationPolicy {
    pub fn site_for(&self, url: &str) -> SiteId;  // trust-sensitive: defines the boundary
}

Security semantics: site_for is the isolation boundary's definition. eTLD+1 via the public-suffix list (e.g. a.example.co.uk + b.example.co.uk → same site example.co.uk; foo.github.io + bar.github.io → DIFFERENT sites because github.io is a public suffix). A PSL error mis-merges or wrongly splits sites → over/under isolation. ProcessReusePolicy::LimitReached bounds total renderers (memory), trading some isolation for resource budget under pressure (Phase 12).

11.9 sbx-browser — orchestrator (the TCB owner)

/// Per-renderer bookkeeping in the browser. Holds the assigned SiteInstance + every
/// RouteId multiplexed onto that renderer's single MessagePipe.
pub struct RenderProcessHost {
    pub pid: ProcessId,
    pub site_instance: SiteInstance,
    pub routes: Vec<RouteId>,
}

/// Registry of ALL child hosts keyed by ProcessId. The single source of truth for which
/// processes exist + their capabilities (cross-refs PolicyEngine).
pub struct ProcessHost { /* HashMap<ProcessId, RenderProcessHost/...> */ }

/// The browser process itself. Owns everything; runs the single-threaded event loop.
pub struct BrowserProcess {
    /* ProcessHost, ProcessLauncher, brokers (PolicyEngine), SiteIsolationPolicy,
       CrashHandler, RouteId router */
}
impl BrowserProcess {
    pub fn navigate(&mut self, tab: u32, url: &str) -> Result<RouteId, SbxError>;
    // site_for(url) → SiteInstance → ProcessReusePolicy → (reuse|launch) → route
}

Concurrency: BrowserProcess is the TCB and runs single-threaded over an epoll loop; all mutation of ProcessHost/PolicyEngine happens here, eliminating data races in the privileged path. I/O fans out to children; no child holds a reference into browser memory.

11.10 sbx-renderer — minimal untrusted child (NOT a web engine)

/// A unit of "content" the renderer hosts; identified by its RouteId on the shared pipe.
pub struct RenderFrame { pub route: RouteId, pub url: String }

/// Sandboxed child. Talks to the broker for all OS resources. Includes a TEST crash hook
/// (force SIGSEGV / forbidden syscall) for blast-radius + red-team tests (Phase 11/13).
/// Explicitly a STAND-IN — real rendering is #260/#305.
pub struct RendererProcess { /* MessagePipe to browser, broker Endpoint, frames */ }

Security semantics: runs at maximal confinement (renderer-baseline seccomp + all namespaces + dropped caps). Any authority it exercises must trace to its CapabilitySet via a brokered FD. A compromise is contained to that set (blast radius = the renderer's capabilities), and the broker re-validates every request so a compromised renderer cannot escalate.

11.11 sbx-crash — crash handling + recovery

/// Minimal crash artifact (NOT a full Breakpad minidump): enough to triage + report.
pub struct Minidump {
    pub pid: ProcessId,
    pub signal: i32,             // e.g. SIGSEGV, SIGSYS (seccomp kill)
    pub registers: Vec<u64>,     // best-effort snapshot
    pub maps: String,            // /proc/<pid>/maps snapshot
}

/// What to do after a child dies.
pub enum RestartPolicy {
    Never,
    Restart { max: u32, window_secs: u32 },  // rate-limited to avoid crash loops
    ReloadTab,                               // re-navigate fresh renderer
}

/// SIGCHLD/exit watcher in the browser. On child death: build Minidump, pick RestartPolicy,
/// emit the "sad tab" signal. A renderer crash MUST NOT take down the browser.
pub struct CrashHandler { /* SIGCHLD self-pipe + per-pid RestartPolicy */ }
impl CrashHandler {
    pub fn on_sigchld(&mut self) -> Vec<(ProcessId, Minidump)>; // reaps, never blocks loop
}

Detection sources: SIGCHLD (process exit), IPC disconnect (pipe EOF on recv), and explicit MsgKind::Control shutdown. Blast-radius invariant: child death is observed, reaped, and surfaced as a "sad tab" with the browser + sibling renderers unaffected; Restart is rate-limited (max/window_secs) so a renderer that crash-loops cannot DoS the browser. A signal == SIGSYS Minidump is the fingerprint of a seccomp KillProcess — a likely sandbox-escape attempt (Phase 13 red-team signal).

12. Phase 0 — Skeleton, workspace, and the "spawn a child, exchange hello over a UDS" smoke test

Foundation phase. No sandbox, no zygote, no brokering yet (those = trait stubs returning Ok(())/PhaseDeferred). Goal: a compiling 12-crate workspace, type shells, green CI, and ONE end-to-end path — sbx browser process forks/execs a child, exchanges a hello Message over a socketpair MessagePipe, child exit(0), parent reaps. Proves the build, the IPC primitive, and process lifecycle before any trust-boundary code lands.

12.1 Objectives

  • Cargo workspace, 12 sbx-* members (section "Workspace Crates"); pinned toolchain; cargo build --workspace green.
  • Type shells only: ProcessId, ProcessType, ProcessRole, Capability, CapabilitySet, SbxError, Message, MessagePipe, Sandbox trait, Policy. No syscall logic (apply() = Ok(()) stub).
  • sbx CLI dispatch skeleton; only run, ipc-ping functional this phase; rest = PhaseDeferred exit code 2.
  • Declare Linux PRIMARY; macOS(Seatbelt)/Windows backends behind cfg + PhaseDeferred stubs — document, don't implement.
  • Infra: CI (fmt → clippy -D warnings → build → test), Makefile, Dockerfile (privileged for later sandbox tests), rust-toolchain.toml, deny.toml.
  • Smoke test: fork/exec child → hello Message exchange over UDS MessagePipe → child exit(0) → parent waitpid reaps. End-to-end skeleton path.

12.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

Theory applied: ADR-01 (Rust for TCB), ADR-02 (workspace crate layering), ADR-03 (Linux primary), ADR-05 (UDS transport). The workspace layout concretizes P6 (Barth et al. 2008 — browser/renderer trust split) by giving each trust-boundary crate its own compilation unit, making the boundary visible in the dependency DAG from phase 0.

File Contents (shells)
Cargo.toml (root) [workspace] members=[12 sbx-*]; resolver="2"; shared [workspace.package] edition=2021, rust-version=1.78
sbx-core/src/lib.rs ProcessId(u32), RouteId(u32), ProcessType{Browser,Renderer,Gpu,Network,Utility,Storage}, ProcessRole{Privileged,Sandboxed}, SiteId(String), BrowsingInstanceId(u32), HandleId(u32), Capability{...}, AccessMode, CapabilitySet, SbxError{Ipc,Sandbox,PolicyDenied,SpawnFailed,ChildCrashed,BadHandle} — all #[derive(Debug,Clone,PartialEq)]; SbxError: std::error::Error
sbx-ipc/src/lib.rs MsgKind enum; MessageHeader{route,kind,req_id,num_handles}; Message{header,payload:Vec<u8>,handles:Vec<Handle>}; Handle(RawFd) shell (no SCM_RIGHTS yet); MessagePipe wrapping UnixStream; pub fn pair()->(MessagePipe,MessagePipe) via socketpair(AF_UNIX,SOCK_SEQPACKET); send(&Message)/recv()->Message with len-prefixed framing (handles ignored this phase)
sbx-shm/src/lib.rs Prot{ReadOnly,ReadWrite}, SharedBuffer, Mapping, ShmRing — empty shells, todo!()-free no-op constructors
sbx-sandbox/src/lib.rs Disposition, SyscallRule, SeccompFilter, Namespaces, Rlimits, Policy, trait Sandbox{fn apply(&self,&Policy)->Result<(),SbxError>}, LinuxSandbox whose apply returns Ok(()); mod macos/mod windows cfg-gated returning Err(SbxError::Sandbox)/PhaseDeferred
sbx-zygote,sbx-broker,sbx-launcher,sbx-site,sbx-browser,sbx-renderer,sbx-crash type shells per spine; constructors + Debug; logic = later phases
sbx-cli/src/main.rs clap subcommand enum (run/spawn/policy/ipc-ping/sandbox-test/crash-test/sites/attack); only run,ipc-ping wired; others eprintln!("PhaseDeferred"); exit(2)
  • Framing: [u32 LE total_len][header bincode/manual][payload]; recv reads exact len; reject total_len > 64 KiB → SbxError::Ipc.
  • Smoke flow (sbx run --smoke / default): parent pair() → fork(); child dup pipe fd to a known fd, execv(self, ["--child"]); child sends Message{kind:Hello,payload=b"hello"}, parent recvs, asserts payload, replies HelloAck; child exit(0); parent waitpid → assert WEXITSTATUS==0.

12.3 Deliverable

  • Compiling 12-crate workspace; cargo build/test --workspace green; clippy -D warnings clean; cargo fmt --check clean.
  • sbx ipc-ping round-trips a Message in-process; sbx run (smoke) forks child + completes hello exchange + clean reap, prints OK pid=<n> rtt=<µs>.
  • Dockerfile builds image with --cap-add notes for later seccomp/namespace tests; make ci reproduces the pipeline locally.

12.4 Acceptance Criteria

# Criterion Measure
A1 Workspace builds cargo build --workspace exit 0, all 12 crates present
A2 Lint gate clippy --all-targets -D warnings exit 0; fmt --check exit 0
A3 Smoke exchange child sends Hello, parent receives byte-identical b"hello"; parent HelloAck received
A4 Clean lifecycle child exit status == 0; parent waitpid reaps, NO zombie (/proc/<pid> gone), no orphan
A5 Framing safety Message > 64 KiB → SbxError::Ipc, no panic, no OOM
A6 Deferred honesty sandbox-test/attack/etc. exit 2 + print PhaseDeferred; macOS/Windows apply returns error, never silently "succeeds"
A7 CI reproducible make ci == GitHub Actions result

12.5 Conformance Fixtures

Fixture Input Expected
fixtures/smoke_hello.txt run smoke OK pid=<n> rtt=<µs>, exit 0
fixtures/msg_roundtrip.json encode→decode Message{Hello,payload=b"hello"} byte-identical roundtrip
fixtures/frame_oversize.bin declared total_len=70000 SbxError::Ipc, no panic
fixtures/frame_truncated.bin header says len N, M<N bytes SbxError::Ipc (short read), no hang
fixtures/frame_zerolen.bin total_len=0 rejected as malformed, no recv busy-loop
fixtures/cli_deferred.txt sbx attack stderr PhaseDeferred, exit 2
fixtures/child_segv.txt (attack-ish) child raises SIGABRT before send parent observes non-zero/signaled exit, reports ChildCrashed, no parent crash

12.6 Pitfalls

  • fd inheritance leak: child inherits ALL parent fds across fork; the other socketpair end + unrelated fds leak in. Root cause: no O_CLOEXEC/no explicit close. Fix: SOCK_CLOEXEC on socketpair, close peer end in each process, dup2 only the kept fd. Debug: ls -l /proc/<child>/fd.
  • fork in multithreaded process: async-signal-unsafe between fork and exec (allocator/locks). Root cause: tokio/threads spawned pre-fork. Fix: keep smoke path single-threaded; only async-signal-safe calls (dup2,execv,_exit) post-fork.
  • SOCK_STREAM vs SEQPACKET: STREAM coalesces/splits → framing bugs; use SOCK_SEQPACKET to preserve message boundaries (also needed for SCM_RIGHTS atomicity in Phase 2).
  • zombie via missed SIGCHLD: parent exits before waitpid → zombie/orphan. Fix: blocking waitpid in smoke; full reaper is Phase 3. Debug: ps -eo stat | grep Z.
  • clippy in CI but not local: pin clippy via rust-toolchain.toml so versions match; otherwise new lints break CI only.
  • execv self-path: re-exec via /proc/self/exe not argv[0] (cwd-relative argv0 breaks); guard infinite re-exec with the --child arg.

12.7 Performance Concerns

  • Smoke not perf-critical but sets baselines: log rtt (hello→ack) target < 200 µs in-host; fork+exec child < 5 ms (real spawn budget tuned with zygote in Phase 6).
  • Avoid per-message heap thrash: pre-size payload Vec; framing buffer reused.
  • Workspace build time: resolver="2", shared deps in [workspace.dependencies] to dedupe; cold cargo build target < 90 s on CI runner.

12.8 Edge Cases

  • fork() returns -1 (EAGAIN/rlimit NPROC) → SbxError::SpawnFailed, no half-spawned state.
  • Child execv fails (ENOENT) → child _exit(127); parent maps to SpawnFailed, not Hello timeout.
  • Parent dies mid-exchange → child recv gets EOF (0 bytes) → child _exit(non-zero), no hang.
  • Empty payload Message (len=0 payload, valid header) → legal, distinct from zero-len frame.
  • Repeated pair() exhausting fds → constructor errors cleanly, no leak.

12.9 Failure Analysis

Failure Detection Containment (this phase)
Child crash pre-send parent recv EOF + waitpid signaled report ChildCrashed; parent survives (proves blast-radius primitive ahead of Phase 11)
Framing corruption length/short-read check SbxError::Ipc, drop, no panic — hardening seam for Phase 1 IPC fuzz
fd exhaustion socketpair/fork errno SpawnFailed, cleanup, no zombie
Deferred subcommand misuse exit-2 contract explicit PhaseDeferred, never a fake success (avoids false security assurance)

12.10 Operational Notes

  • rust-toolchain.toml: channel="1.78.0", components rustfmt,clippy; deny.toml: deny unsafe outside sbx-ipc/sbx-sandbox allowlist, deny yanked/unmaintained advisories.
  • Dockerfile: base debian:stable-slim + rust 1.78; document run flags for later phases (--cap-add=SYS_ADMIN, --security-opt seccomp=unconfined, --privileged only for namespace/seccomp CI) — NOT needed for Phase 0 smoke.
  • make targets: build,test,ci(fmt+clippy+build+test),smoke,docker.
  • Logging: tracing with process_type+pid fields from the start (cross-boundary tracing matures in Phase 12).
  • Platform guard: #[cfg(not(target_os="linux"))] compiles but LinuxSandbox::apply etc. are unavailable; CI sandbox jobs are linux-only; macOS/Windows = build-only smoke.

13. Phase 1 — IPC core (typed message pipe over UNIX domain sockets)

Establishes the sbx-ipc transport: MessagePipe over a single UNIX domain socket, length-prefixed bounds-checked Message framing, req_id request/response correlation, and the Interface/Endpoint<T> typed binding. This is the spine every later phase rides on — it crosses the trust boundary, so it must be correct against a fully untrusted, malicious peer. No handle passing yet (Phase 2 adds SCM_RIGHTS); header.num_handles is wire-reserved and MUST be 0.

13.1 Objectives

  • MessagePipe: bidirectional UDS channel; pair() (socketpair, parent/child) + named-socket connect (late joiners) — same MessagePipe type either way.
  • Wire format: length-prefixed Message{MessageHeader, payload}, bounds-checked on every decode; one frame = one message; never partial-deliver.
  • Correlation: monotonic req_id (u64); request -> response match; out-of-order + concurrent in-flight; async + blocking send/recv.
  • Typed layer: Interface (method -> MsgKind) bound to Endpoint<T>; compile-time-ish contract over the raw byte pipe.
  • Robust disconnect/error semantics: SbxError::Ipc on EOF/ECONNRESET/oversize/short-read; broker never panics on malformed input.

13.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

Theory applied: P5 (Chromium Mojo — typed pipe, message framing, req_id correlation), ADR-05 (UDS + SCM_RIGHTS as the transport), ADR-07 (Interface/Endpoint<T> typing layer), ADR-08 (hand-written fixed-header framing). I1 (trust-boundary parse hardening) and I2 (broker-never-crashes) are the invariants this phase must satisfy: every malformed input from an untrusted peer must return Err(Ipc), never panic.

Module / file Adds
sbx-ipc/src/wire.rs MsgKind (Request/Response/Event/Control); MessageHeader{route,kind,req_id,num_handles,payload_len:u32}; fixed 24-byte header, little-endian; encode()/decode()
sbx-ipc/src/message.rs Message{header,payload:Vec<u8>,handles:Vec<Handle>} (handles empty this phase); Message::request/response/event ctors
sbx-ipc/src/pipe.rs MessagePipe(UnixStream-backed); pair()->socketpair(AF_UNIX,SOCK_SEQPACKET); connect(path)/Listener::bind(path) for named; send(&Message), recv()->Result<Message>; nonblocking + poll-driven async variant
sbx-ipc/src/framing.rs write_frame: [u32 len][24B header][payload]; read_frame: read len -> bounds-check vs MAX_MESSAGE (default 16 MiB) -> read exact -> decode header -> assert payload_len == len-24 & num_handles==0
sbx-ipc/src/endpoint.rs trait Interface{ const NAME:&str; type Request; type Response; }; Endpoint<T>{pipe, next_req_id:AtomicU64, pending:Map<u64,oneshot>}; call(req)->Response (assigns req_id, awaits match), serve(handler) loop
sbx-ipc/src/error.rs maps io::ErrorKind -> SbxError::Ipc; oversize/short/bad-magic -> SbxError::Ipc (never unwrap)
  • Framing algo: SOCK_SEQPACKET gives message boundaries, but DO NOT trust them — still length-prefix and bounds-check (defends SOCK_STREAM fallback + truncation). recv() reads exactly len bytes; len > MAX_MESSAGE -> drop frame + Err(Ipc) (no allocation of attacker-chosen size).
  • Correlation algo: Endpoint::call -> req_id = fetch_add(1), insert oneshot into pending, send; serve/recv-loop dispatches Response by header.req_id, completes the oneshot; unmatched req_id -> log + drop (untrusted peer may forge ids).
  • Async: edge-triggered readiness (epoll/mio); a single recv task demuxes by req_id; senders are Clone via Arc.

13.3 Deliverable

sbx ipc-ping spawns/links two MessagePipe ends; sends N typed Requests, receives matched Responses. cargo test -p sbx-ipc green. Echo + concurrency tests pass. MAX_MESSAGE, header size, LE byte order documented in wire.rs.

13.4 Acceptance Criteria

  • Echo: 10k round-trips, every Response.req_id == Request.req_id, payloads byte-identical, zero loss/reorder corruption.
  • Concurrency: 64 in-flight requests across 8 tasks on one pipe -> all correlate correctly; no req_id collision (assert unique).
  • Framing: payload_len of 0, 1, MAX_MESSAGE, MAX_MESSAGE+1 -> last is rejected Err(Ipc), process survives.
  • Malformed/attack: truncated frame, len=u32::MAX, num_handles>0, garbage header bytes -> all return Err(Ipc), never panic, never OOM, never hang (recv has read timeout/EOF path).
  • Disconnect: peer drop mid-frame -> recv() -> Err(Ipc) within one poll cycle; pending calls resolve to Err(Ipc).

13.5 Conformance Fixtures

Fixture Input Expected
fx/ipc/echo_roundtrip.json N=10000 request payloads all responses matched, identical
fx/ipc/header_golden.bin canonical 24B header, known fields decode -> exact MessageHeader; re-encode == bytes (round-trip stable)
fx/ipc/concurrency_64.json 64 interleaved req_ids unique ids, all matched
fx/ipc/malformed_len_max.bin len=0xFFFFFFFF prefix Err(Ipc), no allocation
fx/ipc/malformed_truncated.bin header claims 4KiB, 100B sent Err(Ipc) on read-exact timeout/EOF
fx/ipc/malformed_handles_set.bin num_handles=3, no FDs Err(Ipc) (handles forbidden Phase 1)
fx/ipc/disconnect_midframe.bin half a frame then close Err(Ipc), pending calls error

13.6 Pitfalls

  • SOCK_STREAM vs SEQPACKET boundary loss: stream coalesces/splits writes -> a length prefix is mandatory regardless; relying on one read() == one message corrupts under load. Debug: strace -e recvmsg, check returned byte counts != frame size.
  • Partial read assumed complete: read() may return < requested without EOF; treat short read as "loop, not done." Root cause: ignoring read return. Debug: inject 1-byte-at-a-time socket.
  • Attacker-controlled len -> OOM: Vec::with_capacity(len) before bounds-check lets a peer request 4 GiB. Always check len <= MAX_MESSAGE first. Debug: fuzz len field, watch RSS.
  • req_id reuse/wrap: trusting peer-supplied req_id to index pending; a malicious peer forges ids to complete the wrong oneshot. Only the local side owns its pending map; ignore unknown ids. Debug: assert id in map before complete.
  • Blocking recv deadlock: request/response both blocking on one thread -> self-deadlock. Async demux or separate recv thread required. Debug: both peers stuck in recvmsg.
  • Endianness drift: native-endian encode breaks cross-arch; lock little-endian. Debug: golden header_golden.bin mismatch on BE.

13.7 Performance Concerns

  • Targets: small-message (64B) round-trip < 10 µs local; throughput > 1 GiB/s for 64 KiB payloads (single pipe); large bulk moves to sbx-shm/ShmRing (Phase 2), not the pipe.
  • One sendmsg/recvmsg per frame; avoid extra copies — writev header+payload (vectored) to skip a concat alloc.
  • MAX_MESSAGE 16 MiB caps per-message latency + memory; recv buffer reused (no per-recv alloc on hot path).
  • Async demux: single recv task; oneshot wakeups O(1) hash lookup; avoid lock contention via sharded/DashMap pending if needed.

13.8 Edge Cases

  • Zero-length payload (header-only control message) -> valid.
  • req_id u64 wraparound after 2^64 calls -> documented non-concern; monotonic.
  • Simultaneous close (both ends) -> both recv -> Err(Ipc) cleanly, no SIGPIPE crash (set MSG_NOSIGNAL/SO_NOSIGPIPE).
  • Named socket: connect before listener bound -> ECONNREFUSED -> SpawnFailed/retry; stale socket file -> unlink-on-bind.
  • Response arrives for already-cancelled/dropped call -> drop silently.
  • Event (MsgKind::Event, no req_id correlation) interleaved with requests -> routed to handler, not pending.

13.9 Failure Analysis

Failure Detection Recovery
Peer crash / EOF mid-stream recv -> 0 bytes / ECONNRESET Err(Ipc); resolve all pending to Err; surface to launcher (-> Phase 11 crash path)
Oversize/malformed frame from untrusted peer bounds-check fails drop frame, Err(Ipc), keep broker alive; log peer ProcessId
Send on closed pipe EPIPE (no signal) Err(Ipc); caller treats peer as gone
recv task starvation (flood of events) queue depth metric backpressure: bounded channel, drop/throttle events, never the request path

13.10 Operational Notes

  • Tracing span per call keyed on (route, req_id); record send->recv latency; counters: ipc_msgs_sent/recv, ipc_bytes, ipc_malformed_dropped, ipc_disconnects.
  • RUST_LOG=sbx_ipc=debug dumps header fields (not payload — payload may carry untrusted data).
  • MAX_MESSAGE env-overridable for tests only; production value compiled/locked.
  • Cross-boundary note: the broker side treats every decoded Message as untrusted input — fixtures in 1.5 double as the Phase 13 IPC-fuzz seed corpus (fx/ipc/malformed_* feed sbx attack).

14. Phase 2 — Handle and shared-memory passing (SCM_RIGHTS, memfd)

Builds the capability-passing substrate: FD duplication via SCM_RIGHTS ancillary data over the Phase 1 UDS MessagePipe, the single-owner Handle, and sbx-shm (memfd SharedBuffer, Mapping, F_SEAL, ShmRing). This is the primitive by which the broker grants a renderer only an opened FD, never a path — the keystone of deny-by-default. All work stays inside the trust boundary's transport; policy validation is Phase 7.

14.1 Objectives

  • Pass owned OS FDs peer↔peer via sendmsg/recvmsg SCM_RIGHTS cmsg; receiver gets a duplicated fd in its own table (capability transfer, not path transfer).
  • Handle: single-owner, close-on-drop, non-Clone, move-only; reject double-close/leak; track open count for tests.
  • Wire-bind handle count: MessageHeader.num_handles MUST equal received cmsg fd count; mismatch → SbxError::BadHandle, close strays.
  • sbx-shm: memfd_create-backed SharedBuffer; Mapping ro/rw via mmap; F_SEAL_* to freeze size/writes; ShmRing SPSC for large zero-copy bulk.
  • Deliverable: send an fd + a shm region across the boundary; peer reads/writes; throughput + ownership tests pass.

14.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

Theory applied: P11 (Garfinkel 2003 — TOCTOU via paths; FDs as capabilities eliminate the race), ADR-06 (SCM_RIGHTS Handle as the capability-passing primitive), ADR-09 (memfd + F_SEAL_* for safe shm), ADR-10 (ShmRing for large zero-copy). B1 (TLPI ch.57–61 — cmsg layout, MSG_CMSG_CLOEXEC, MSG_CTRUNC truncation rules). INV-3 (no FD leak or double-close across the boundary) drives the Handle drop/move semantics; INV-4 (shm seals immutable before handoff) drives seal-before-pass ordering.

Module / file Adds Detail
sbx-ipc/src/handle.rs Handle{fd:RawFd} from_raw_fd(takes ownership), as_raw_fd, try_clone()→dup, into_raw_fd(disarm). Drop→close; unsafe Send only (no Clone). AsFd.
sbx-ipc/src/scm.rs send_with_handles(sock,&Message), recv_with_handles(sock)->Message Build cmsghdr SOL_SOCKET/SCM_RIGHTS, i32[num_handles] of as_raw_fd; sendmsg(MSG_NOSIGNAL). Recv: preallocate cmsg buf CMSG_SPACE(num_handles*4), recvmsg(MSG_CMSG_CLOEXEC), walk CMSG_FIRSTHDR/CMSG_NXTHDR, wrap each fd in Handle.
sbx-ipc/src/pipe.rs MessagePipe::{send,recv} route handle-bearing msgs through scm.rs when num_handles>0 Cap num_handles ≤ 8 (SCM_MAX_FD typ. 253; we cap low). Payload + ancillary in one sendmsg so framing+fds stay atomic.
sbx-shm/src/buffer.rs SharedBuffer{handle,len} create(len)→memfd_create("sbx-shm",MFD_CLOEXEC|MFD_ALLOW_SEALING), ftruncate(len). Carries as a Handle over IPC.
sbx-shm/src/mapping.rs Mapping, Prot mmap(len, prot=R|(W if RW), MAP_SHARED, fd). Deref<[u8]>; DerefMut only when ReadWrite. Drop→munmap.
sbx-shm/src/seal.rs seal(&SharedBuffer, flags) fcntl(F_ADD_SEALS, F_SEAL_SHRINK|F_SEAL_GROW|F_SEAL_WRITE|F_SEAL_SEAL). Receiver verifies seals via F_GET_SEALS before trusting size.
sbx-shm/src/ring.rs ShmRing SPSC over a SharedBuffer: header {head:AtomicU64,tail:AtomicU64,cap} in-band at offset 0, data after. Acquire/Release ordering; power-of-two cap, mask indexing; push_slice/pop_slice, no locks.

Algorithms: (a) cmsg parse — reject if cmsg_level≠SOL_SOCKET ∥ cmsg_type≠SCM_RIGHTS; truncation (MSG_CTRUNC in msg_flags) → close all received fds, BadHandle. (b) count check — received fd count vs num_handles; surplus/short → close received, error. (c) ring — single producer writes data then Release store of tail; consumer Acquire loads tail, reads, Release store of head.

14.3 Deliverable

sbx ipc-ping --pass-fd <path> (extends Phase 1): browser opens a file, sends the FD + a 64 MiB sealed SharedBuffer to the child; child reads via the brokered FD, mmaps the buffer, verifies a CRC the parent wrote, echoes a CRC of its own write-back through a ShmRing. Exit 0 iff CRCs match and fd-table accounting is clean.

14.4 Acceptance Criteria

  • Round-trip a real FD: child reads exactly the parent's file bytes via the passed fd; the path is never sent on the wire (assert payload contains no path string).
  • num_handles mismatch (inject extra/missing fd) → SbxError::BadHandle, zero fd leak (verify via /proc/self/fd count pre/post).
  • Sealed SharedBuffer: receiver write attempt on a F_SEAL_WRITE region → SIGBUS/EPERM; size cannot grow/shrink post-seal.
  • Handle double-close impossible: dropping a moved-from Handle is a no-op; into_raw_fd disarms Drop (no spurious close).
  • ShmRing 1 GiB streamed, zero-copy: throughput ≥ 5 GB/s single-core; zero data corruption vs memcpy baseline.

14.5 Conformance Fixtures

Fixture Input Expected
fd_roundtrip.txt known 4 KiB file child SHA-256 == parent SHA-256
handle_count_extra.json hdr num_handles=1, 2 fds sent BadHandle; /proc/self/fd delta == 0
handle_count_short.json hdr num_handles=2, 1 fd sent BadHandle; received fd closed
cmsg_truncated.bin undersized recv cmsg buf MSG_CTRUNC detected → BadHandle, all fds closed
cmsg_wrong_type.bin SCM_CREDENTIALS cmsg rejected; no fd installed
seal_then_write.rs write to sealed RO map SIGBUS (caught) / mapping refused PROT_WRITE
ring_throughput_1g.bin 1 GiB through ShmRing ≥5 GB/s, CRC32 match
ring_wrap.json writes spanning buffer end correct wrap, no torn reads

14.6 Pitfalls

  • fd leak on the error path: parse fails after kernel installed fds → must close every received fd before returning. Root cause: kernel installs cmsg fds regardless of app validation. Debug: watch /proc/<pid>/fd count climb under fuzzing.
  • Missing MSG_CMSG_CLOEXEC: received fds inherited across a later exec → sandbox-escape vector (untrusted child keeps a privileged fd). Always set it; verify FD_CLOEXEC on recv.
  • SIGPIPE on peer death: sendmsg to a dead child raises SIGPIPE, killing the broker. Use MSG_NOSIGNAL (and/or signal(SIGPIPE, SIG_IGN) at startup).
  • memfd without MFD_ALLOW_SEALING: later F_ADD_SEALS → EINVAL. The flag must be set at memfd_create, not retrofittable.
  • Handle accidental Clone/Copy: would double-own one fd → double-close, then close of an unrelated reused fd → data corruption in TCB. Enforce move-only at compile time; no derive.
  • Ring without acquire/release fences: consumer reads stale data or torn slices across the trust boundary. Root cause: relaxed atomics reorder data vs index publish. Debug: TSan + cross-core stress.
  • Partial sendmsg of payload: large payloads short-write while ancillary already delivered → desync. Keep handle-bearing messages small; assert full byte count or fail.

14.7 Performance Concerns

  • SCM_RIGHTS cost ~µs/fd (kernel fd-table dup); negligible vs spawn — but cap num_handles low (≤8) to bound cmsg buffer + parse loop.
  • Bulk data: NEVER via IPC payload (copy + syscall per chunk). Use ShmRing for >4 KiB: zero-copy, mmap'd once; target ≥5 GB/s SPSC.
  • One sendmsg carries payload+fds (single syscall); avoid a second send for ancillary.
  • mmap/munmap per buffer ~µs + page-fault on first touch; prefer long-lived SharedBuffer reuse + MADV_DONTNEED to recycle.
  • Ring header on its own cache line (pad head/tail 64 B apart) to kill false sharing across producer/consumer cores.

14.8 Edge Cases

  • num_handles==0 → fall back to Phase 1 plain send/recv (no cmsg).
  • Zero-length SharedBuffer → reject (mmap len 0 = EINVAL); enforce len>0.
  • Non-power-of-two ShmRing cap → round up or reject; mask indexing requires pow2.
  • Receiver hits RLIMIT_NOFILE mid-recv → kernel returns fewer fds / EMFILE; treat as BadHandle, close partials.
  • memfd sent to a child later denied mmap by seccomp (Phase 5) → fd valid but unusable; documented interaction, not an error here.
  • Producer outruns consumer on ShmRing (full) → push_slice returns short/0; caller backpressures, never overwrites unread.

14.9 Failure Analysis

Failure Detection Containment
Malformed cmsg from compromised child type/level/MSG_CTRUNC checks close fds, BadHandle, drop message; broker survives
fd-count spoof (claim N, send M) header-vs-actual compare close all received, no Handle constructed
Sealed-buffer tamper attempt F_GET_SEALS verify on receive reject buffer if expected seals absent
Ring index corruption by untrusted writer bounds-mask head/tail ∈ [0,cap) on read clamp/reject; never index OOB into mapping
fd exhaustion DoS per-peer in-flight handle quota reject beyond quota, PolicyDenied (full enforcement Phase 7)

Trust note: receiver treats all incoming cmsg + ring indices as hostile input — validated before any fd install or memory access.

14.10 Operational Notes

  • Logging: every fd send/recv logs (route, req_id, num_handles); never log fd numbers as stable IDs (per-process, reused).
  • Debug: lsof -p <pid> / /proc/<pid>/fd and /proc/<pid>/maps to audit leaked fds + stray mappings; strace -e trace=sendmsg,recvmsg,memfd_create,mmap,fcntl.
  • Metric: sbx_handles_passed_total, sbx_handle_leak_detected_total (must stay 0), sbx_shm_bytes_total, ring throughput gauge.
  • Portability: MSG_CMSG_CLOEXEC + memfd_create + F_SEAL are Linux-specific; macOS/Windows shm/handle backends are PhaseDeferred stubs.
  • Invariant gate for CI: post-test /proc/self/fd count == baseline (no leak) is a hard assertion.

15. Phase 3 — Process launcher and lifecycle

sbx-launcher: turns "fork+exec a child" (Phase 0 smoke) into a reusable, typed, supervised lifecycle. The browser launches N children of a given ProcessType, hands each a MessagePipe, completes a versioned handshake, then monitors + reaps them via a single SIGCHLD-driven reaper. No sandbox, no zygote (Policy carried but LinuxSandbox::apply not yet invoked; use_zygote ignored → direct path). Restart hooks are exposed but not driven (consumed by sbx-crash §23). This phase owns the untrusted-child spawn mechanics the trust boundary later sits on; correctness of fd handling + reaping here is a security precondition (a leaked fd or a missed reap becomes a sandbox-escape or zombie-storm vector downstream).

15.1 Objectives

  • ProcessLauncher::launch(LaunchOptions) -> Result<ChildProcess, SbxError>: typed spawn via re-exec of /proc/self/exe with role arg/env; one socketpair MessagePipe per child.
  • Child-side entry: detect --child/SBX_PROCESS_TYPE, dup2 inherited channel fd to a fixed slot, reconstruct MessagePipe, run role main.
  • Handshake (handshake.rs): exchange identity (pid, ProcessType, proto version) + initial Endpoint; reject version mismatch → SbxError::SpawnFailed.
  • ChildProcess{pid,channel} + ProcessHandle (cloneable supervisory token: pid, type, exit-status cell, restart hook).
  • Single global reaper: SIGCHLD handler → self-pipe → waitpid(WNOHANG) loop drains all exits; no per-child blocking thread.
  • Restart hooks present (on_exit(FnMut(ExitInfo))) but inert this phase; deterministic teardown (kill+reap all children on launcher drop).

15.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

Theory applied: B1 (TLPI ch.24–28, ch.34 — fork/exec/waitpid semantics, SIGCHLD reaping, zombie-prevention via SA_NOCLDWAIT), B6 (Love — async-signal-safe signal handling), ADR-05 (UDS channel for process bootstrapping), P6 (Barth et al. 2008 — TCB launchers untrusted children). INV-5 (process identity never forged — ProcessId allocated by launcher, not by child) and INV-6 (child fd inheritance bounded — only channel fd survives exec) govern this phase.

File Adds
sbx-launcher/src/lib.rs re-export ProcessLauncher, LaunchOptions{process_type,policy,use_zygote}, ChildProcess{pid:ProcessId,channel:MessagePipe}, ProcessHandle, ExitInfo, ExitKind
sbx-launcher/src/launcher.rs launch(): pair() → fork() → child dup2(peer→FD 3), set SBX_PROCESS_TYPE/SBX_CHANNEL_FD=3/SBX_PROTO, execv(/proc/self/exe,[exe,"--child"]); parent closes peer fd, registers handle, calls handshake
sbx-launcher/src/child.rs child_entry(): read env, MessagePipe::from_raw_fd(3), drive child handshake; ProcessHandle{pid,ptype,exit:Arc<OnceCell<ExitInfo>>,on_exit:Arc<Mutex<Option<Box<dyn FnMut(ExitInfo)+Send>>>>}; Reaper (self-pipe + registry HashMap<ProcessId,Weak<HandleInner>>)
sbx-launcher/src/handshake.rs MsgKind::{Hello,HelloAck} (Phase 0); add Identity{pid,ptype,proto} payload + ProtoVersion const; parent_handshake/child_handshake; bind first Endpoint<ControlIface> 
pub struct LaunchOptions { pub process_type: ProcessType, pub policy: Policy, pub use_zygote: bool }
pub struct ProcessLauncher { reaper: Reaper, next_pid: AtomicU32, proto: u32 }
impl ProcessLauncher {
    pub fn launch(&self, opts: LaunchOptions) -> Result<ChildProcess, SbxError>;
    pub fn on_child_exit(&self, h: &ProcessHandle, cb: impl FnMut(ExitInfo)+Send+'static); // inert P3
}
pub struct ExitInfo { pub pid: ProcessId, pub kind: ExitKind } // kind: Exited(i32)|Signaled(i32)|Unknown
  • fd discipline: socketpair(AF_UNIX, SOCK_SEQPACKET|SOCK_CLOEXEC); parent closes its copy of the peer end pre-fork-return; child clears CLOEXEC on the kept fd via dup2 to FD 3 (un-CLOEXEC'd) so it survives execv, leaving all other fds CLOEXEC → closed.
  • Reaper init: install SIGCHLD (SA_RESTART|SA_NOCLDSTOP) once at launcher construction; handler does only write(self_pipe,1) (async-signal-safe). A reaper task reads the pipe, loops waitpid(-1,WNOHANG), matches pid→handle, fills exit, fires on_exit.
  • Handshake: parent sends Hello{Identity}; child replies HelloAck{Identity}; both assert proto==ProtoVersion and recv pid matches waitpid pid; timeout (default 2 s) → kill child + SpawnFailed.
  • ProcessId allocated by launcher (next_pid), distinct from OS pid; reaper maps OS pid → ProcessId via a HashMap<libc::pid_t,ProcessId>.

15.3 Deliverable

  • sbx spawn --type renderer launches one typed child, handshakes, prints OK pid=<n> type=Renderer proto=<v>; child idles until Shutdown, exits 0, parent reaps.
  • ProcessLauncher launches N children of mixed types concurrently; all handshake; killing one (kill -9) is detected by the reaper, ExitInfo{Signaled(9)} recorded, others unaffected.
  • Integration test lifecycle: spawn → talk (Hello/HelloAck) → graceful exit → reap; and spawn → external kill → reap. Launcher Drop kills+reaps all survivors (no orphans/zombies).

15.4 Acceptance Criteria

# Criterion Measure
A1 Typed spawn child observes ProcessType via env; mismatch impossible (asserted in handshake Identity.ptype)
A2 Handshake parent+child exchange Identity; proto mismatch → SpawnFailed, child killed+reaped, NO hang
A3 N concurrent launch N=64 children, all handshake < 250 ms aggregate; pid set unique
A4 Graceful reap child exit(0) → ExitInfo{Exited(0)} recorded; /proc/<pid> gone; zero zombies (`ps -o stat
A5 Kill detected kill -9 <child> → reaper records Signaled(9); sibling children + parent unaffected (blast-radius primitive)
A6 fd hygiene child /proc/<pid>/fd contains ONLY {0,1,2, FD 3 = channel}; peer/socketpair-2nd-end absent (no fd leak across trust boundary)
A7 No reaper races 100× rapid spawn+exit: every exit fired exactly once; no lost/double on_exit; no zombie
A8 Clean teardown launcher drop with live children → all SIGKILLed + reaped; no orphan reparented to init

15.5 Conformance Fixtures

Fixture Input Expected
fixtures/launch_renderer.txt sbx spawn --type renderer OK pid=<n> type=Renderer proto=<v>, exit 0, reaped
fixtures/launch_n64.txt launch 64 mixed children 64 unique pids, 64 handshakes, all reaped, 0 zombies
fixtures/child_fd_list.txt ls /proc/<child>/fd post-spawn exactly 0 1 2 3 (3→socket); attack-relevant: no extra fd
fixtures/kill_detected.txt spawn then kill -9 reaper logs ExitInfo{pid,Signaled(9)}; parent survives
fixtures/handshake_badproto.txt child sends proto=999 parent SpawnFailed, child killed, reaped, no hang (attack)
fixtures/handshake_silent.txt child never sends HelloAck 2 s timeout → kill + SpawnFailed, no leaked process (attack)
fixtures/handshake_flood.bin child sends 10k junk msgs pre-HelloAck parent bounded-reads, rejects, SpawnFailed; no OOM/CPU spin (attack)
fixtures/exec_enoent.txt corrupt /proc/self/exe re-exec child _exit(127) → SpawnFailed (not handshake timeout)
fixtures/rapid_churn.txt 100× spawn+immediate exit 100 exits, each fired once, 0 zombies (race fixture)

15.6 Pitfalls

  • SIGCHLD coalescing: multiple children die between handler invocations → kernel delivers ONE SIGCHLD (signals don't queue). Fix: handler is a wakeup; reaper loops waitpid(WNOHANG) until 0/ECHILD. Symptom if wrong: zombies despite handler firing (debug: ps -o stat,pid | grep Z). Self-pipe full (storm of exits): handler write would block (illegal in handler) — pipe nonblocking, handler ignores EAGAIN (one byte already pending ⇒ reaper drains regardless).
  • Handshake recv on a non-handshake fd: child reconstructs pipe from the WRONG fd if SBX_CHANNEL_FD drifts from the dup2 target. Root cause: magic-number mismatch. Fix: single const CHANNEL_FD: RawFd = 3; assert in both launch and child_entry. Debug: child recv blocks forever; check lsof -p.
  • CLOEXEC self-deadlock: forget to clear CLOEXEC on FD 3 → execv closes the channel → child's first recv = EBADF. Fix: dup2(peer,3) yields a non-CLOEXEC fd (dup2 clears it); never fcntl it back. Debug: child immediate EBADF on recv.
  • Reaper steals the handshake waitpid: a global waitpid(-1) reaper can reap a child before the parent handshake completes, racing the recv/exit-status read. Root cause: reaper + handshake both touch the same child. Fix: handshake uses only the pipe (never waitpid); reaper records exit but handshake distinguishes EOF-on-pipe (SpawnFailed) from HelloAck.
  • PID reuse aliasing: OS pid reused after reap; a stale HashMap<pid_t,ProcessId> entry misattributes a new child's exit. Root cause: not removing the map entry on reap. Fix: erase pid→ProcessId atomically when exit is filled. Debug: on_exit fires on wrong ProcessHandle.
  • fork() in a multithreaded launcher: tracing background thread or future spawner makes post-fork pre-exec code async-signal-unsafe (alloc/lock in child). Fix: between fork and execv call ONLY dup2/setenv-via-pre-built-array/execv/_exit; build env array before fork. (Carried from Phase 0; re-asserted as TCB rule.)

15.7 Performance Concerns

  • Direct re-exec spawn (no zygote): target < 5 ms fork+exec+handshake per child on host; this is the baseline the zygote (§18) must beat (target < 15 ms incl. sandbox seal, but COW-cheaper than fresh exec).
  • Handshake RTT (Hello→HelloAck): < 200 µs (Phase 0 budget); 2 SEQPACKET round-trips.
  • Reaper is O(exits), not O(children): single thread, WNOHANG drain; no thread-per-child. 1000 idle children cost ~0 reaper CPU.
  • N=64 concurrent launch: serial fork is fine (< 250 ms aggregate); do NOT parallelize fork across threads (re-introduces multithread-fork hazard).
  • Pre-build the execv argv/envp arrays once per launch; avoid per-fork allocation on the async-signal-unsafe path.

15.8 Edge Cases

  • fork() → -1 (EAGAIN/rlimit NPROC): SpawnFailed, peer fd closed, no half-registered handle.
  • Child execv fails (ENOENT/EACCES): child _exit(127); parent's handshake recv sees EOF → maps to SpawnFailed (distinguished from proto timeout by immediate EOF vs 2 s).
  • Parent dies mid-handshake: child recv EOF → child _exit(non-zero); child reparented to init then reaped by init (no zombie). Launcher Drop (if parent survives) kills survivors.
  • Double-reap attempt: waitpid returns ECHILD for an already-reaped pid → ignored, exit already filled (idempotent). Same child SIGSTOP'd (not exited): SA_NOCLDSTOP suppresses spurious SIGCHLD; reaper does not mistake stop for exit.
  • ProcessId exhaustion (u32 wrap): allocator saturates → SpawnFailed rather than alias an existing id.
  • Handshake message > 64 KiB (oversize Identity): framing layer (§13) rejects → SbxError::Ipc → SpawnFailed; never trusts oversize child input.

15.9 Failure Analysis

Failure Detection Containment (this phase)
Child crash pre-handshake pipe EOF + reaper Signaled SpawnFailed; parent + siblings survive (blast-radius primitive ahead of §23)
Child hangs in handshake 2 s timeout kill(SIGKILL) + reap + SpawnFailed; no leaked process
Missed SIGCHLD / lost wakeup self-pipe + WNOHANG drain loop reaper still drains on next signal/poll; bounded zombie window
PID reuse misattribution erase-on-reap invariant + assert wrong-handle dispatch impossible; on_exit once-only
fd leak into child A6 /proc/<pid>/fd audit in CI extra fd = test FAIL; closes a future sandbox-escape vector early
Launcher panics with live children Drop impl + panic-safe teardown children SIGKILLed + reaped; no orphan storm
Reaper thread death supervised; missing reaper → spawn refused fail-closed: launch errors if reaper not live (no silent zombie accrual)

15.10 Operational Notes

  • tracing spans carry process_type + both ids (sbx_pid, os_pid) from launch through reap (cross-boundary tracing matures §24); log spawn, handshake_ok, exit{kind}.
  • Re-exec uses /proc/self/exe (Linux primary); macOS/Windows = PhaseDeferred (their spawn = posix_spawn/CreateProcessW, stubbed). Guard infinite re-exec with the --child arg + SBX_PROCESS_TYPE presence check.
  • SBX_CHANNEL_FD=3 is an ABI between launcher and child entry; document in CONTRIBUTING.md. Children MUST NOT inherit any other fd (CLOEXEC-by-default policy).
  • Reaper is process-global (one SIGCHLD owner per browser process); a second launcher instance reuses it. Conflicts with foreign SIGCHLD handlers → documented incompatibility (we own SIGCHLD).
  • Restart hooks (on_child_exit) are wired but inert (sbx-crash §23 supplies RestartPolicy; no restart loops occur this phase by construction). Teardown order on shutdown: send Shutdown control msg → grace window → SIGTERM → SIGKILL → reap; surfaced via sbx spawn/sbx run.

16. Phase 4 — Linux sandbox part 1: namespaces and filesystem confinement

First OS-level confinement layer. sbx-sandbox::LinuxSandbox enters an unprivileged USER namespace (fake root), creates PID/NET/MNT/IPC/UTS namespaces, pivot_roots into a minimal read-only rootfs, applies rlimits, sets PR_SET_NO_NEW_PRIVS, and drops ALL capabilities. seccomp-bpf is Phase 5 — this phase is namespaces + filesystem + caps only. Fail-closed: any setup error abort()s the child before it touches untrusted input.

16.1 Objectives

  • Implement Sandbox::apply(&Policy) for LinuxSandbox: unprivileged USER ns → uid/gid maps → PID/NET/MNT/IPC/UTS ns → pivot_root minimal RO rootfs → rlimits → no_new_privs → drop all caps.
  • No real root, no CAP_SYS_ADMIN, no setuid helper — USER ns is the privilege source. unshare(CLONE_NEWUSER) first, then nest other namespaces.
  • Defense layer 1 of 2 (seccomp = layer 2): renderer has zero network, zero foreign PID visibility, RO rootfs, empty cap set, NOFILE/AS/NPROC capped. Strict ORDER is security-critical — wrong order = silent escape or EPERM; fail-closed everywhere.

16.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

Theory applied: P3 (Chromium Linux Sandboxing — namespace layer as the first confinement tier), R4 (minijail — namespace + pivot_root + rlimit orchestration sequence), ADR-15 (unprivileged user-ns eliminates SUID helper), ADR-16 (pivot_root not chroot — detaches old root). B1 (TLPI ch.36–38 — capabilities, no_new_privs); user_namespaces(7), pivot_root(2), clone(2) man pages applied here. INV-7 (sandbox applied before untrusted data) and INV-8 (capability set is zero in every child) are enforced this phase.

Files (per §10 repo layout) under sbx-sandbox/src/:

File Adds
linux/mod.rs impl Sandbox for LinuxSandbox; orchestrates apply in locked order
linux/namespaces.rs Namespaces clone-flags; unshare/clone wrappers; ns predicates
linux/userns.rs write_uid_map/write_gid_map; setgroups=deny gate
linux/fs.rs pivot_root (or chroot fallback); RO bind-mounts; /proc remount
linux/rlimit.rs Rlimits → setrlimit loop
linux/caps.rs capset to empty; PR_CAPBSET_DROP bounding set; no_new_privs
linux/errors.rs map syscall errno → SbxError::Sandbox

LOCKED apply() order (each step fails-closed):

1. prctl(PR_SET_NO_NEW_PRIVS, 1)          // before any unshare; sticky, irreversible
2. unshare(CLONE_NEWUSER)                 // fake root; gain CAP_* inside new userns
3. write /proc/self/setgroups = "deny"    // MUST precede gid_map (CVE-2014-8989 class)
   write /proc/self/uid_map "0 <euid> 1"  // map child uid 0 -> outer euid
   write /proc/self/gid_map "0 <egid> 1"
4. unshare(CLONE_NEWPID|NEWNET|NEWNS|NEWIPC|NEWUTS)  // NEWPID affects CHILDREN -> fork after
5. fork(); child continues (now pid 1 in new pidns)  // parent waitpid
6. mount("","/","",MS_REC|MS_PRIVATE)     // detach mount propagation before pivot
7. pivot_root(new_root, put_old); chdir("/"); umount2(put_old, MNT_DETACH); rmdir
8. mount /proc (RO), bind RO rootfs dirs; remount MS_RDONLY|MS_NOSUID|MS_NODEV|MS_NOEXEC
9. setrlimit(NOFILE, AS, NPROC, CORE=0, FSIZE, ...) from Policy.rlimits
10. cap_drop: capset(empty) + PR_CAPBSET_DROP for all 0..=CAP_LAST_CAP

Key internal signatures (impl Sandbox for LinuxSandbox is LOCKED in §11.4 Core Schema — not restated here):

fn enter_user_ns() -> Result<(), SbxError>;                    // unshare NEWUSER + maps
fn write_id_maps(uid: u32, gid: u32) -> Result<(), SbxError>;  // setgroups deny first
fn enter_namespaces(ns: &Namespaces) -> Result<(), SbxError>;  // PID|NET|MNT|IPC|UTS
fn pivot_into(root: &Path) -> Result<(), SbxError>;   // MS_PRIVATE -> pivot_root -> detach old
fn apply_rlimits(r: &Rlimits) -> Result<(), SbxError>;
fn drop_all_caps() -> Result<(), SbxError>;           // capset empty + bounding set

Rootfs: minimal RO tree (/lib,/lib64,/usr/lib for the renderer binary's .so deps, /dev/null,/dev/urandom via bind, empty /tmp tmpfs noexec). No /etc, no /home, no real /proc of host. Network verification: socket(AF_INET,...) after NEWNET → only lo exists and is DOWN; broker is the only egress.

16.3 Deliverable

  • LinuxSandbox::apply fully wired into sbx-launcher (Phase 3 clone/exec path runs it pre-exec).
  • sbx sandbox-test subcommand spawns a child that calls apply, then self-reports: visible PIDs, cap set, network reachability, rootfs writability → asserts the locked invariants.
  • Child in fully namespaced, rootless, network-less, capability-empty, RO-rootfs environment.

16.4 Acceptance Criteria

# Assertion Check
A1 Child sees only itself + ≤1 helper in pidns getpid()==1; /proc lists ≤2 pids
A2 Empty effective+permitted+inheritable caps capget → all 0; /proc/self/status CapEff: 0000000000000000
A3 Bounding set empty prctl(PR_CAPBSET_READ, n)==0 ∀ n
A4 no_new_privs set /proc/self/status NoNewPrivs: 1
A5 No network connect()/socket(AF_INET) → only lo DOWN; outbound → ENETUNREACH/EPERM
A6 Rootfs RO open(O_WRONLY) on rootfs file → EROFS; /tmp writable but noexec
A7 rlimits enforced NOFILE/AS/NPROC == Policy values; RLIMIT_CORE==0
A8 Fail-closed inject failure at any step k → child abort()s, never proceeds to exec untrusted code; launcher sees nonzero exit

16.5 Conformance Fixtures

sbx-sandbox/tests/fixtures/:

Fixture Expected
ns_present.json report: {userns,pidns,netns,mntns,ipcns,utsns} all distinct inode ids ≠ host
caps_empty.json CapEff=CapPrm=CapInh=CapBnd=0
net_gone.json ifaces == ["lo"], lo state DOWN, ext connect → ENETUNREACH
rootfs_ro.json write attempts → EROFS list; allowed-write paths == ["/tmp"]
rlimits.json each RLIMIT_* soft==hard==policy
attack escape_setgroups.json gid_map written WITHOUT setgroups=deny → apply MUST fail (EPERM), no fallback
attack escape_pivot_skip.json pivot_root step skipped → child sees host / → test MUST detect & fail apply
attack escape_cap_keep.json one cap left in bounding set → A3 fails → flagged
malformed bad_uid_map.json uid_map "0 0 4294967295" (range overflow) → write EINVAL → fail-closed

16.6 Pitfalls

  • gid_map before setgroups=deny → /proc/self/setgroups write fails or grants setgroups() → privilege bug (CVE-2014-8989 class); uid_map written by wrong process/after CLONE → EPERM (only one write, by a process with caps in parent userns). Debug: strace -e trace=write on /proc/self/{setgroups,uid_map,gid_map}; verify deny precedes map, single correct-euid write.
  • CLONE_NEWPID needs a fork → caller does NOT become pid 1; namespace applies to children. Forget fork → first child is pid 1, parent still sees host pids. Debug: check getpid() of the to-be-confined task == 1.
  • pivot_root without MS_PRIVATE → EINVAL or mount propagates to host (shared-subtree propagation). Debug: cat /proc/self/mountinfo, confirm private before pivot.
  • /proc not remounted in new pidns → reading host /proc leaks pids/escapes pidns view → A1 false-passes. Debug: mount -t proc proc /proc inside ns, verify pid count.
  • Ordering: no_new_privs FIRST, cap-drop LAST — set no_new_privs before any unshare (sticky, cannot be unset; a slipped setuid exec before it escalates); drop caps only after id-maps are written (child needs CAP_SETUID in the userns to write uid_map).

16.7 Performance Concerns

  • apply budget: < 2 ms (no fork/exec cost counted here; full spawn budget owned by Phase 6 zygote, target < 10 ms).
  • Mount setup (pivot + RO binds) dominates: ~6–10 syscalls; keep rootfs bind list minimal (≤8 mounts).
  • Avoid per-spawn rootfs construction — build the RO template once, bind-mount read-only per child (zygote reuses it).
  • PR_CAPBSET_DROP loop is CAP_LAST_CAP+1 (~41) prctls — negligible (< 50 µs).

16.8 Edge Cases

  • Host disallows unprivileged userns (/proc/sys/kernel/unprivileged_userns_clone=0, or RHEL max_user_namespaces=0) → unshare(NEWUSER) EPERM → fail-closed with actionable SbxError::Sandbox, surfaced by sbx sandbox-test as "userns disabled".
  • Nested containers / existing userns → uid_map range may be restricted; detect & fail rather than partially confine. CAP_LAST_CAP varies by kernel — read from /proc/sys/kernel/cap_last_cap, don't hardcode.
  • pivot_root unsupported on some FS for / → fall back to chroot+chdir ONLY if explicitly allowed in Policy (weaker; logged).
  • Single-thread requirement: unshare(NEWUSER) fails EINVAL if multithreaded — apply MUST run pre-thread-spawn (right after clone, before any runtime threads).

16.9 Failure Analysis

Failure Detection Containment / Recovery
userns unavailable unshare EPERM abort child; launcher → SpawnFailed; do NOT run renderer unconfined
partial namespace (some unshare ok, one fails) errno mid-sequence abort immediately — never run in half-confined state
pivot_root fails / cap drop fails EINVAL/EBUSY; capset errno abort; rootfs misconfig surfaced, no host-/ exposure; a renderer with caps is unacceptable
id-map write race EPERM/EINVAL abort; fail-closed
  • Invariant: every failure path ends in child termination before exec of untrusted work. No "best effort" sandbox. Launcher treats any apply error as SbxError::SpawnFailed/Sandbox and reaps.

16.10 Operational Notes

  • Preflight: sbx sandbox-test --preflight checks kernel knobs (unprivileged_userns_clone, cap_last_cap, seccomp availability for Phase 5) and prints a readiness report.
  • Required kernel: ≥ 3.8 for userns; recommend ≥ 5.x. Document distro toggles (Debian/Ubuntu sysctl, RHEL user.max_user_namespaces).
  • Diagnostics: on apply failure, log the exact step index + errno + /proc/self/status snapshot (CapEff/NoNewPrivs/NS ids) for triage. Dockerfile (CI) must allow unprivileged userns (enable userns in the test container, not --security-opt seccomp=unconfined) — namespace tests need real kernel namespaces, not mocked.
  • macOS/Windows: this whole phase is Linux-only; other backends remain PhaseDeferred stubs returning SbxError::Sandbox.

17. Phase 5 — Linux sandbox part 2: seccomp-bpf syscall filtering

Builds on Phase 4 (namespaces + pivot_root + no_new_privs + cap-drop). Adds the second independent OS-confinement layer: a kernel syscall filter. Combined with the broker (Phase 7) this is the two-layer defense-in-depth model — a single layer's bug must not yield escape. Crate: sbx-sandbox. NON-GOAL: arg-deep value tracking beyond scalar seccomp_data.args[6] (cBPF cannot deref pointers — see 17.6).

17.1 Objectives

  • SyscallPolicy DSL: ordered SyscallRule { nr, disposition, arg_filter } → compile to a classic-BPF program over struct seccomp_data.
  • Compiler invariant: load arch first, verify == expected AUDIT_ARCH_*, else KillProcess → blocks the x32/ia32 syscall-ABI bypass. Then load nr, branch per rule, fall through to a default disposition (deny by default).
  • Install via seccomp(SECCOMP_SET_MODE_FILTER, SECCOMP_FILTER_FLAG_TSYNC, &prog) — only after PR_SET_NO_NEW_PRIVS=1 (else EACCES for unprivileged). Dispositions Allow/Errno(i32)/Trap/KillProcess/Log → SECCOMP_RET_{ALLOW,ERRNO|n,TRAP,KILL_PROCESS,LOG}; the SIGSYS handler for Trap logs offending nr+arch+IP from siginfo, then aborts deterministically (no continue).
  • Author the baseline renderer policy: allow minimal set; Errno(ENOSYS) or KillProcess the rest.

17.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

Theory applied: P3 (Chromium Linux Sandboxing — seccomp-bpf as the second confinement tier), P8 (minijail — no_new_privs ordering, SyscallPolicy DSL), P10 (LWN seccomp overview — SECCOMP_RET_* semantics, user-notify), R1 (Chromium sandbox/linux/ — arch-check gadget, baseline renderer policy), ADR-11 (hand-built cBPF builder), ADR-12 (deny-by-default KillProcess), ADR-13 (arch-check-first), ADR-14 (no_new_privs before filter). INV-1 (seccomp filter installed before untrusted code) and INV-2 (arch gate present as first BPF instruction) are verified by the red-team harness in Phase 13.

Files (per section 10 layout), all in sbx-sandbox:

File Adds
src/seccomp.rs Disposition/SyscallRule/SyscallPolicy/SeccompFilter/ArgFilter; BpfInsn (16-byte sock_filter), opcode consts, BpfProgram{Vec<BpfInsn>}, jump-fixup helper; SyscallPolicy::compile() -> SeccompFilter (codegen); SeccompFilter::install() → prctl+seccomp(2), arch detection; install_sigsys_handler() reads si_syscall/si_arch
src/syscall_policy.rs baseline_renderer_policy() -> SyscallPolicy

Types (Disposition, ArgFilter, SyscallRule, SyscallPolicy{rules,default}, SeccompFilter, SyscallPolicy::compile, SeccompFilter::install) are LOCKED in §11.4 Core Schema — not restated here.

Compiler algorithm (compile.rs), emitted instruction sequence:

[0] LD  abs &seccomp_data.arch            # offset 4
[1] JEQ  EXPECTED_ARCH, ->nr_load, fall
[2] RET  SECCOMP_RET_KILL_PROCESS          # arch mismatch -> kill (x32/ia32 bypass blocked)
nr_load:
[3] LD  abs &seccomp_data.nr               # offset 0
    ;; per rule i: JEQ nr_i -> (arg-check block | RET disp_i)
    ;; x32 hardening: also JGE 0x40000000 -> KILL (x32 bit set)
...
default:
[N] RET  <default disposition>             # deny by default
  • Arg filter: when Some, after JEQ nr, emit LD args[index] (offset 16 + index*8, low+high word for 64-bit compare), JEQ/JGE/... per CmpOp → matched disposition, else fall to default (cBPF is 32-bit: 64-bit args compared as two 32-bit loads, high word at +4). Jump-fixup: forward jt/jf offsets are u8 (max 255); a rule block pushing a target >255 insns away inserts a trampoline JA (17.8).
  • install.rs: prctl(PR_SET_NO_NEW_PRIVS,1,0,0,0) (idempotent re-assert), build sock_fprog{len,filter}, syscall(SECCOMP_SET_MODE_FILTER, flags, &fprog); TSYNC fails (EBUSY→SbxError::Sandbox) if sibling threads diverge — renderer must be single-threaded at install (17.6). sigsys.rs: sigaction(SIGSYS, SA_SIGINFO|SA_NODEFER); handler is async-signal-safe only (raw write to fd 2, no alloc), logs nr/arch/ip, then KillProcess-equivalent (_exit(128+SIGSYS)).

Baseline renderer policy (renderer.rs) — the allow set (illustrative, x86_64 nrs vary by arch):

| Allowed (Allow) | read, write, readv, writev, close, fstat, lseek, mmap*, munmap, mprotect(no PROT_EXEC via arg_filter), brk, futex, exit, exit_group, rt_sigreturn, rt_sigaction, rt_sigprocmask, nanosleep, sched_yield, getpid, gettid, clock_gettime, restart_syscall, recvmsg/sendmsg (IPC pipe only) | |---| | Errno(ENOSYS) | unknown/new syscalls (forward-compat — don't kill on kernel upgrade) | | KillProcess | open, openat, socket, connect, ptrace, execve, execveat, clone(new ns flags), unshare, mount, keyctl, bpf, perf_event_open, process_vm_readv/writev, kexec*, init_module. *mmap arg_filter rejects PROT_EXEC\|PROT_WRITE (W^X) → Errno(EPERM); open/socket are killed, not errno'd, because legitimate renderer code never calls them directly — it goes through the broker (Phase 7), so a direct call is a strong compromise signal. |

17.3 Deliverable

LinuxSandbox::apply() now installs seccomp as the final step of Policy application (after namespaces, pivot_root, rlimits, no_new_privs, cap-drop). A child running baseline_renderer_policy():

  • Survives the allowed set (does real work over the IPC pipe); is KILLED (SIGSYS, exit 159) on a raw socket(2); gets ENOSYS on an unknown syscall.
  • sbx sandbox-test --policy renderer runs a child that attempts forbidden syscalls and reports each disposition. Validated by the red-team harness stub (Phase 13 seed) + policy unit tests.

17.4 Acceptance Criteria

# Criterion Measure
A1 Forbidden syscall denied child calling raw socket(AF_INET,...) → terminated by SIGSYS (WTERMSIG==31), broker untouched
A2 Errno path getrandom-class unknown nr → returns -ENOSYS, process continues
A3 Arch bypass blocked invoking via ia32/x32 ABI (int 0x80 / `0x40000000
A4 Install ordering + W^X install without prior no_new_privs → EACCES (with it → success); mmap(PROT_EXEC|PROT_WRITE) → EPERM
A5 Allowed work proceeds renderer completes a full IPC echo under the filter, 0 spurious kills over 10k msgs
A6 Two-layer even if a rule were mistakenly Allow-ed, broker still denies the resource (cross-checked in Phase 7 fixture)

17.5 Conformance Fixtures

Fixture Input Expected
seccomp/baseline_renderer.golden + arch_first.txt compiled BPF of baseline policy; disasm of insns [0..2] byte-exact insn dump (regression-locks codegen); first 3 ops = LD arch, JEQ EXPECTED, RET KILL
seccomp/forbidden_socket.json / allowed_read.json {nr: socket,...} / {nr: read} fed to a userspace BPF interpreter SECCOMP_RET_KILL_PROCESS / SECCOMP_RET_ALLOW respectively
seccomp/x32_bypass.json `{nr: 0x40000000 read}`
seccomp/mmap_wx.json + unknown_nr.json mmap with `PROT_EXEC PROT_WRITE`; nr = 9999
Malformed/attack empty policy (0 rules); attack-stub child does open("/etc/shadow") empty policy compiles to [arch-check, LD nr, RET default] (default must be deny); attack-stub killed by SIGSYS, file never opened (broker audit log)

Fixtures run against a pure-Rust bpf_eval(prog, &seccomp_data) -> u32 so CI validates codegen without needing the kernel; an integration test under --ignored runs the real child on a seccomp-capable host.

17.6 Pitfalls

Pitfall Root cause Debug
Filter rejected EACCES; random kills after thread spawn seccomp installed before no_new_privs; OR TSYNC requires all threads share the filter, renderer threaded before install assert ordering (grep Seccomp /proc/self/status); install in single-threaded child pre-fork-of-workers (strace -f)
x32 bypass (silent allow) only checked nr, not arch; x32 sets bit 0x40000000 dump insn[0..2]; ensure arch-first + JGE 0x40000000→KILL
64-bit arg filter never matches; jump offset overflow compared only low 32 bits of an 8-byte arg; separately, jt/jf u8 max 255 on a large policy load high word at +4 too; insert trampoline JA or split with JA chains
Trap loops forever SIGSYS handler returned (syscall retried) handler must _exit, never return; SA_NODEFER
Killing on new syscall after kernel upgrade glibc started using a syscall not in allow set default Errno(ENOSYS) for unknown, audit-log via Log, widen allow set deliberately

17.7 Performance Concerns

  • cBPF runs per syscall in-kernel; cost ∝ insns until match. Order hot syscalls (read/write/futex/recvmsg) first → ~5–15 insns typical, <100 ns/syscall overhead target.
  • Compile once at policy build (µs), install once per child (single seccomp(2), zero steady-state userspace cost); avoid pathological linear scan (target ≤256 insns for baseline, keep allow set tight). SECCOMP_RET_LOG audits are cheap but flood dmesg/auditd under attack — reserve Log for diagnostics, not production-hot paths.

17.8 Edge Cases

  • Empty policy → [arch-check, LD nr, RET default]; default MUST be Errno/KillProcess (never Allow). vfork/threads at install require single-threaded; TSYNC EBUSY is fatal.
  • SECCOMP_RET_KILL_PROCESS vs KILL_THREAD: always use KILL_PROCESS (whole renderer dies, not one thread leaking a live process). Syscall numbers are not portable across arches → policy is arch-keyed; compiling for the wrong arch is a build-time error.
  • restart_syscall/rt_sigreturn must both be allowed — the former for nanosleep/futex resumption after a signal, the latter or signal delivery (incl. the SIGSYS handler's own exit) deadlocks.
