  • ptrace/seccomp interaction: a debugger attaching can observe but not bypass; bpf syscall is killed so the renderer cannot install a weaker filter (seccomp filters only stack stricter — but kill anyway as signal).

17.9 Failure Analysis

Failure Detection Containment
Compiler emits wrong disposition; filter not installed (silent) golden fixture diff in CI; post-install self-check issues a known-forbidden syscall expecting kill — if it survives → SbxError::Sandbox block merge (codegen is TCB-critical); fail-closed: launcher aborts child, refuses to run untrusted work
Layer-1 bypass discovered red-team harness (Phase 13) broker layer still denies → blast radius = caller's CapabilitySet only
SIGSYS handler corrupted/unsafe crash in handler handler is alloc-free, async-signal-safe; on doubt KILL_PROCESS disposition (no handler) for max-sensitive syscalls

Fail-closed mandate: if seccomp cannot be installed (old kernel, missing CONFIG_SECCOMP_FILTER), the launcher MUST refuse to spawn the untrusted renderer rather than run it unconfined.

17.10 Operational Notes

  • Verify install at runtime: Seccomp: 2 (filter mode) in /proc/<pid>/status; Seccomp_filters: N. Debug a kill via dmesg | grep -i seccomp (nr/ip) or auditd SECCOMP records (syscall=,arch=); strace -f -e trace=%process pinpoints the exact failing syscall when a renderer mysteriously dies.
  • Policy is versioned; widening the allow set is a security-reviewed change (each new Allow expands attack surface). Record rationale per rule.
  • Kernel requirements: CONFIG_SECCOMP=y/CONFIG_SECCOMP_FILTER=y, TSYNC+KILL_PROCESS need ≥ Linux 4.14 (probe at startup, gate on MSRV-equivalent kernel floor); macOS (Seatbelt) / Windows (job-object) syscall confinement are PhaseDeferred stubs — Linux seccomp is primary.

18. Phase 6 — The zygote (pre-forked, pre-sandboxed renderer template)

Implements sbx-zygote: a long-lived template process started early, holding warm read-only pages (runtime, common libs) under a base sandbox; a ForkServer that, per ForkRequest{process_type, policy, channel}, fork()s a child that inherits COW pages + the passed channel, then seals/tightens its seccomp policy before any untrusted work; wired into sbx-launcher via use_zygote so a renderer spawn is a cheap fork, not a fresh exec.

18.1 Objectives

  • Cheap renderer spawn: COW fork() from a warm zygote vs clone+exec (Phase 3) — eliminate per-spawn loader/relocation cost.
  • Base-then-seal sandbox: zygote pre-applies the intersection-safe base (namespaces from Phase 4, no_new_privs); each fork tightens to the final per-type seccomp (Phase 5) before untrusted code — never loosens.
  • COW page sharing: warmed RX text/.rodata shared across all renderers — measurable RSS savings (shared vs PSS).
  • Single-fork() invariant: zygote stays single-threaded so child is in a consistent address space (no held locks / orphaned threads post-fork).
  • Targets met: spawn latency + sealed-sandbox correctness proven by the two Phase deliverable tests.

18.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

Theory applied: P4 (Chromium Zygote design — COW template, fork-server protocol, post-fork seccomp seal), B2 (Bovet & Cesati ch.9 — COW page semantics: shared read-only until first write → private dirty), R3 (Chromium zygote_host — fork protocol, sandbox sealing order), ADR-17 (zygote fork server vs fresh exec). The COW memory arithmetic from the Back-of-Envelope Capacity Model (BOE-1) derives the target private RSS delta per renderer. INV-9 (each forked child seals its seccomp policy before touching untrusted data) is established this phase.

Module / file Adds
sbx-zygote/src/zygote.rs Zygote{ sock: MessagePipe, base: Policy, warm: WarmSet }; Zygote::spawn(base) -> (Zygote, ForkServer); warms pages (touch/mlock RX text + common libs) before entering the fork loop.
sbx-zygote/src/fork_server.rs ForkServer::serve_forever(): recv ForkRequest → fork() → child path; ForkRequest{process_type, policy, channel: Handle}; reply = child ProcessId + reaped status over the request req_id.
sbx-zygote/src/seal.rs seal_child(pt: ProcessType, policy: &Policy) -> Result<(),SbxError>: order = set argv[0]/comm → unshare leftover ns if any → install per-type SeccompFilter (Policy::seccomp) last → only then exec-less jump into child entry.
sbx-sandbox (reuse) base policy = namespaces (USER/PID/NET/MNT/IPC/UTS) + no_new_privs + cap drop applied in the zygote; seccomp deferred to seal.
sbx-launcher/src/lib.rs LaunchOptions.use_zygote; ProcessLauncher holds an optional zygote Endpoint; launch() → if use_zygote: build (MessagePipe,MessagePipe) pair(), send one end as ForkRequest.channel via SCM_RIGHTS, return ChildProcess{pid, channel}; else fall back to Phase-3 clone/exec.
sbx-cli sbx spawn --zygote flag; sbx run starts the zygote at boot.

ForkServer protocol (request/response over the dedicated zygote pipe):

browser --ForkRequest{pt,policy,channel:Handle}--> zygote
zygote: fork()
  parent -> reply{pid} ; record pid for reaping ; loop
  child  -> close listen sock ; dup channel ; seal_child(pt,policy) ; run child entry
  • Reaping: zygote is the direct parent of forked children → it owns SIGCHLD/waitpid; it forwards exit/crash to the browser over IPC (Phase 11 consumes). Browser is NOT the parent of zygote-forked renderers — handled by re-parenting note in 6.8.
  • Channel handoff: only the renderer's IPC pipe end crosses via SCM_RIGHTS; the zygote never sees renderer payload traffic.

18.3 Deliverable

sbx run boots a zygote; sbx spawn --zygote --type renderer forks N renderers from it. Each is correctly sandboxed (sealed seccomp + base ns). cargo test -p sbx-zygote passes a spawn-latency test and a sandbox-sealed test. sbx reports per-spawn latency + shared/PSS RSS.

18.4 Acceptance Criteria

# Criterion Measure
A1 Zygote spawn latency p50 < 2 ms, p99 < 5 ms (fork+seal), vs Phase-3 exec baseline ≥ 4–8× slower
A2 COW RSS savings private/dirty per renderer < 1.5 MB; warm RX pages shared (PSS ≪ RSS) across ≥8 renderers
A3 Sealed sandbox forbidden syscall in a zygote-forked renderer → SIGSYS kill (SECCOMP_RET_KILL_PROCESS); identical disposition to Phase-5 exec'd child
A4 No-loosen invariant sealed filter ⊆ never wider than base; arch check present; no_new_privs set pre-seal (assert via /proc/self/status)
A5 Single-thread fork zygote thread count == 1 at fork point (assert)
A6 Channel integrity forked renderer can round-trip a hello Message on the passed channel; zygote cannot read it

18.5 Conformance Fixtures

Fixture Input Expected
zygote_fork_ok.txt ForkRequest{Renderer, baseline_policy, ch} child pid > 0; child exits 0; reply matches req_id
zygote_seal_renderer.json renderer policy post-seal /proc/PID/status: Seccomp: 2, NoNewPrivs: 1, CapEff: 0000…0
zygote_cow_rss.txt spawn 8 renderers sum private-dirty < 12 MB; shared RX present in /proc/PID/smaps_rollup
zygote_attack_forbidden.txt child calls socket(AF_INET,…) killed by SIGSYS; exit signal == SIGSYS; broker never contacted
zygote_malformed_req.txt truncated ForkRequest / bad num_handles SbxError::Ipc; no fork; zygote stays alive, serves next req
zygote_missing_handle.txt ForkRequest with num_handles=1 but no FD SbxError::BadHandle; no fork

18.6 Pitfalls

Pitfall Root cause Detect / debug
Multi-threaded zygote → deadlocked child fork() copies one thread but inherits locked mutexes held by other threads assert thread count==1 pre-fork; gdb on hung child shows wait on futex; keep zygote strictly single-threaded
Seal applied before dup channel seccomp may block needed dup/fcntl order: dup → seal-last; strace (pre-seal only) shows ENOSYS/SIGSYS site
FD leak across fork child inherits zygote's listen sock + others → confused-deputy / escape O_CLOEXEC on all zygote FDs except the explicit channel; close listen sock first-thing in child; audit /proc/PID/fd
Warm pages dirtied → COW breaks writing to a "read-only" warm region copies the page mprotect RX-only + mlock; check Private_Dirty in smaps does not grow per spawn
PR_SET_NO_NEW_PRIVS not inherited assumption wrong: it is inherited across fork, but seccomp filter is too — double-install bug install seccomp once per child; verify Seccomp: count
Zygote orphaned if browser dies children re-parented to init, sandbox persists but unsupervised PR_SET_PDEATHSIG=SIGKILL on zygote tied to browser; verify zygote exits when browser killed

18.7 Performance Concerns

  • fork() cost ∝ page-table size → keep zygote RSS small; warm only genuinely shared text, not heap.
  • Avoid fork()+exec entirely on the hot path (exec re-pays loader/relocation/TLS setup — the whole point).
  • Seccomp install (prctl) is the dominant post-fork cost (~tens of µs for a ~100-rule cBPF); precompile the filter in the zygote, ship the program bytes into the child — do not rebuild per fork.
  • Batch reaping; single epoll on the fork-server socket; no per-request thread.

18.8 Edge Cases

  • Re-parenting: zygote-forked renderers' parent is the zygote, not the browser → browser learns pid via reply; crash signal flows zygote→browser (Phase 11). Document the two-hop ownership.
  • fork() fails (EAGAIN/ENOMEM, rlimit RLIMIT_NPROC) → reply SbxError::SpawnFailed; zygote survives.
  • Policy strictly wider than base requested → reject (PolicyDenied): zygote may only tighten.
  • Non-renderer type (GPU/Network) requested from renderer zygote → allowed only if base ⊇ target; else launcher uses direct exec path.
  • Zygote restart: if zygote itself dies, launcher falls back to direct clone/exec until a new zygote is warmed.

18.9 Failure Analysis

Failure Blast radius Containment
Seal step skipped (bug) child runs untrusted code unsandboxed fail-closed: seal_child returns Err → child _exit(127) before entry; CI fixture A3 gates
Compromised renderer post-fork only that child's CapabilitySet broker re-validates (Phase 7); seccomp still enforced; siblings unaffected (separate address spaces post-COW-write)
Zygote (in TCB-adjacent role) compromised high — can fork arbitrary children zygote runs under base sandbox + minimal caps; it holds no broker authority; cannot open files/sockets itself
Stale warm pages (lib updated on disk) functional, not security versioned warm set; zygote recycled on browser version change

18.10 Operational Notes

  • Metrics: zygote_fork_latency_us (histogram), zygote_fork_total, zygote_fork_fail_total, renderer_private_dirty_kb, zygote_alive gauge.
  • Logs: each fork → {req_id, pid, process_type, seal_ok}; seal failures error level.
  • Tunables: --zygote-warm-libs, --max-renderers (rlimit NPROC), --no-zygote (force exec path for debugging/strace).
  • Debug: disable zygote to strace a renderer (seccomp blocks ptrace in sealed children); /proc/PID/smaps_rollup for COW audit; PDEATHSIG verified in sbx sandbox-test.
  • Security review gate: any change to seal.rs ordering or FD-close set requires re-running zygote_attack_forbidden + A3/A4.

19. Phase 7 — The broker (capability-validated resource access)

The renderer is NET-namespaced + seccomp-filtered: open/openat/connect/socket either ENOSYS/EACCES or unreachable. To touch a resource it MUST ask the privileged broker, which validates against the caller's CapabilitySet and returns ONLY a brokered Handle (FD) via SCM_RIGHTS — never a path/host string. This is layer-2 of defense in depth; seccomp is layer-1.

19.1 Objectives

  • sbx-broker: Broker trait + FileBroker / SocketBroker / ResourceBroker; one IPC-driven request loop in the TCB.
  • PolicyEngine: ProcessId -> CapabilitySet map; validate every BrokerOp deny-by-default; Denied(reason) is specific, never leaks privileged paths.
  • Renderer-asks-broker over sbx-ipc: BrokerRequest -> BrokerResponse; resource crosses ONLY as a validated FD (SCM_RIGHTS), never as a path/host.
  • Invariant: the broker NEVER trusts caller-supplied paths/hosts beyond glob/allow-list matching; it open/connects itself, then duplicates the FD inward.
  • Deliverable proof: sandboxed renderer reads an ALLOWED file + is DENIED a disallowed one, both through the broker.

19.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

Theory applied: P11 (Garfinkel 2003 — TOCTOU via path strings; pass FD not path to avoid the race class), P12 (Provos et al. 2003 — OpenSSH privilege-separation: monitor validates + grants, slave only requests), B4 (Anderson Security Engineering §4 — confused-deputy; broker must validate against the caller's identity, not caller-supplied authority), ADR-20 (capability-based brokering), ADR-06 (SCM_RIGHTS FD as the grant primitive). INV-10 (deny-by-default: unlisted BrokerOp → PolicyDenied, no implicit grant) and INV-11 (broker never returns a path, only a brokered FD) are established this phase.

File (crates/sbx-broker/src/) Contents
lib.rs Broker trait; re-exports; BrokerOp, BrokerRequest, BrokerResponse
ops.rs enum BrokerOp { OpenFile{path,mode}, Connect{host,port}, MapShm{bytes} }; wire (de)serialize from Message.payload
policy.rs PolicyEngine { caps: HashMap<ProcessId, CapabilitySet> }; validate(pid, &BrokerOp) -> Result<(), String>
file.rs FileBroker → open_file
socket.rs SocketBroker → connect
resource.rs ResourceBroker → memfd + seal
service.rs serve(pipe: &MessagePipe, &mut PolicyEngine) loop: recv Message → decode → validate → execute → reply

Signatures (no bodies beyond illustration):

pub trait Broker { fn service(&mut self, req: BrokerRequest) -> BrokerResponse; }
impl PolicyEngine {
    pub fn validate(&self, pid: ProcessId, op: &BrokerOp) -> Result<(), String>; // deny by default
}

validate looks up the caller's CapabilitySet by pid (absent ⇒ deny), then glob/allow-list-matches op against it per BrokerOp variant (OpenFile/Connect/MapShm) — never trusting raw request content over the matched grant.

  • FileBroker::open_file: after validate, canonicalize (realpath) → re-check glob on the canonical path (defeats ../ + symlink escape) → open(O_NOFOLLOW|O_CLOEXEC, mode∈{RO,RW}) → wrap raw fd in Handle.
  • SocketBroker::connect: getaddrinfo(host) → re-validate each resolved IP vs cap (DNS-rebind guard) → socket()+connect() non-blocking with timeout → Handle.
  • ResourceBroker::map_shm: memfd_create ≤ SharedMemory{max_bytes} cap → F_ADD_SEALS(SEAL_SHRINK|SEAL_GROW) → Handle.
  • service::serve: req_id echoed in reply header; from: ProcessId taken from the AUTHENTICATED channel binding (the host that owns the pipe), NEVER from payload — payload-supplied pid is ignored/rejected.
  • BrokerResponse::Handle(h) serialized to Message{ handles: vec![h] } → SCM_RIGHTS; Denied/Error carry zero handles.

19.3 Deliverable

sbx broker wired into sbx-launcher child handshake: every sandboxed child gets one broker MessagePipe. sbx attack --broker and an integration test spawn a real seccomp+namespace renderer that (a) reads /sandbox/allowed.txt via broker → bytes returned; (b) requests /etc/shadow → Denied. Renderer's own openat is independently blocked by seccomp.

19.4 Acceptance Criteria

# Assertion Measure
A1 Allowed OpenFile → renderer reads expected bytes via brokered FD content == fixture
A2 Disallowed OpenFile → Denied(reason), no FD in reply num_handles==0
A3 Renderer direct openat() → blocked by seccomp (EACCES/SIGSYS) independent of broker
A4 Unknown pid (no CapabilitySet) → Denied deny-by-default holds
A5 ..//symlink-to-/etc/passwd under allowed glob → Denied post-canonicalize escape blocked
A6 Denied reason contains no privileged absolute path of a different resource no info leak
A7 Broker survives 10⁶ malformed requests no panic, no FD leak (/proc/self/fd stable)

19.5 Conformance Fixtures

Fixture Input Expected
allow_open.json OpenFile{"/sandbox/allowed.txt",RO}, cap matches Handle; bytes==allowed.txt
deny_open.json OpenFile{"/etc/shadow",RO} Denied("OpenFile denied: /etc/shadow")
deny_traversal.json OpenFile{"/sandbox/../etc/passwd",RO} Denied (canonical mismatch)
deny_symlink.fixture symlink /sandbox/x→/etc/passwd, OpenFile{"/sandbox/x"} Denied (O_NOFOLLOW+realpath)
deny_no_caps.json request from pid with empty CapabilitySet Denied
deny_rebind.json Connect{"evil.test",443} resolving to 127.0.0.1 Denied (IP not in cap)
malformed/*.bin truncated header, num_handles=255, giant payload len, non-UTF8 path, unknown BrokerOp tag Error/Denied; broker never crashes
wrong_mode.json OpenFile{allowed,RW} but cap=RO Denied (mode escalation)

19.6 Pitfalls

  • TOCTOU symlink race: validate-then-open on a path lets attacker swap a symlink between calls. Root cause: path revalidated, not the opened inode. Fix: O_NOFOLLOW + open-then-fstat, or openat2(RESOLVE_NO_SYMLINKS|RESOLVE_BENEATH). Debug: strace shows open resolving outside root.
  • Confused deputy via payload pid: trusting from in payload lets renderer A request as renderer B's caps. Fix: bind ProcessId to the socket, ignore payload. Debug: log channel-owner vs payload pid mismatch.
  • FD leak in broker (TCB): error path forgets to close the opened fd before returning Denied/Error → exhausts RLIMIT_NOFILE, DoS the TCB. Fix: RAII Handle Drop closes; debug via /proc/<broker>/fd count under fuzz. Glob over raw (non-canonical) path: /sandbox/* matches /sandbox/../../etc literally — canonicalize first, re-glob canonical; debug: assert prefix-under-root post-realpath.
  • SCM_RIGHTS partial recv: short read drops the ancillary FD silently → renderer gets bytes-less reply or orphan FD in broker. Fix: MSG_CMSG_CLOEXEC, check cmsg_len, reject truncated (compare num_handles vs received cmsg count).
  • DNS rebinding: validate host string, then resolve later → resolved IP differs. Fix: resolve inside broker, validate the IP (dual-resolution log for debug).

19.7 Performance Concerns

  • Broker round-trip target < 50 µs (warm) per OpenFile on the local pipe; dominated by open()+sendmsg. Cold file open dominated by VFS, not IPC.
  • validate is O(globs in cap); keep CapabilitySet small (renderer ≈ 1–4 entries). Precompile globs once at grant time, not per request.
  • Single-threaded broker loop is fine (renderer I/O is rare); if hot, shard per-ProcessType worker — but the TCB stays minimal. No async runtime in the TCB (smaller attack surface).
  • SCM_RIGHTS dup is cheap (< 5 µs); avoid copying file bytes through the broker — pass the FD, renderer reads directly (zero-copy).

19.8 Edge Cases

  • Cap grants a glob to a dir that doesn't exist → open ENOENT → mapped to Error/BadHandle/errno, not Denied (distinguish policy-deny from OS-fail). Connect to allowed host that times out → Error, not Denied; renderer retries within its cap.
  • Renderer requests fd, then broker reply races renderer death → sendmsg EPIPE → broker drops fd (closes), no leak. MapShm request exceeds max_bytes cap by 1 byte → Denied (boundary); empty path / null byte in path → reject pre-canonicalize as malformed (Denied).
  • Concurrent requests same renderer (pipelined req_ids) → replies matched by req_id, never reordered to wrong caller.

19.9 Failure Analysis

Failure Detection Containment
Broker bug grants beyond cap red-team sbx attack + A1–A8 in CI seccomp layer-1 still blocks direct syscalls; defense-in-depth
Broker crash/panic child IPC disconnect; sbx-crash watches broker too broker panic == process-wide outage → treat as TCB fault, fail closed (deny all)
Renderer floods broker (DoS) request-rate metric per ProcessId per-renderer rate cap + budget; offending renderer killed, others unaffected
Leaked privileged FD to wrong renderer fuzz asserts FD-count + recipient binding FD scoped to authenticated channel; never broadcast
Path-canonicalization bypass deny_traversal/deny_symlink fixtures openat2 RESOLVE_BENEATH; build-fail if absent on MSRV kernel → documented floor

19.10 Operational Notes

  • Broker logs every decision: {pid, op, decision(allow|deny), reason} — the security audit trail; deny reasons sampled, never the file contents.
  • Capability grants are static per ProcessType profile in Phase 7 (dynamic grant/revoke deferred to Phase 8); broker reads the profile at child launch.
  • Metric hooks: broker_requests_total{op,decision}, broker_denied_total, broker_latency_us (feed Phase 12). Alert on anomalous denied spikes (probe attempt).
  • macOS/Windows brokering = PhaseDeferred; Linux SCM_RIGHTS path is primary. The broker binary is part of the TCB → reviewed at the same bar as sbx-browser; no untrusted parsing without a fuzz fixture.
  • Kill switch: if PolicyEngine cannot load a CapabilitySet, default is deny-all + log + sad-tab, never allow.

20. Phase 8 — Capability model and per-process security policy

Crates: sbx-core (Capability, CapabilitySet), sbx-broker (PolicyEngine enforcement). Builds on Phase 7 (broker plumbing) and Phase 5 (seccomp). Principal = SiteInstance. The capability layer is the second defense-in-depth layer (broker policy); seccomp is the first. A bug in one layer must not yield escape.

20.1 Objectives

  • Define Capability variants + CapabilitySet as the per-process authority record; deny-by-default (empty set = nothing brokered).
  • Author per-ProcessType default capability profiles: renderer = minimal (brokered specific files, no socket cap, no GPU); network = socket caps; GPU = GpuChannel + relaxed file caps; utility = minimal; storage = scoped file caps.
  • Bind capabilities to the principal (SiteInstance) at process creation; key PolicyEngine's ProcessId -> CapabilitySet map on the host's site instance.
  • grant/revoke (additive/subtractive) post-spawn; revoke is immediate (next BrokerOp re-checks live set).
  • Make every BrokerOp and every sensitive IPC route a least-privilege enforcement point — no resource access without a matching Capability.
  • Acceptance proven by a profile matrix test: each ProcessType can do exactly its allowed ops, nothing more.

20.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

Theory applied: B4 (Anderson Security Engineering §4 — least privilege, access control, object capability model), P12 (Provos et al. 2003 — smallest monitor surface, deny-by-default), ADR-20 (CapabilitySet as the per-process authority description), ADR-22 (drop all Linux capabilities; privileged ops only via broker). INV-12 (renderer default profile holds no OpenFile/ConnectSocket capability — verified against the profile matrix) is the primary invariant established this phase.

Module / file Adds
sbx-core/src/capability.rs Capability enum (below), AccessMode{Read,Write,ReadWrite}, CapabilitySet(BTreeSet<Capability>); CapabilitySet::allows(&self, op: &BrokerOp) -> bool — pure, OS-free predicate (no syscalls in sbx-core)
sbx-broker/src/profiles.rs fn default_profile(t: ProcessType, site: &SiteId) -> CapabilitySet — the profile matrix
sbx-broker/src/policy.rs PolicyEngine { caps: HashMap<ProcessId, CapabilitySet> }; register(pid, set), grant, revoke, check(req) -> Decision; enum Decision { Allow, Deny(reason) } wired into Broker::service (Phase 7) — check precedes any FD open

Capability matching semantics (LOCKED variants from spine):

pub enum Capability {
    OpenFile     { path_glob: String, mode: AccessMode },  // glob match + mode ⊇ requested
    ConnectSocket{ host: String, port: u16 },              // host glob + exact/0=any port
    SharedMemory { max_bytes: u64 },                       // requested len ≤ max_bytes
    ReadClipboard,                                         // presence
    GpuChannel,                                            // presence
}

allows(op) algorithm: linear scan of set; first matching Capability of the op's family → true. OpenFile: glob-match path_glob (canonicalized, no ..) AND granted.mode ⊇ requested.mode. ConnectSocket: host glob-match AND (cap.port == req.port || cap.port == 0). SharedMemory: req.len ≤ max_bytes. Presence caps: membership test. No match → Deny.

Profile matrix (default_profile):

ProcessType OpenFile ConnectSocket SharedMemory GpuChannel ReadClipboard
Renderer /sbx/cache/<site-hash>/** RO only — (none; net via Network proc) ≤ 64 MiB — —
Network resolv/cert bundle RO *:80,*:443 + configured ≤ 32 MiB — —
Gpu /dev/dri/** RW, shader cache RW — ≤ 256 MiB yes —
Utility — (none) — ≤ 8 MiB — —
Storage /sbx/profile/<site-hash>/** RW — ≤ 64 MiB — —
Browser n/a (TCB; not brokered) n/a n/a n/a n/a

Renderer's OpenFile glob is site-scoped (<site-hash> = blake3(SiteId)[..16]): two SiteInstances on different sites get disjoint path namespaces even at the capability layer. Set built at spawn in sbx-browser RenderProcessHost, passed to PolicyEngine::register(pid, profile) over the privileged-side call (never crosses the trust boundary as data the child can forge).

20.3 Deliverable

sbx policy show --type <renderer|network|gpu|utility|storage> [--site <url>] prints the resolved CapabilitySet. sbx policy check --type T --op '<broker-op>' returns Allow/Deny + reason, exit 0/1. PolicyEngine integrated into sbx-broker so every brokered op is gated. Distinct least-privilege sets per type, enforced.

20.4 Acceptance Criteria

  • Renderer profile: OpenFile{/etc/passwd, Read} → Deny; ConnectSocket{*, *} → Deny (no socket cap); OpenFile{/sbx/cache/<own-site>/x, Read} → Allow; OpenFile{.../x, Write} → Deny (RO).
  • Cross-site: renderer for a.com requesting b.com's cache path → Deny (glob disjoint).
  • Network profile: ConnectSocket{api.x:443} → Allow; ConnectSocket{ssh-host:22} → Deny; OpenFile{/dev/dri/0, RW} → Deny.
  • GPU profile: GpuChannel request → Allow; ConnectSocket{*} → Deny.
  • Utility profile: every brokered op → Deny (empty file/socket caps).
  • SharedMemory{len = max+1} → Deny on every type; len = max → Allow.
  • revoke(pid, cap) then re-issue same op → was Allow, now Deny (no caching of stale decision).
  • Profile matrix test green: matrix[type][op] expected ∈ {Allow,Deny} matches check for all cells.

20.5 Conformance Fixtures

Fixture Input Expected
profiles/renderer.golden default_profile(Renderer, "https://a.com") (debug-fmt) exact set; CI diff
profiles/matrix.csv rows = (type, op), col = expected drives matrix test
ops/deny_passwd.json renderer, OpenFile{/etc/passwd,Read} Deny("no OpenFile cap matches")
ops/deny_cross_site.json renderer@a.com, b.com cache path Deny("glob mismatch")
ops/allow_net_443.json + deny_shm_overflow.json network ConnectSocket{x:443}; utility SharedMemory{len=9MiB} Allow; Deny("exceeds max_bytes")
attack ops/glob_escape.json + null_byte.json renderer OpenFile{/sbx/cache/a/../../etc/shadow,Read}; OpenFile{"/sbx/cache/a/x\0/etc/passwd"} both Deny (canonicalize strips .. → outside glob; reject NUL/non-UTF8)
attack ops/port_zero_confuse.json renderer forging cap with port:0 N/A — child cannot supply caps; register is privileged-side only
ops/empty_set.json empty CapabilitySet, any op Deny (deny-by-default)

20.6 Pitfalls

  • Glob match before canonicalization → ../symlink traversal escapes site scope. Root cause: matching raw path. Fix: canonicalize (resolve .., reject symlinks crossing scope) then glob; reject relative paths. Debug: log canonical vs requested path on Deny.
  • mode ⊇ inverted (granting RW when only RO held). Root cause: subset/superset confusion. Fix: granted.contains_all(requested) — RO cap must NOT satisfy a Write op. Test both directions.
  • Capabilities sent from the child as IPC payload → renderer forges its own set. Fix: register is called only on the privileged side at spawn; the child never names a Capability — it names an op, broker holds the set. Profile built once, mutated globally (shared &mut CapabilitySet across pids) → revoke on one pid affects others (aliasing). Fix: per-pid owned CapabilitySet in the map.
  • port: 0 sentinel collides with "any port" vs "connect to port 0" (overloaded sentinel). Fix: 0 means wildcard; real port-0 connects are unsupported and denied (no realistic use). eTLD+1 not yet computed at register time (site hash from raw host) → www.a.com and a.com get different scopes. Fix: derive SiteId via Phase 9 public-suffix list before default_profile (ordering dependency).

20.7 Performance Concerns

  • allows() on critical path of every BrokerOp. Target: < 5 µs p99 per check (small set ≤ ~8 caps → linear scan fine; no alloc).
  • Glob compile: precompile per-cap glob to a matcher at register, cache in the cap (or set) — avoid recompiling per op. Canonicalization may stat/realpath; restrict to broker-side, cache resolved roots.
  • CapabilitySet is BTreeSet for deterministic golden output; membership is O(log n), n tiny.
  • No lock on the hot read path beyond the per-pid entry; use RwLock<HashMap> (read-mostly), or sharded map if broker is multithreaded.

20.8 Edge Cases

  • Empty set → all ops Deny (utility steady state, verified by empty_set.json). Duplicate/overlapping caps (two OpenFile globs, one a superset) → harmless; first match wins; dedup at insert.
  • SharedMemory{max_bytes: 0} → every shm request Deny (effectively no shm). Unicode/IDN host in ConnectSocket → normalize (punycode) before glob, else xn--… vs unicode mismatch.
  • Glob ** vs *: * does not cross /; site root must use ** to cover subdirs — documented, tested. Process type with no profile entry (future type) → default_profile returns empty set (fail-closed), not panic.

20.9 Failure Analysis

Failure Cause Detection Containment / Recovery
Over-grant (renderer gets net cap) profile bug matrix test red in CI block release; seccomp connect still ERRNO (layer-1 backstop)
Under-grant (renderer can't read own cache) glob too tight renderer functional test fails widen glob to site scope; never to global
Stale Allow after revoke decision cached revoke fixture never cache Decision; check live set per op
Forged cap accepted child-supplied set code review + port_zero_confuse enforce privileged-only register; child speaks ops only
Canonicalize bypass symlink in rootfs glob_escape fixture reject symlink-crossing; pivot_root rootfs is RO (Phase 4)

Defense in depth: even a PolicyEngine over-grant is partially backstopped by seccomp (e.g. a renderer with a forbidden connect is still ERRNO-blocked at layer 1), and vice versa — neither layer alone is the security guarantee.

20.10 Operational Notes

  • sbx policy show is the audit tool; CI snapshots *.golden so any profile change is a reviewed diff (capability changes = security-sensitive PRs).
  • Log every Deny with {pid, site, op, reason} at WARN (a flood of denies on a renderer = compromise/probe signal → feeds Phase 12 metrics + Phase 13 red-team). Allows logged at TRACE only (volume).
  • Profiles are data-driven (loadable from a signed config in future) but ship compiled-in defaults; hardening reviews target profiles.rs.
  • Principle: tighten by default; widening any profile requires explicit justification in the PR + a new matrix row. New ProcessType → must add a profile (compile-time exhaustive match on ProcessType forces it).

21. Phase 9 — Site isolation (process-per-site-instance)

Crate: sbx-site (OS-free policy; no IPC, no syscalls). Principal = SiteInstance. Consumes sbx-core (SiteId, BrowsingInstanceId, ProcessId). Output consumed by Phase 10 (sbx-browser) for navigation -> process assignment.

21.1 Objectives

  • SiteId derivation = scheme + "://" + eTLD+1 via a bundled public-suffix list (PSL); correct on foo.github.io (private suffix github.io -> site foo.github.io) and a.b.co.uk (ICANN suffix co.uk -> site b.co.uk).
  • SiteInstance / BrowsingInstance: instances that may script each other (window.open / same-BrowsingInstance) share one BrowsingInstance and one process; cross-BrowsingInstance never shares.
  • SiteIsolationPolicy: the authoritative SiteId -> ProcessId map + BrowsingInstanceId -> {SiteId -> ProcessId} overlay.
  • ProcessReusePolicy: process-per-site-instance until a process-count limit, then reuse an existing process for the same site (never co-locate distinct sites unless limit forces same-site reuse).
  • Cross-origin isolation framing (COOP/COEP intent) recorded on the instance; no enforcement engine here (NON-GOAL: real web engine).

21.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

Theory applied: P1 (Reis et al. USENIX Security 2019 — process-per-site-instance, eTLD+1 site definition, BrowsingInstance grouping, memory/perf cost data), P2 (Chromium Site Isolation Design — navigation→SiteInstance→process assignment, BrowsingInstance scripting rule), P9 (Kocher Spectre 2018 — speculative read across in-process origins; process isolation is the fix), B3 (Zalewski — site vs origin distinction), ADR-18 (process-per-site-instance granularity), ADR-19 (PSL for eTLD+1 derivation). INV-13 (distinct sites never co-resident in the same process unless LimitReached and emitted as a metric) is established this phase.

File (in sbx-site/src/) Contents
lib.rs re-exports; SiteIsolationPolicy struct + impl
psl.rs PublicSuffixList loaded from data/public_suffix_list.dat via include_str!; longest-rule match, ! exceptions, * wildcard
site.rs site_for(url) -> SiteId; URL parse -> scheme/host; IP-literal & opaque-origin handling
instance.rs SiteInstance, BrowsingInstance, allocation + scripting-group invariant
reuse.rs ProcessReusePolicy, assign(...), process-count limit logic
  • PSL match: split host into labels; find longest matching rule (exact or *), apply ! exception (suffix = rule minus first label); eTLD+1 = suffix + one preceding label. Tables compiled once into HashSet<&str> (rules) + wildcard/exception sets.
  • site_for (signature locked): pub fn site_for(&self, url: &str) -> SiteId. Algorithm: parse scheme+host; if host is IP literal or host == suffix (no registrable label) or scheme opaque (data:,about:,file:) -> SiteId(opaque-token); else SiteId(format!("{scheme}://{etld1}")).
  • Assignment (reuse.rs): assign(site, bi_id) -> (ProcessReusePolicy, ProcessId):
    1. existing process for (bi_id, site) -> ReuseExistingForSite;
    2. else if process_count < limit -> new ProcessId, ProcessPerSiteInstance;
    3. else pick an existing same-site process (LRU) -> ReuseExistingForSite; if none same-site -> LimitReached (caller co-locates per fallback, logged).
  • Data: sites: HashMap<(BrowsingInstanceId, SiteId), ProcessId>, procs: HashMap<ProcessId, SiteId>, next_pid, next_bi, limit: usize.
pub struct SiteIsolationPolicy { psl: PublicSuffixList, sites: HashMap<(BrowsingInstanceId, SiteId), ProcessId>,
    procs: HashMap<ProcessId, SiteId>, limit: usize /* default ~ memory-derived */ }
impl SiteIsolationPolicy { pub fn site_for(&self, url: &str) -> SiteId;
    pub fn assign(&mut self, site: &SiteId, bi: BrowsingInstanceId) -> (ProcessReusePolicy, ProcessId); }

21.3 Deliverable

  • sbx-site crate: PSL-backed site_for, SiteInstance/BrowsingInstance allocation, SiteIsolationPolicy map, ProcessReusePolicy.
  • sbx sites <url>... subcommand: prints url -> SiteId -> ProcessId (policy) for a navigation sequence; demonstrates distinct sites -> distinct PIDs, same-site/same-BI -> shared PID, reuse at limit.

21.4 Acceptance Criteria

# Assertion (measurable)
A1 site_for("https://a.b.co.uk/x") == SiteId("https://b.co.uk"); site_for("https://foo.github.io/") == SiteId("https://foo.github.io")
A2 https://a.example.com & https://b.example.com -> same SiteId -> same ProcessId in same BI; https://evil.com -> different ProcessId
A3 http://x.com vs https://x.com -> distinct SiteIds (scheme part of site) -> distinct processes
A4 Two SiteInstances in different BrowsingInstances for the same site -> distinct ProcessIds (no cross-BI sharing)
A5 With limit = N, the N+1-th distinct site forces ReuseExistingForSite/LimitReached, never a fresh PID; same-site nav always reuses
A6 site_for is pure/deterministic: identical input -> identical SiteId across runs

21.5 Conformance Fixtures

Fixture Input Expected
psl_multilabel.txt a.b.co.uk, x.y.github.io, plain.com, localhost b.co.uk, x.github.io, plain.com, opaque(localhost)
psl_exception.txt www.ck (!www.ck rule) site www.ck (exception shortens suffix)
site_map.json nav sequence + BI ids per-URL SiteId + assigned ProcessId
reuse_under_limit.json limit=2, 3 distinct sites, then same-site revisit PIDs {p0,p1,reuse} + revisit hits prior PID
malformed_urls.txt "", http://, https://1.2.3.4/, http://[::1]/, not a url, https://xn--n3h.com IP/empty/IDN -> opaque or punycode-normalized; no panic, Result/opaque token
attack_psl.txt host ...., 10k-label host, trailing-dot x.com., mixed-case X.CoM bounded work; trailing-dot stripped; case-folded; no OOM/no panic

21.6 Pitfalls

Pitfall Root cause Debug
foo.github.io co-located with bar.github.io treated github.io as ICANN eTLD, ignored private PSL section check PSL section flag; both must yield distinct sites
Trailing-dot / case bypass (x.com., X.com) -> map miss -> unintended new process host not normalized before lookup normalize (strip trailing ., lowercase) before PSL match
Cross-BI sharing leak map keyed by SiteId only, not (BI, SiteId) assert key tuple includes BrowsingInstanceId
Stale process reuse after child death procs not pruned on crash (Phase 11) on CrashHandler exit event, evict ProcessId from both maps
IP literal scripts another IP IPv4/IPv6 treated as registrable host force IP literals to per-origin opaque site
Unbounded PSL labels DoS linear scan over attacker host cap label count / host length pre-scan

21.7 Performance Concerns

  • site_for target < 5 µs (PSL HashSet lookups, no allocation beyond the result String); map assign O(1) amortized.
  • PSL loaded once at startup (include_str! -> parse < 10 ms, ~9k rules); zero per-call I/O.
  • limit default derived from RAM (e.g. min(physical_mem / ~250 MB, hard_cap)); above limit, reuse keeps process count bounded -> predictable memory.

21.8 Edge Cases

  • Opaque origins (about:blank, data:, file:): no eTLD+1 -> per-document opaque SiteId; about:blank inherits initiator's SiteInstance (same BI).
  • Single-label / PSL-tip host (com, bare TLD, localhost) -> no registrable domain -> opaque per-origin site.
  • IDN/punycode: normalize to ASCII (xn--) before match; never mix Unicode + ASCII forms.
  • Port ignored for site (site = scheme+host only); userinfo/path/query stripped.
  • https vs http and ws/wss -> scheme-distinct sites (no downgrade co-location).

21.9 Failure Analysis

Failure Impact Mitigation
PSL stale / missing rule two real sites share a process -> weakened isolation (security regression) pin PSL version + checksum in CI; fixture psl_multilabel.txt gates merge
Wrong eTLD+1 (over-broad) unrelated sites co-located -> blast radius widened exhaustive multi-label + exception fixtures
Map desync vs real PIDs navigation routed to dead/wrong process single source of truth in sbx-browser; crash evicts entries
limit too low excessive reuse -> distinct-site co-location tune via memory; surface as metric (Phase 12)

21.10 Operational Notes

  • Embedded PSL = build artifact (no network); update via bumping data/public_suffix_list.dat + checksum; version logged at startup.
  • sbx sites + tracing emit SiteId/ProcessId/policy per navigation for audit.
  • macOS/Windows: identical (OS-free crate) — no PhaseDeferred branches here.
  • Metrics exported to Phase 12: process_count, reuse_rate, distinct_sites, limit_reached_total.
  • Security note: site isolation is a blast-radius control (a compromised renderer holds only its site's data + CapabilitySet); it is not the syscall/broker boundary — defense in depth requires all three layers.

22. Phase 10 — The browser orchestrator (process hosts + IPC routing)

The capstone integration phase: sbx-browser becomes the single privileged TCB process that owns every child, drives navigation -> site -> process assignment, and multiplexes all frame traffic over per-process pipes via RouteId. All prior crates (launcher+zygote, broker, site, crash, ipc) wire together here behind one BrowserProcess.

22.1 Objectives

  • ProcessHost registry: all child hosts keyed by ProcessId; single owner of every ChildProcess + its MessagePipe.
  • RenderProcessHost: binds a renderer to its SiteInstance + the set of RouteIds (frames) it hosts.
  • IPC router: multiplex many RenderFrames over ONE per-process pipe via RouteId; demux inbound on the browser's event loop.
  • Navigation flow: url -> SiteIsolationPolicy.site_for -> SiteId -> SiteInstance -> assign/reuse a RenderProcessHost (ProcessReusePolicy) -> create RenderFrame route.
  • Wire sbx-broker (per-renderer PolicyEngine), sbx-site (assignment), sbx-crash (SIGCHLD/disconnect) into one supervised loop.
  • Invariant: router NEVER forwards a Message to a RouteId not owned by the sending RenderProcessHost -> cross-renderer frame injection denied.

22.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

Theory applied: P2 (Chromium Site Isolation Design — RenderProcessHost, navigation→SiteInstance→process assignment), P5 (Chromium Mojo — RouteId multiplexing: many frames over one pipe), R2 (Chromium content/browser/ — host registry, process reuse, IPC routing), ADR-23 (RouteId-keyed router bounds fd count). INV-14 (the router never delivers a Message to a RouteId not owned by the sending process — cross-renderer frame injection denied) is the critical invariant established here.

Modules under sbx-browser/src/:

File Contents
process_host.rs ProcessHost { hosts: HashMap<ProcessId, RenderProcessHost>, next_pid }; register/lookup/remove; owns pipes
render_process_host.rs RenderProcessHost { pid, site_instance, routes: Vec<RouteId>, pipe: MessagePipe, caps: CapabilitySet }
router.rs Router: RouteTable: HashMap<RouteId, ProcessId>; route_inbound, route_outbound, alloc_route
navigation.rs Navigator::navigate(url) -> RouteId; the assign-or-reuse algorithm
browser_process.rs BrowserProcess: event loop, owns ProcessHost+launcher+brokers+SiteIsolationPolicy+CrashHandler+Router

Key signatures (no bodies):

impl ProcessHost {
    fn register(&mut self, h: RenderProcessHost) -> ProcessId;
    fn host_mut(&mut self, pid: ProcessId) -> Option<&mut RenderProcessHost>;
    fn host_for_site(&self, si: &SiteInstance) -> Option<ProcessId>; // reuse lookup
    fn remove(&mut self, pid: ProcessId) -> Option<RenderProcessHost>; // on crash
}
impl Router {
    fn alloc_route(&mut self, owner: ProcessId) -> RouteId;        // unique, monotonic
    fn route_inbound(&self, from: ProcessId, m: &Message) -> Result<(), SbxError>; // owner check
    fn forward(&mut self, to: RouteId, m: Message) -> Result<(), SbxError>;
}
impl BrowserProcess {
    fn navigate(&mut self, url: &str) -> Result<RouteId, SbxError>;
    fn run(&mut self) -> Result<(), SbxError>;                      // poll-based event loop
}

Navigation algorithm (per URL): site = site_for(url) -> build/lookup SiteInstance in current BrowsingInstance -> ProcessReusePolicy: ReuseExistingForSite if a host already serves site and count < limit, else ProcessPerSiteInstance -> if spawn: ProcessLauncher.launch(LaunchOptions{Renderer, policy, use_zygote:true}) (COW fork from Zygote), seal sandbox, set per-PID CapabilitySet in PolicyEngine -> alloc_route(pid) -> push RouteId into host.routes + RouteTable -> send CreateFrame{route,url} to renderer.

Event loop: poll() over all per-process pipe FDs + broker socket + crash signalfd. Inbound Message: validate header.route ownership via RouteTable[route]==from; broker ops dispatched to Broker::service (validated vs that PID's CapabilitySet), brokered Handle returned via SCM_RIGHTS. POLLHUP/EOF on a pipe -> hand PID to CrashHandler.

22.3 Deliverable

sbx run --nav <url> --nav <url> ... (and sbx sites): a live BrowserProcess that, given a navigation sequence, spawns the correct sandboxed renderers, assigns/reuses per ProcessReusePolicy, routes per-frame Messages by RouteId, brokers each renderer's resource requests, and survives a forced child crash. Multi-navigation integration test green.

22.4 Acceptance Criteria

# Criterion (measurable)
A1 Navigations to a.com, a.com/x, b.com -> exactly 2 renderer processes (a.com reused, b.com new)
A2 Cross-site nav (a.com->b.com in same tab) swaps to a different ProcessId; old route torn down
A3 Message{route=R_b} arriving from renderer-A pipe -> route_inbound returns PolicyDenied, msg dropped, A NOT killed (logged)
A4 Each renderer's broker request validated vs its CapabilitySet; out-of-set op -> BrokerResponse::Denied
A5 Forced renderer crash -> ProcessHost.remove + sad-tab signal; browser + other renderers keep running (PIDs alive)
A6 RouteId never reused across PIDs within a run (monotonic alloc; no aliasing)
A7 50 sequential navigations -> 0 leaked FDs (per-PID pipe + brokered handles closed on host removal)

22.5 Conformance Fixtures

Fixture Input Expected output
nav_reuse.txt nav a.com, a.com/p, b.com 2 PIDs; site-map: {a.com->P1, b.com->P2}; P1.routes=2
nav_crossorigin.txt nav a.com then b.com (same tab) swap P1->P2; tab's route rebound; P1 reaped if last route
route_table.golden after nav_reuse RouteId->ProcessId table dump matches golden
attack_route_spoof.txt / attack_unknown_route.txt renderer-A sends route=<B's route>; inbound route=0xDEAD (unallocated) PolicyDenied/BadHandle respectively; dropped, no panic; A alive, counter route_owner_violation+=1
malformed_msg.bin truncated header / num_handles > actual FDs parse error; pipe quarantined; broker uncrashed
broker_overreach.txt renderer opens /etc/shadow (not in glob) Denied("/etc/shadow"); no FD passed

22.6 Pitfalls

Pitfall Root cause Debug
Cross-renderer message injection router trusts header.route without owner check assert RouteTable[route]==from per inbound; fixture attack_route_spoof
FD leak on host removal crash path drops RenderProcessHost but not its brokered Handles lsof//proc/self/fd diff per nav; explicit close in remove
Wrong reuse -> two sites share a process; event-loop starvation site_for not normalizing scheme/eTLD+1 (e.g. www. kept); one chatty renderer monopolizes poll log SiteId per nav, cross-check PSL output; per-PID fairness/round-robin, cap msgs/iteration, watch IPC-rate metric
Reaped PID still in RouteTable; RouteId reuse after PID death crash removal races inbound already-queued msg; non-monotonic alloc recycles ids epoch/generation tag on PID, drop msgs from dead PID; global monotonic counter, never recycle within run (A6)

22.7 Performance Concerns

  • Navigation-to-route latency target: < 12 ms reused host, < 25 ms cold (dominated by Zygote COW fork, see Phase 6).
  • Router demux: O(1) RouteTable lookup; outbound forward O(1). No per-message allocation on the hot path (reuse buffers).
  • Event loop: single epoll/poll set; FD-count scales with process count (target < 16 renderers, avoid O(n) scans per wakeup). Per-RenderProcessHost overhead: 1 pipe pair + small routes Vec; target browser-side overhead < 64 KB/host.

22.8 Edge Cases

  • Navigation to about:blank / opaque origin -> unique SiteInstance, no reuse (distinct principal).
  • Same-site sub-frame nav -> reuse parent's RenderProcessHost, new RouteId only.
  • Process-count limit hit (LimitReached) -> ProcessReusePolicy coalesces same-BrowsingInstance sites into an existing host.
  • Renderer dies mid-navigation (before CreateFrame ack) -> abort assignment, free pre-alloc'd RouteId, surface error. Two navigations to same site race -> single host created (idempotent host_for_site under the loop's single-thread invariant).
  • Renderer sends broker request for a RouteId it lost (frame closed) -> BadHandle, denied.

22.9 Failure Analysis

Failure Detection Containment
Renderer crash (SIGSEGV/SIGSYS) SIGCHLD + POLLHUP on pipe CrashHandler -> Minidump + ProcessHost.remove + sad-tab; browser unaffected (blast radius = 1 host)
Renderer IPC disconnect (clean exit/EOF) POLLHUP, no signal remove host, free routes, no Minidump; reload per RestartPolicy::ReloadTab
Malformed Message; spawn failure (zygote/launcher) wire parse error; Err(SpawnFailed) from launch quarantine that pipe, broker/TCB stays up (A3); navigation returns error, no partial host or leaked route
Router forwards to dead PID generation mismatch drop message; never write to closed FD (avoids SIGPIPE in TCB)

22.10 Operational Notes

  • sbx sites prints the live SiteId -> ProcessId map + per-host route count + CapabilitySet summary (audit aid).
  • Tracing: span per navigation (url, SiteId, assigned/reused, ProcessId, RouteId); cross-boundary trace-id propagated in MessageHeader.req_id lineage.
  • Metrics surfaced here, consumed by Phase 12: process_count, route_owner_violation, broker_deny_total, nav_latency_ms, host_removed_total.
  • TCB discipline: the browser loop is single-threaded over untrusted input; all renderer bytes treated as hostile; every route/broker decision is deny-by-default and logged. Renderer compromise stays bounded to its CapabilitySet (broker re-validates) — orchestrator grants no ambient authority.

23. Phase 11 — Crash handling and recovery (blast-radius isolation)

Crate sbx-crash. Detect child death (SIGCHLD/waitpid + IPC disconnect) → capture Minidump artifact → enforce renderer crash != browser crash → apply RestartPolicy → signal "sad tab" → distinguish crash from seccomp KILL (security event). Wires into sbx-browser's ProcessHost/RenderProcessHost from Phase 10.

23.1 Objectives

  • Blast-radius isolation (security): any child death (segfault, abort, OOM, SECCOMP_RET_KILL_PROCESS) leaves browser TCB + sibling renderers fully alive; no shared-fate.
  • Single-source crash detection: unify SIGCHLD/waitpid(WNOHANG) reaping and IPC EOF/ECONNRESET into one CrashEvent per ProcessId (dedupe; either edge may fire first).
  • Classify death cause: Exited{code} | Signaled{sig} | SeccompKill{nr?} | IpcLost → crash vs security (seccomp/sandbox kill logged distinctly).
  • Capture minimal Minidump (pid, signal, cause, /proc snapshot) before reaping clobbers state where possible.
  • RestartPolicy: Never | Restart{max, window_secs} (token-bucket backoff) | ReloadTab; emit "sad tab" on terminal failure.

23.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

Theory applied: B1 (TLPI ch.34 — SIGCHLD, signalfd, zombie-prevention), B6 (Love ch.10 — async-signal-safe signal handling in crash watcher), P6 (Barth et al. 2008 — renderer death must not propagate to browser; blast radius = renderer's CapabilitySet), ADR-24 (Minidump not full core dump). INV-6 (crash detected by ≥1 of three paths: SIGCHLD, IPC-disconnect, exit; browser process is never a victim of renderer death) is the governing invariant.

Files: sbx-crash/src/{lib,handler,minidump,policy,watcher,report}.rs. Core types (LOCKED): CrashHandler, Minidump, RestartPolicy.

pub enum CrashCause { Exited{code:i32}, Signaled{sig:i32}, SeccompKill{nr:Option<i64>}, IpcLost }
pub struct CrashEvent { pub pid: ProcessId, pub ptype: ProcessType, pub cause: CrashCause, pub at: Instant }
pub struct Minidump { pub pid:u32, pub signal:i32, pub cause:CrashCause,
    pub maps:Vec<MapLine>, pub stat:ProcStat, pub captured_at:SystemTime } // minimal artifact
pub enum SadTabReason { Crashed, Killed /*security*/, OutOfRetries }
impl CrashHandler {
    pub fn on_sigchld(&mut self) -> Vec<CrashEvent>;          // drains waitpid(-1, WNOHANG) loop
    pub fn on_ipc_disconnect(&mut self, pid: ProcessId);      // marks pending; confirmed by waitpid
    pub fn handle(&mut self, ev: CrashEvent) -> CrashAction;  // Minidump + policy -> action
}
pub enum CrashAction { Respawn(ProcessId), SadTab{pid:ProcessId, reason:SadTabReason}, Reaped }
  1. Watcher (watcher.rs): install SIGCHLD handler → write byte to a self-pipe/signalfd (async-signal-safe; no waitpid in handler). Browser event loop selects on signalfd + IPC pipes.
  2. Reap (on_sigchld): loop waitpid(-1, WNOHANG) until 0/ECHILD; decode WIFSIGNALED/WTERMSIG/WIFEXITED/WEXITSTATUS. Map SIGSYS (from seccomp KILL_PROCESS/TRAP) → SeccompKill.
  3. Minidump (minidump.rs): on signal death snapshot /proc/<pid>/{maps,stat} (best-effort, pre-reap; tolerate races); serialize to crashes/<pid>-<ts>.mini. No core dump (rlimit RLIMIT_CORE=0).
  4. Dedupe: on_ipc_disconnect sets pending[pid]; authoritative cause comes from waitpid. If IPC EOF without exit within T_grace (250 ms) → escalate SIGKILL then reap (zombie/hung-on-exit guard).
  5. Policy (policy.rs): per-RenderProcessHost token bucket (max restarts / window_secs). Under budget → Respawn (re-launch via Phase 10 path, fresh SiteInstance binding, new RouteIds). Over budget / SeccompKill / Never → SadTab.
  6. Security log + report (report.rs): SeccompKill → structured WARN (pid, ptype, site, nr, ip) to security log + CrashReport hook (pluggable sink; default = local file). Seccomp kills are never auto-respawned silently (loop = attack signal).
  7. Browser wiring: CrashAction returned to ProcessHost; on Respawn/SadTab it tears down the dead host's RouteIds in the router, frees brokered handles + CapabilitySet, notifies UI.

23.3 Deliverable

sbx crash-test --kind {segfault|abort|oom|seccomp} forces a renderer to die; browser detects it, writes a Minidump, shows a sad tab, stays up (process + other renderers healthy). sbx attack --seccomp-kill death classified as security event in the log.

23.4 Acceptance Criteria

# Assertion Target
A1 Browser PID + sibling-renderer PIDs alive after victim dies 100% (blast-radius contained)
A2 CrashEvent emitted for segfault, abort, OOM, seccomp-kill, IPC-EOF all 5 detected
A3 SeccompKill classified security, logged with nr+ip yes; not silently respawned
A4 Crash detect→action latency (SIGCHLD edge → sad-tab/respawn) p99 < 50 ms
A5 Restart{max=3,window=60s}: 4th crash in window → OutOfRetries sad tab exact, no infinite respawn
A6 No zombies leak; /proc/<pid> gone after handle 0 zombies
A7 Brokered handles + RouteIds of dead child freed 0 leaked FDs/routes

23.5 Conformance Fixtures

Fixture Input Expected
crash_segfault.txt renderer raises SIGSEGV Signaled{11}, sad tab, browser up
crash_seccomp_kill.txt forbidden syscall → RET_KILL_PROCESS SeccompKill, security WARN, no respawn
crash_oom.txt child exceeds RLIMIT_AS Signaled{9}/Exited, sad tab
restart_budget.txt 4 crashes/60 s, max=3 3 respawns → OutOfRetries
dedupe_ipc_then_sigchld.txt IPC EOF, then exit one CrashEvent, not two
ipc_eof_no_exit.txt (attack) child closes pipe, spins SIGKILL after 250 ms, reaped
minidump_schema.json post-segfault artifact valid: pid, signal=11, maps≥1, stat present
concurrent_crash.txt 3 renderers die same tick 3 distinct events, all reaped

23.6 Pitfalls

Pitfall Root cause Debug
waitpid in signal handler not async-signal-safe → deadlock/UB self-pipe/signalfd only; reap in loop
Lost SIGCHLD (coalesced) signals don't queue → multiple deaths, one signal always loop WNOHANG until 0/ECHILD, not one reap
Zombie leak EOF handled, exit not reaped reap is authoritative; IPC EOF only hints
Respawning a seccomp-kill treating security kill as transient crash classify first; SeccompKill → never auto-respawn
Minidump reads dead /proc /proc/<pid> vanishes at reap snapshot before reap; tolerate ENOENT gracefully
Reused PID confusion PID recycled before handling key on (pid, launch-epoch/start_time from stat)
Restart storm masks crash-loop bug unbounded respawn token bucket; surface crash-rate metric (Phase 12)

23.7 Performance Concerns

  • Crash path is cold — optimize for correctness, not throughput; budget p99 < 50 ms detect→act.
  • signalfd + level-triggered select: O(deaths) reaps per wakeup, no busy-poll.
  • Minidump capture bounded: cap /proc/maps lines (e.g. ≤4096), async write off the event loop → no browser stall.
  • Respawn reuses zygote COW fork (Phase 6) → re-spawn latency ~ fresh renderer (sub-10 ms target), not full exec.

23.8 Edge Cases

  • Browser itself faulting → out of scope (TCB); only abort+log, no self-restart.
  • Child dies during spawn/handshake (before RenderProcessHost registered) → reap, no sad tab, mark launch failed.
  • Double-source race: IPC EOF and SIGCHLD same tick → dedupe via pending map.
  • waitpid ECHILD (already reaped by stray handler) → idempotent no-op.
  • Zygote/fork-server death → fatal-ish: stop new spawns, signal degraded mode (not a single sad tab).
  • Crash while a brokered request is in flight → broker drops the FD, PolicyEngine clears caller's CapabilitySet.

23.9 Failure Analysis

Failure Detection Containment
Renderer compromise then crash SIGCHLD + cause blast radius = that CapabilitySet only; broker re-validates → no escalation
Seccomp kill loop (active attack) crash-rate spike, repeated SeccompKill same site OutOfRetries, security alert, stop respawning that SiteInstance
Hung child (no exit, no EOF) liveness ping timeout (Phase 12) SIGKILL → reap path
CrashHandler bug stalls loop watchdog on event loop fail-safe: log + keep browser responsive; missed reap ≠ escape
Report sink down hook returns err local-file fallback; artifact never lost

23.10 Operational Notes

  • Artifacts: crashes/<pid>-<ts>.mini; rotate/cap dir size; no PII / no core dumps (RLIMIT_CORE=0).
  • Logs: crashes at INFO; SeccompKill/sandbox-kill at WARN with nr+ip → feeds security monitoring.
  • Metrics (exported Phase 12): crash_total{ptype,cause}, respawn_total, sad_tab_total, seccomp_kill_total.
  • Tune RestartPolicy{max,window_secs} per ProcessType via config; default renderer Restart{3,60}.
  • Runbook: rising seccomp_kill_total for one site ⇒ probable exploit attempt — capture Minidump, inspect denied nr, do not relax policy.

24. Phase 12 — Resource management and observability

Per-process budgets (mem/CPU/fd via rlimits) + priority + memory-pressure-driven ProcessReusePolicy; a browser-only metrics + cross-process trace surface. Defense-in-depth angle: budgets cap a compromised renderer's resource-exhaustion blast radius; tracing/metrics live ONLY in the TCB (children emit no logs — untrusted output is a forgery + side-channel vector). PhaseDeferred: cgroup v2 budgets (rlimits are the portable floor; cgroups noted, not built).

24.1 Objectives

# Objective Verifiable as
O1 Per-ProcessType rlimit budgets (RSS via RLIMIT_AS, CPU RLIMIT_CPU, fds RLIMIT_NOFILE, procs RLIMIT_NPROC=0 post-zygote) set in child pre-exec over-budget child OOM-killed, never browser
O2 Process priority: renderer nice +5 (background), GPU/network nice 0; cgroup weight = PhaseDeferred note nice reflected in /proc/<pid>/stat
O3 ProcessReusePolicy reacts to memory pressure: over watermark → ReuseExistingForSite instead of new process new same-site nav reuses host under pressure
O4 Browser exposes metrics: process-count-by-type, crash rate, seccomp-kill count, IPC msgs/s + bytes/s, broker req/s + deny rate, spawn-latency histogram, per-process RSS sbx metrics dumps all; Prometheus text
O5 Cross-process trace: 128-bit TraceId propagated in MessageHeader so a navigation is followable browser→renderer→broker trace assembles ≥3 spans across boundary
O6 Structured logging in browser ONLY; children silent grep child stdout/stderr = empty

24.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

Theory applied: B1 (TLPI ch.36 — RLIMIT_AS/RLIMIT_CPU/RLIMIT_NOFILE, prlimit(2)), proc(5) (smaps_rollup PSS for per-process memory sampling), ADR-12 (deny-by-default; budgets are enforcement not advisory). The Back-of-Envelope Capacity Model derives the renderer RSS budget from §4 C-8/C-9; those derived numbers feed the RLIMIT_AS value set here. INV-7 (per-process budget applies before untrusted work; over-budget renders browser unaffected) is the governing invariant.

  • sbx-core/src/budget.rs — pub struct ResourceBudget { as_bytes: u64, cpu_secs: u32, nofile: u32, nproc: u32, nice: i8 }; pub fn default_for(t: ProcessType) -> ResourceBudget (renderer 512 MiB/∞cpu/64fd/0proc/+5; gpu 1 GiB/0; network 256 MiB/0). OS-free; no syscalls here.
  • sbx-sandbox/src/rlimit.rs — extend Rlimits with from_budget(&ResourceBudget); applied in the child between pivot_root and seccomp seal via setrlimit(2) (hard==soft so child can't raise). nproc=0 blocks fork() bombs after zygote split.
  • sbx-launcher — LaunchOptions { budget: ResourceBudget, .. }; priority() calls setpriority(PRIO_PROCESS, 0, nice) pre-exec; record spawn_started_at Instant → emit SpawnLatency sample on handshake-complete.
  • sbx-browser/src/metrics.rs — pub struct Metrics { registry: Registry }; counters/gauges/histograms (table §28.1); Counter::inc, Histogram::observe. RSS sampler thread polls /proc/<pid>/statm (page0 × pagesize) per host every 1 s. seccomp_kills incremented from sbx-crash when child exit = SIGSYS.
  • sbx-browser/src/trace.rs — pub struct TraceId(pub u128); pub struct SpanId(pub u64); added to MessageHeader (extends Phase 1 wire: +24 B header). Span { trace, parent, name, start, dur }; browser begins root span on navigation, stamps header; renderer/broker echo trace/parent back. Collector in browser only.
  • sbx-browser pressure loop — MemoryPressure { warn_pct: 80, critical_pct: 90 } from /proc/meminfo MemAvailable; on warn → SiteIsolationPolicy::process_reuse = ReuseExistingForSite; on critical → also refuse non-essential spawns (SpawnFailed). Hysteresis: drop back at < warn-10.
  • sbx-cli — sbx metrics (Prometheus text exposition to stdout); sbx trace <nav-id> prints assembled span tree.

24.3 Deliverable

sbx run --load <profile> drives N navigations across M sites; prints the full metric set, a spawn-latency histogram, and shows ProcessReusePolicy flipping to reuse when MemAvailable crosses the watermark. sbx metrics emits Prometheus text; sbx trace assembles a cross-boundary span tree. Budgets enforced: an over-RSS child is OOM-killed in isolation. Children produce zero log output.

24.4 Acceptance Criteria

ID Criterion Threshold
A1 Renderer allocating > as_bytes is killed (ENOMEM/OOM); browser survives, "sad tab" fires child dies, browser RSS flat
A2 RLIMIT_NPROC=0 blocks post-zygote fork() in renderer fork() → EAGAIN, no new pid
A3 Spawn-latency histogram p50 ≤ 5 ms (zygote path), p99 ≤ 15 ms from O1 samples, ≥1000 spawns
A4 Under memory pressure (MemAvailable < 80%) a new same-site nav reuses an existing host (process count flat) reuse counter +1, spawn 0
A5 Trace assembles ≥3 spans (browser→renderer→broker) sharing one TraceId span tree depth ≥3
A6 Metric set complete + monotonic counters; deny_rate = denied/total ∈ [0,1] all 8 metrics present
A7 Child stdout/stderr empty over a full load run byte count == 0

24.5 Conformance Fixtures

File Asserts Expected
fixtures/metrics/expected_names.txt metric registry surface sbx_process_count{type}, sbx_crash_total, sbx_seccomp_kill_total, sbx_ipc_msgs_total, sbx_ipc_bytes_total, sbx_broker_req_total{op,verdict}, sbx_spawn_latency_seconds (hist), sbx_process_rss_bytes{pid}
fixtures/load/profile_pressure.toml N=50 navs, fake MemAvailable=75% reuse triggers; process_count ≤ limit; reuse_total > 0
fixtures/trace/nav_resource.json golden span tree 3 spans, shared TraceId, parent links valid
fixtures/budget/oom_renderer.toml renderer mallocs 1 GiB w/ 512 MiB cap exit SIGKILL/OOM; crash_total{type=renderer}+1
Attack: fixtures/attack/metric_forgery renderer writes fake counts to its stderr/IPC browser ignores; metrics derive ONLY from TCB-side counters
Attack: fixtures/attack/trace_inject renderer returns oversized/garbage TraceId clamped/dropped; collector never panics, no unbounded alloc
Malformed: fixtures/trace/truncated_header.bin header missing trace bytes parsed as no-trace; message still routes

24.6 Pitfalls

Pitfall Root cause Debug
RLIMIT_AS kills child on benign mmap (thread stacks, guard pages) RLIMIT_AS caps virtual addr space, not RSS; jemalloc/glibc reserve huge VAS budget on RLIMIT_AS is coarse → prefer cgroup memory.max (PhaseDeferred); set generous AS, real cap via cgroup
Reading /proc/<pid>/statm after child death → stale/ENOENT race pid reaped between sample + read sampler must hold the ChildProcess handle, skip on ENOENT, never re-poll a reaped pid
Trace TraceId becomes a covert channel renderer-chosen id leaks/encodes data to broker logs TCB OWNS the id; renderer only echoes; never log renderer-supplied free-form fields
Reuse-under-pressure violates site isolation consolidating cross-site into one host breaks the security model reuse ONLY within same SiteId; cross-site NEVER merges, even under critical pressure → A4 must keep sites separate
Metrics scrape blocks the IPC router synchronous registry lock on hot path counters lock-free (atomics); scrape snapshots, never stalls routing
setrlimit order vs seccomp seccomp may deny setrlimit if set after seal apply rlimits BEFORE installing the filter (matches Phase 4/5 ordering)

24.7 Performance Concerns

  • Metric counters = relaxed atomics on IPC/broker hot path → < 5 ns/op; no lock, no alloc per message.
  • RSS sampler = 1 Hz, O(processes); reads statm (one short read) not smaps (slow) → < 1 ms/process.
  • Trace overhead = +24 B/header + one Instant::now() per span; sampling ratio knob (default 1.0 for tests, 0.01 prod) to bound collector memory.
  • Pressure poll = /proc/meminfo @ 1 Hz; hysteresis prevents reuse-flap thrash.
  • Spawn-latency histogram = fixed log buckets (0.5/1/2/5/10/15/50 ms) → O(1) observe.

24.8 Edge Cases

  • MemAvailable unreadable (restricted /proc) → fail to critical (conservative: prefer reuse) + log once.
  • Process count already at limit when reuse engages → LimitReached; queue/block nav, never spawn over budget.
  • Child exits before first RSS sample → no process_rss_bytes series emitted (no zero-fill).
  • nproc=0 would block zygote's OWN fork → budget applied in the FORKED child post-split, NOT the zygote.
  • Histogram on clock non-monotonicity (suspend/resume) → use Instant (monotonic), clamp negative to 0.
  • Trace overflow: > max spans/trace → drop tail, increment trace_dropped_total, never grow unbounded.

24.9 Failure Analysis

Failure Detection Containment Recovery
Renderer resource-exhaustion (mem/cpu/fd bomb) rlimit kill / OOM budget caps blast to that child only SIGCHLD → Minidump → RestartPolicy (Phase 11)
Fork bomb attempt RLIMIT_NPROC=0 fork()→EAGAIN, no children spawn none needed; seccomp clone arg-filter is 2nd layer
Metrics-subsystem panic supervised metrics thread isolated thread; IPC router unaffected restart sampler thread; counters persist
Pressure misread → over-spawn process_count vs limit invariant hard LimitReached ceiling refuse spawn; degrade to reuse
Trace collector unbounded growth trace_dropped_total rising per-trace span cap + ring drop oldest; alert on drop rate

24.10 Operational Notes

  • Metrics: Prometheus text via sbx metrics; key alerts → seccomp_kill_total rate (sandbox-escape attempt signal), broker deny_rate spike (compromised-renderer signal), crash rate.
  • Logs: structured JSON, browser-only; include trace_id; NEVER log renderer-supplied payload bytes (untrusted).
  • Tuning knobs: per-ProcessType budgets + nice in config; pressure watermarks (80/90) + hysteresis; trace sample ratio.
  • cgroup v2 (memory.max, cpu.weight, pids.max) = PhaseDeferred; rlimits are the portable enforcement floor shipped now.
  • Runbook: rising seccomp_kill_total → pull sbx trace for the offending route, inspect Minidump, treat as a security event, not a perf regression.

25. Phase 13 — Security validation, conformance, fuzzing, and benchmarks

The security done gate. Everything built in P0–P12 is now adversarially proven: a malicious renderer (the sbx attack red-team harness) attempts every forbidden syscall/file/socket/IPC op from inside a real sandboxed child — each MUST be denied or kill the child, asserted by the harness. seccomp dispositions are conformance-tested per rule; the TCB (broker + IPC framing + PSL) is fuzzed (malformed Message/BrokerRequest never crash/confuse it); C-1..C-12 are benchmarked with regression gates. One bypass or one TCB crash fails the build (C-SEC-*, C-AV-4).

25.1 Objectives

  • sbx attack red-team harness: from a real seccomp+namespace renderer, attempt forbidden ops → assert denied/killed (zero escapes); machine-checkable verdict per attempt.
  • seccomp conformance: every SyscallRule in the baseline profile → observed disposition matches Allow/Errno(e)/Trap/KillProcess exactly (no rule un-exercised).
  • TCB fuzzing: sbx-ipc framing + sbx-broker BrokerOp decode + sbx-site PSL → no panic/UB/leak/hang on arbitrary bytes (C-SEC-1, C-AV-4).
  • Benchmarks vs §4 targets (C-1..C-12) with CI regression gates (fail on >X% drift).
  • Published security report (escape matrix) + benchmark report (target vs measured); both are release artifacts.

25.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

Theory applied: P3 (Chromium Linux Sandboxing — the security "done" gate: attack harness proving no syscall/file/socket escape), P7 (gVisor — defense-in-depth structure; filter + broker = two independent layers), ADR-26 (cargo-fuzz on IPC deserializer + red-team attack subcommand), ADR-11/ADR-12 (the cBPF filter being tested). I1/I2 from §7 (filter installed before untrusted code; broker never crashes on malformed input) are the invariants this phase verifies empirically with the attack harness and fuzz corpus.

File Contents
crates/sbx-cli/src/attack.rs `sbx attack [--syscall
crates/sbx-renderer/src/attack_payload.rs in-child probes behind cfg(attack): raw syscall(nr,..), traversal BrokerOp, malformed Message emit; reports own outcome over IPC
crates/sbx-sandbox/tests/seccomp_conformance.rs table-driven: install baseline filter in forked child, invoke each SyscallRule.nr, capture errno/WTERMSIG==SIGSYS
fuzz/fuzz_targets/ipc_message.rs cargo-fuzz/libFuzzer: bytes → Message::decode → assert `Ok
fuzz/fuzz_targets/broker_request.rs bytes → BrokerOp decode + PolicyEngine::validate (no real open)
fuzz/fuzz_targets/psl.rs arbitrary host → SiteIsolationPolicy::site_for; never panic/alloc-blowup
benches/spawn.rs benches/ipc.rs benches/broker.rs benches/shm.rs Criterion; emit JSON → bench-gate compares vs targets.toml
crates/sbx-cli/src/report.rs renders security-report.md (escape matrix) + bench-report.md

Core types:

struct AttackCase { name: &'static str, class: AttackClass, expect: Expect } // Expect: Denied|Killed(Signal)|Errno(i32)
enum AttackClass { Syscall, FileTraversal, OutOfCapBroker, IpcAbuse, ArchBypass, NsEscape }
enum Verdict { Contained, ESCAPED }            // ESCAPED is fatal: exit nonzero, fail CI
struct EscapeMatrix { cases: Vec<(AttackCase, Verdict)> } // -> security-report.md
  • Attack execution model: harness (browser side, TCB) launches a child whose attack_payload runs each probe in a fresh forked grandchild (so a KillProcess SIGSYS kills only that probe, not the reporter); grandchild outcome (exited(code) / signaled(SIGSYS) / errno) is relayed to the harness, which maps to Verdict.
  • Syscall probes (renderer profile forbids): ptrace, execve/execveat, socket(AF_INET), raw open/openat of /etc/passwd, mount, unshare(CLONE_NEWUSER), keyctl, bpf, clone(CLONE_NEWNS), kexec_load, process_vm_readv on the broker pid → each SIGSYS(KILL) or Errno.
  • ArchBypass: invoke a syscall via the x32 ABI (nr | __X32_SYSCALL_BIT) + 32-bit int 0x80 path → filter's arch-check-first must KillProcess (proves §4.4 / P5 bypass guard).
  • NsEscape: attempt to re-unshare/setns out of pid/net/mnt ns, read /proc/1/root, traverse via .. past pivot_root → all blocked (EACCES/SIGSYS).
  • Broker probes: path-traversal (/sandbox/../etc/shadow), symlink escape, out-of-cap OpenFile/Connect, payload-spoofed from: ProcessId, oversized MapShm → all Denied (re-uses P7 fixtures, now asserted through a live sandbox).
  • IpcAbuse: send Message with num_handles=65535, payload.len > cap, truncated header, dangling req_id, FD-count mismatch vs cmsg → broker replies Error/tears down channel, stays up (poll broker liveness after).
  • Conformance harness asserts the filter is complete: any SyscallRule.nr with no probe → test fails (unexercised_rules.is_empty()), preventing silent policy rot.
  • bench-gate: parse Criterion JSON, compare p50/p99 vs targets.toml (C-1..C-12); fail if regressed > 10% or above the §4 hard ceiling.

25.3 Deliverable

sbx attack --all exits 0 ⇔ every AttackCase is Contained (zero ESCAPED); prints the escape matrix. cargo fuzz run <target> -- -max_total_time=<budget> finds 0 crashes for the CI budget; seccomp_conformance is green; cargo bench && bench-gate passes all C-1..C-12. CI publishes security-report.md + bench-report.md. This is the project's security gate: red without all four → no merge/release.

25.4 Acceptance Criteria

# Assertion Measure
A1 All Syscall/ArchBypass/NsEscape cases → KillProcess(SIGSYS) or Errno; 0 ESCAPED escape_matrix.escaped()==0
A2 x32/ia32 ABI probe → KillProcess (arch check fires) grandchild WTERMSIG==SIGSYS
A3 All OutOfCapBroker/FileTraversal → Denied, no FD returned num_handles==0
A4 All IpcAbuse cases → broker alive after (responds to ipc-ping) liveness probe ok
A5 seccomp conformance: each rule's disposition == declared; unexercised_rules==0 table 100%
A6 IPC/broker/PSL fuzz → 0 crashes/hangs/UB for CI budget (≥ 5 min/target, ASan+UBSan) sanitizer clean
A7 Fuzz: broker fd-count + browser RSS stable over corpus (no leak) /proc/self/fd flat
A8 C-1..C-12 within target; none above hard ceiling bench-gate pass
A9 A single injected escape (mutation test) makes sbx attack fail gate is sensitive

25.5 Conformance Fixtures

Fixture Input Expected output
attack/syscall_cases.toml ptrace, execve, socket(AF_INET), open(/etc/passwd), mount, unshare(NEWUSER), bpf, keyctl, process_vm_readv→broker each Killed(SIGSYS)|Errno; matrix all Contained
attack/arch_bypass.toml `nr __X32_SYSCALL_BIT; int 0x80` openat

seccomp/expected_dispositions.csv every baseline SyscallRule.nr observed == declared disposition
fuzz/corpus/ipc/*.bin + fuzz/corpus/psl/*.txt valid+truncated+giant-len+0xFF handle-count+non-UTF8 path; .., a.b.c...co.uk, 1e5-label host, IDN/punycode, empty Ok|Err never panic; bounded SiteId, no panic/OOM
bench/targets.toml + reports/security-report.golden.md C-1..C-12 p50/p99+ceilings; escape-matrix render bench-gate diff ≤10%/≤ceiling; byte-stable (modulo timestamp)

25.6 Pitfalls

  • Self-defeating harness — probe kills the reporter: a SIGSYS(KILL) on the renderer's main thread takes the IPC reporter with it → harness sees disconnect, can't distinguish "contained" from "harness bug". Root cause: probe + reporter share a process. Fix: probe in a forked grandchild, reporter waitpids + relays WTERMSIG. Debug: if every case reports "disconnect", the fork split is missing.
  • False-green: sandbox not actually applied: harness runs the payload unsandboxed (forgot policy/use_zygote) → forbidden syscalls "succeed" but harness mislabels expectation, or worse passes. Root cause: launch path bypassed Sandbox::apply. Fix: assert post-seal state (no_new_privs==1, caps empty, seccomp mode==2) before probing; abort if unsealed. Debug: grep Seccomp /proc/<pid>/status must be 2.
  • Fuzzer can't reach the parser: target wraps Message::decode but a length pre-check rejects all inputs first → 100% coverage of the guard, 0% of the decoder → false "0 crashes". Root cause: fuzz harness mis-models the wire boundary. Fix: feed bytes at the exact recv boundary; track coverage, gate on decoder-region hit. Debug: cargo fuzz coverage shows cold decode arms.
  • Errno-vs-Kill ambiguity: a syscall returning ENOSYS could mean "seccomp Errno" OR "kernel lacks it" → conformance test passes for the wrong reason. Fix: pin disposition per nr in expected_dispositions.csv; for KILL rules assert SIGSYS, not errno. Debug: compare against seccomp audit log (SECCOMP_RET_LOG).
  • Benchmark measures the wrong thing: spawn bench includes process teardown / first-touch page faults → p50 inflated, gate flaps (timing window too wide). Fix: time fork→seal→handshake only; warm zygote; pin CPU, disable turbo, drop caches between cold runs. Debug: variance > 20% ⇒ machine noise, not regression.
  • Fuzz FD/mem leak masked by short runs: a per-message FD leak in the broker only OOMs after 10⁶ inputs; a 30 s fuzz misses it. Fix: dedicated long-soak job asserts /proc/<broker>/fd + RSS flat over ≥ 10⁷ iters (plot fd-count slope; nonzero ⇒ leak). TOCTOU in attack timing: traversal probe races broker canonicalization, intermittent pass. Fix: deterministic fixture (pre-created symlink), repeat N×, require all-deny (flaky-only-under-load ⇒ revisit P7 openat2 RESOLVE_BENEATH).

25.7 Performance Concerns

  • Benchmarks isolate the measured op: spawn = fork→seal→handshake (excl. teardown); IPC RT excl. connection setup; broker excl. cold VFS — else gates flap on noise.
  • CI gate tolerance 10% over rolling baseline + absolute §4 ceiling; pin taskset, disable turbo/SMT, nice -20, warm caches; ≥ 30 samples, report p50/p99 not mean.
  • Fuzzing is the long pole: parallel libFuzzer jobs (-jobs=$(nproc)), persistent corpus cached across CI runs (coverage-guided convergence); per-target time budget, not iteration count.
  • Red-team harness latency dominated by spawn (one sandboxed child per class, ~10 ms warm) — batch all cases of a class into one child where isolation permits to keep sbx attack --all < ~2 s.

25.8 Edge Cases

  • Kernel lacks KILL_PROCESS (< 4.14) → fall back assert KILL_THREAD/SIGSYS; document floor; don't false-fail.
  • Unprivileged userns disabled on CI host → harness MUST SpawnFailed loudly (fail closed), never silently probe an unsandboxed child (would pass vacuously) — A-fail with actionable message.
  • Fuzzer discovers an input that is valid but pathological (max num_handles within cap) → not a crash; assert bounded resource use, not rejection. PSL fuzz: host with 10⁴ dots / 64 KB label → site_for returns in O(labels), no quadratic blowup, no stack overflow (recursion → iteration).
  • Bench on a 1-core CI runner → C-1 (warm fork) still meaningful; throughput targets (C-5/C-6) scaled or marked machine-dependent, gated on ceiling only. A new syscall added to baseline profile without a conformance row → unexercised_rules non-empty → test fails (forces probe coverage; prevents policy rot).

25.9 Failure Analysis

Failure Detection Containment / response
Real sandbox escape (forbidden op succeeds) sbx attack Verdict::ESCAPED release-blocking; both layers reviewed; ship only after fix + new fixture
TCB panic on fuzzed Message/BrokerOp libFuzzer crash + minimized repro add repro to corpus; fix to Err, never panic (C-AV-4); regression-gated
Conformance drift (disposition ≠ declared); perf regression > 10% / over ceiling seccomp_conformance diff; bench-gate JSON diff profile reverted/updated + CSV synced, gate blocks merge; bisect commit, revert or justify w/ updated targets.toml (review-gated)
Slow leak (fd/RSS) under soak long-fuzz slope monitor trace to RAII Handle Drop / cmsg path; fix + soak re-run
Harness false-green (unsealed child); arch-bypass slips (x32 allowed) pre-probe seal assertion; A2 fail abort harness, treat as P-fail until launch path proven sealed; re-order filter (arch check first, P5 invariant), add ABI fixture

25.10 Operational Notes

  • sbx attack --all + fuzz smoke + bench-gate are required CI checks; security-report.md (escape matrix) + bench-report.md published per run + on releases (the auditable security posture).
  • Red-team suite is append-only: each disclosed Chromium/Firefox sandbox-escape class (TOCTOU, confused-deputy, ABI bypass, ns-escape, fd-smuggling) gets a permanent AttackCase — the suite is the institutional memory of "what must stay denied".
  • Long-soak fuzz runs nightly (≥ 10⁷ iters/target, ASan+UBSan+RUSTFLAGS=-Zsanitizer); PR runs the 5-min smoke; corpus persisted as a cache artifact. macOS/Windows backends are PhaseDeferred: their attack/conformance cases assert Err(SbxError::Sandbox) at apply (must not pretend to confine), so a stub never reads as a pass.
  • Gate sensitivity is itself tested (A9): a CI mutation-test job injects one policy hole and asserts sbx attack fails — a green suite that can't catch an escape is worthless.
  • Reports feed the §28.4 dashboards: attack_cases_total{verdict}, fuzz_crashes_total, bench_p50_us{metric}; alert on any nonzero escaped/crashes.

Threat Model

The single most important section: this project's only reason to exist is to contain a hostile renderer. Method: STRIDE-flavored, asset-centric, assuming arbitrary code execution (ACE) inside any child.
