We do not model bugs inside the renderer (it is presumed fully owned); we model what that owned renderer can reach. Every threat below carries Vector → Mitigation → Detection → Telemetry → Recovery, maps to an invariant (INV-n, §7) and the phase that lands it.

Trust boundaries

  TCB (trusted, memory-safe Rust)            UNTRUSTED (assume ACE)
  +-----------------------------+            +--------------------------+
  | browser/broker process      |  <==IPC==> | renderer / GPU / network |
  | sbx-browser ProcessHost      |  SCM_RIGHTS| / utility children       |
  | sbx-broker PolicyEngine      |  sbx-shm   | sbx-renderer (stand-in)  |
  | sbx-site, sbx-launcher,      |            | seccomp + ns + caps=0    |
  | sbx-zygote, sbx-crash        |            +--------------------------+
  +-----------------------------+   ===== TRUST BOUNDARY =====
  Above the line: full authority (real uid 0 capabilities at spawn time, opens any file/socket).
  Below the line: zero ambient authority; everything mediated by seccomp (deny) OR broker (validate).
  • TCB = the browser/broker process, full stop. Its compromise = total compromise; sized to be small, memory-safe, and deps-audited (T9).
  • Every child is untrusted, uniformly — renderer most so; GPU/network have relaxed (not absent) sandboxes and are therefore also in-scope attackers, not trusted helpers.
  • The trust boundary is the IPC socket + the broker API. Bytes crossing it are adversarial. The TCB acts on no un-validated child input (INV-3).
  • Two-layer rule (INV-14): seccomp (kernel) AND broker (TCB) each fail-safe alone — defeating one must not yield escape.

Adversary model

Adversary Capability assumed Out of scope
A1 Compromised renderer ACE in child: arbitrary syscalls, arbitrary IPC bytes, arbitrary broker ops, can read its own address space —
A2 Malicious web origin Controls page content → drives A1; mounts cross-site read attempts (Spectre-class) —
A3 Malicious IPC peer Sends malformed/oversized/forged Message, fd floods, bogus route/num_handles —
A4 Hostile co-tenant / local user Other UID on host probes our sockets, /proc, memfds Root on host (host root ⇒ game over)
A5 Supply chain Malicious/buggy crate pulled into TCB Compiler/std/kernel backdoor

Explicitly trusted: the Linux kernel, the seccomp/namespace implementation, rustc/std, hardware. Explicit non-goals (no defense claimed): host kernel LPE via an unpatched kernel bug reachable from the allowed syscall set (we shrink the set, we don't fix the kernel); a real renderer engine's own bugs (#260/#305); physical/DMA attacks.

Assets (what the attacker wants)

A. Host filesystem (creds, keys, other users' data) · B. Network egress (exfil, pivot) · C. Cross-site data in other renderers (the Spectre prize) · D. The TCB itself (own the browser ⇒ own everything) · E. Host availability (DoS).

Threats, mitigations, detection, recovery

T1 — Sandbox escape via a forbidden syscall (→ asset A/B/D)

  • Vector: A1 invokes a syscall outside the renderer's allow-set (mount, ptrace, keyctl, bpf, clone(CLONE_NEWUSER), open, socket, …) to touch the host or kernel.
  • Mitigation: sbx-sandbox baseline renderer policy (Phase 5) — deny by default; allow-set is a small audited whitelist; everything else → KillProcess (not Errno, for the dangerous class) so an exploit chain dies rather than silently degrades. Layered with namespaces+caps=0 (Phase 4) so even an allowed syscall has empty authority. INV-1, INV-2.
  • Detection: SECCOMP_RET_KILL_PROCESS raises SIGSYS; sbx-crash SIGCHLD watcher observes WIFSIGNALED == SIGSYS. Trap variant logs nr/arch/ip via the async-signal-safe SIGSYS handler (§17 5.6) before _exit.
  • Telemetry: seccomp_kill_total{type,nr} (any nonzero in prod = P0 incident, not noise) · seccomp_trap_total{nr} · trace event seccomp.violation{nr,arch,ip}.
  • Recovery: child is already dead by the kernel; browser treats as a crash → "sad tab" (T-crash path), RestartPolicy. A killed-on-syscall renderer is never auto-restarted into the same navigation more than max times (T7) — repeated SIGSYS from one origin ⇒ flag the origin, stop reloading.

T2 — Syscall-ABI bypass (x32 / ia32 compat) (→ defeats T1)

  • Vector: issue the same logical syscall under a different ABI — x86_64 x32 (__X32_SYSCALL_BIT 0x40000000) or ia32 compat (int 0x80) — reaching an unfiltered syscall number, the classic seccomp bypass.
  • Mitigation: the compiled cBPF's first instructions load seccomp_data.arch, JEQ AUDIT_ARCH_<expected> else RET KILL_PROCESS, before any nr comparison; plus a JGE 0x40000000 → KILL guard for the x32 bit. Compiler emits this prologue unconditionally — not optional (INV-2).
  • Detection: unit test asserts compiled prog's first 3 insns == [LD arch, JEQ expected, RET KILL]; integration test fires an __X32_SYSCALL_BIT-tagged call and expects kill.
  • Telemetry: seccomp_arch_reject_total · seccomp_install_total{arch_guard=true}.
  • Recovery: as T1 (process dead). Build-time panic if the prologue invariant is absent — a missing arch gate can never ship.

T3 — Escape via the broker (path traversal · out-of-capability · symlink TOCTOU) (→ asset A/B)

  • Vector: A1 cannot open()/connect() itself (seccomp denies, NET ns has no egress), so it asks the broker — then tries to over-reach: OpenFile{path:"../../etc/shadow"}, a path outside its path_glob, a ConnectSocket to an un-granted host, or a symlink/.. that resolves outside the grant (TOCTOU between check and open).
  • Mitigation: PolicyEngine (Phase 7/8), deny by default. Pipeline per BrokerRequest: (1) look up caller's CapabilitySet by kernel-attested ProcessId, never by anything in the request; (2) realpath()/openat2(RESOLVE_NO_SYMLINKS|RESOLVE_BENEATH) to canonicalize and pin under the grant root — reject any resolve that escapes; (3) match canonical path against the Capability::OpenFile{path_glob,mode} (mode too: no write to a read grant); (4) open in the TCB, pass back only the FD via SCM_RIGHTS — the renderer never receives a path (INV-4, INV-13). openat2 closes the symlink-TOCTOU window (resolution is atomic & kernel-enforced).
  • Detection: every denied request logged with caller pid, op, reason; conformance + RED-TEAM (sbx attack) assert traversal/over-cap/symlink all → Denied.
  • Telemetry: broker_request_total{op,decision} · broker_denied_total{op,reason} (traversal/out_of_cap/symlink/mode) · span broker.service{from_pid,op,decision}.
  • Recovery: request returns BrokerResponse::Denied(reason); renderer survives (a denial is expected adversary behavior, not a crash). A spike in broker_denied_total for one pid ⇒ alert; optionally kill the child after a denial-rate threshold (anomaly = likely exploit probing).

T4 — IPC attack on the TCB (malformed Message · fd flood) (→ asset D, the crown jewel)

  • Vector: A3 sends crafted bytes at the broker's pipe to corrupt/crash/RCE the TCB: oversized payload length, integer-overflow/underflow on the length field, num_handles larger than the fds actually sent (or vice-versa), unknown route/req_id, partial frames, SCM_RIGHTS fd-exhaustion flood (send fds faster than they're consumed → TCB hits RLIMIT_NOFILE).
  • Mitigation: sbx-ipc wire decoder is fully bounds-checked (INV-9): every length validated <= MAX_MESSAGE (e.g. 64 KiB payload) before allocation; num_handles <= MAX_HANDLES_PER_MSG (e.g. 8) and must equal the count of fds in the cmsg (mismatch → drop+close all, error); no trust in any length to size a copy (checked-arithmetic, no unsafe slicing); per-pipe inbound fd budget (cap concurrent unconsumed fds, e.g. 64) so a flood gets EMFILE-resilient backpressure not OOM; received fds get CLOEXEC immediately. The decoder is memory-safe Rust → a parse bug is a clean Err, not a corruptible buffer (the entire rationale for Rust in the TCB).
  • Detection: the fuzz gate (Phase 13): cargo-fuzz on the Message decoder + handle-attach path — must run N CPU-hours with zero crashes before the security "done" gate. Decode errors counted, never panic! on attacker input (panics are converted to SbxError::Ipc at the boundary).
  • Telemetry: ipc_decode_error_total{reason} (oversize/underflow/handle_mismatch/unknown_route/short_frame) · ipc_fd_budget_reject_total · ipc_bytes_in · span ipc.decode.
  • Recovery: a malformed Message ⇒ drop the message and close that pipe (the peer is hostile); the child is then treated as crashed (T-crash) → sad tab. The TCB process is never taken down by child input (INV-3). One bad pipe never affects sibling routes.

T5 — Cross-site data theft via side channels (Spectre/Meltdown-class) (→ asset C)

  • Vector: A2 cannot read cross-origin data through the language, so it uses a transient-execution side channel to read whatever is in the same address space — including other origins' data if co-located.
  • Mitigation: Site Isolation (Phase 9) is the architectural answer: site = scheme + eTLD+1 via the public-suffix list; process-per-site-instance ⇒ cross-site data is never in-process, so there is nothing in-address-space for a side channel to leak (INV-8). This is mitigation-by-architecture, not a per-channel patch (we don't chase individual gadgets). BrowsingInstance groups script-connected SiteInstances (they may leak to each other and that is acceptable — same trust domain).
  • Detection: invariant test: no two distinct isolated sites map to one ProcessId in ProcessHost; SiteIsolationPolicy::site_for conformance vs PSL fixtures (incl. *.github.io, multi-level suffixes).
  • Telemetry: site_process_assignments_total{decision} · cross_site_colocation_violations_total (must be 0) · gauge processes_by_site.
  • Recovery: none needed at runtime if the invariant holds; a colocation-violation assertion is a build/test failure, never shipped. Process-limit pressure (T7) must degrade by reuse-within-site, never by co-locating cross-site — the limit yields a queued/blocked navigation, not a broken isolation guarantee.

T6 — Privilege escalation via the zygote / a shared fd (→ asset D)

  • Vector: the zygote is pre-forked with more authority than a sealed renderer; A1 races to act before its fork seals the sandbox, or abuses an fd/page inherited from the zygote (e.g. the fork-server socket, a shared mapping) to reach the broker with elevated trust or to influence sibling forks.
  • Mitigation: seal-before-untrusted-work (INV-10, Phase 6): a ForkServer-spawned child finishes tightening its seccomp + dropping any zygote-only fds before returning to renderer code; the zygote itself holds minimal authority (no broker write-capability, no host fds beyond what every renderer needs). The fork-server control socket is closed in the child post-fork (CLOEXEC + explicit close). COW pages are read-only (INV-1); no shared writable state between zygote-siblings. Each child gets its own IPC channel + its own CapabilitySet keyed on its ProcessId — siblings can't reuse each other's grants.
  • Detection: post-seal self-check (debug) reads /proc/self/status Seccomp==2, CapEff==0, and an fd sweep confirms only the channel fd remains; RED-TEAM forks then immediately probes a forbidden syscall → expects kill.
  • Telemetry: zygote_fork_total{type} · zygote_seal_verified_total · sandbox_apply_verified_total{type} (shared with INV-1).
  • Recovery: seal failure ⇒ child abort() (fail closed, INV-5) — a child that cannot prove it sealed never runs untrusted work.

T7 — Resource-exhaustion DoS (fork bomb · memory bomb · fd bomb) (→ asset E)

  • Vector: A1 tries to take down the host/browser: fork()/clone() loop, unbounded allocation, fd leak, CPU spin.
  • Mitigation: rlimits per child (Phase 4/12): RLIMIT_NPROC (caps fork bomb — often =0/1 for a single-threaded renderer), RLIMIT_AS/RLIMIT_DATA (memory budget, e.g. renderer cap 512 MiB–1 GiB), RLIMIT_NOFILE (fd cap), RLIMIT_CPU / cgroup CPU weight; PID-namespace bounds visible processes. Global process cap in sbx-site (ProcessReusePolicy::LimitReached) bounds total renderer count regardless of one child. Broker request rate limiting per pid bounds broker-side amplification.
  • Detection: getrusage/cgroup polling per child (Phase 12); OOM-kill observed via SIGCHLD; rlimit hits return EAGAIN/ENOMEM/EMFILE to the child (contained).
  • Telemetry: process_memory_bytes{pid,type} · process_count{type} · child_oom_kills_total · broker_request_rate{pid} · spawn_rejected_limit_total.
  • Recovery: an over-budget child is killed by the kernel (rlimit/OOM) → sad tab; the host and TCB are unaffected because the budget is per child and the global cap is enforced in the TCB. New navigations over the process cap block/queue, they don't crash.

T8 — Confused-deputy against the broker (→ asset A/B)

  • Vector: A1 tricks the privileged broker into using its authority on the attacker's behalf — by forging the requester identity, smuggling a privileged path, or relaying through another child.
  • Mitigation: the principal is the validated caller, never the request content. The PolicyEngine derives identity from the kernel-attested peer (ProcessId bound to the connection at spawn, optionally cross-checked via SO_PEERCRED), and validates ops against that principal's CapabilitySet (INV-4). The broker holds no ambient "do what you're told" mode — there is no request field that can name a different principal or a path outside the grant (T3 canonicalization applies). FDs are returned to the requesting connection only.
  • Detection: RED-TEAM attempts a request claiming another pid's grant / a relayed open → Denied; conformance asserts identity comes from the socket, not the payload.
  • Telemetry: broker_denied_total{reason=identity|out_of_cap} · span attr from_pid (always the attested pid).
  • Recovery: Denied; child survives; rate/anomaly alerting as T3.

T9 — Supply-chain compromise of the TCB (→ asset D)

  • Vector: A5 lands malicious or memory-unsafe code in a dependency that runs inside the TCB, defeating the whole model from within.
  • Mitigation: minimal, audited dependency surface — the TCB leans on std + nix/libc only (the locked syscall surface); cargo-deny (deny.toml) bans unvetted licenses, duplicate/yanked crates, and flags cargo-audit advisories in CI; #![forbid(unsafe_code)] in the IPC decoder & policy crates where feasible, audited unsafe elsewhere; reproducible builds; the TCB and the untrusted renderer share as little code as possible to shrink the trusted surface. Memory-safe Rust removes the C++ memory-unsafety class that dominates Chromium sandbox CVEs (the project's core rationale).
  • Detection: CI gate: cargo-deny check + cargo-audit must pass; dependency diff reviewed on every bump; cargo-geiger tracks unsafe count (must not grow silently).
  • Telemetry: build-time (advisory count, unsafe LOC, dep count) surfaced in CI dashboards, not runtime metrics.
  • Recovery: a failing deny.toml/audit gate blocks the merge — supply-chain risk is caught pre-ship, not in prod.

T10 — Host-environment / co-tenant attacks (→ asset A/D)

  • Vector: A4 (another UID, or a process in the same host) connects to our UDS, scans our memfds in /proc/<pid>/fd, or reads /proc to map the TCB.
  • Mitigation: UDS lives in a private dir (0700, owner-only) or an abstract/SO_PEERCRED-checked socket — the broker rejects connections whose peer UID ≠ expected; memfd seals (F_SEAL_SHRINK|GROW|WRITE, Phase 2) make shared buffers tamper-evident/immutable to a co-tenant; the child's PID namespace + pivot_root hide the host /proc; the TCB sets PR_SET_DUMPABLE=0 to deny /proc/<pid>/mem reads by non-root.
  • Detection: connection-time peer-cred check logs rejects; seal-violation mmap(PROT_WRITE) on a sealed buffer → EPERM (observable).
  • Telemetry: ipc_peer_reject_total{reason=uid} · shm_seal_set_total.
  • Recovery: rejected connections dropped; sealed-buffer write attempts fail at the kernel. Host root is out of scope (stated): nothing below the kernel can defend against it.

Residual risk & assumptions (stated, not hidden)

Residual Why accepted / mitigated-not-eliminated
Kernel LPE via an allowed syscall We minimize the allow-set; a 0-day in an allowed syscall is the kernel's surface, not ours. Reduce by allow-set audits.
Side channels within one BrowsingInstance Same-site/script-connected docs share a process by design (T5) — accepted trust domain.
GPU/network process relaxed sandbox Larger allow-set ⇒ larger surface; mitigated by still-dropped caps + brokering + their own (looser) policies. Tracked as higher-risk children.
macOS/Windows backends PhaseDeferred stubs — no confinement claim off-Linux; the security "done" gate is Linux-only.
Compiler / std / hardware trust Explicitly trusted; out of model.

Validation hook

Every threat T1–T10 has a corresponding assertion in the Phase 13 RED-TEAM harness (sbx attack) and/or the fuzz/conformance gates. The security "done" gate (§25) is: for each Tn, the harness demonstrates the forbidden action is denied or kills the child, and the TCB survives every malformed-IPC fuzz iteration. A threat without a passing red-team assertion is treated as unmitigated.

Failure Modes & Recovery

Taxonomy of every distinct failure the process model can exhibit. Severity ranking (highest first): (S0) child running unsandboxed, (S0) broker over-grant, (S0) cross-site co-location — these are silent security regressions that defeat the entire design; everything else degrades availability, not the trust boundary. Each entry: symptoms / root causes / detection / auto-recovery / manual recovery / operator actions / forensic artifacts / long-term mitigation.

27.1 Severity model

Sev Class Definition Auto-restart?
S0 trust-boundary breach sandbox absent/weakened, capability over-grant, isolation collapse never — halt or fail-closed
S1 security event confined attack detected (seccomp kill, broker deny storm) no — quarantine + alert
S2 availability child crash/hang/leak; browser + other children survive yes, per RestartPolicy
S3 capacity process-count / memory-pressure limits hit shed/queue, no restart

Invariant: fail closed. Any uncertainty in the sandbox/broker/site path → deny, abort the child, never proceed unconfined or over-granted. SbxError::Sandbox/PolicyDenied are non-recoverable for that child.

27.2 Failure catalogue

F1 — Renderer crash (segfault / abort / SIGILL) — S2

Field Value
Symptoms child exits on SIGSEGV/SIGABRT/SIGBUS/SIGILL; IPC pipe EOF; route stops responding
Root cause renderer-internal bug (untrusted code); OOM-killer; stack overflow; not a security event per se
Detection CrashHandler SIGCHLD watcher → WIFSIGNALED with non-SIGSYS signal; MessagePipe read → 0 bytes
Auto-recovery mark RenderProcessHost dead; emit sad tab on its RouteIds; apply RestartPolicy::Restart{max,window_secs} or ReloadTab; broker drops the dead ProcessId's CapabilitySet
Manual recovery operator reloads tab; if crash-loop hits max in window_secs → RestartPolicy falls back to permanent sad tab (no thrash)
Operator actions inspect Minidump; check crash-rate metric; correlate by site/URL
Forensic artifacts Minidump{pid, signal, registers-ish, maps snapshot}; last N IPC messages on the route; spawn timestamp
Long-term mitigation crash-rate SLO + alerting; per-site crash dashboards; flaky-site quarantine

F2 — Seccomp kill (forbidden syscall) — S1 (security event, NOT blind-restartable)

Field Value
Symptoms child exits on SIGSYS; seccomp_data.nr + arch recorded by Disposition::Trap/Log path before KillProcess
Root cause (a) renderer compromised → attacker probing forbidden syscall; OR (b) legitimate code path not in baseline policy (policy gap)
Detection SIGCHLD watcher distinguishes SIGSYS from F1 signals → routes to the security-event channel, not the plain crash channel
Auto-recovery child stays dead; do NOT auto-restart blindly — restart only after triage classifies (b); (a) → quarantine the site, raise alert
Manual recovery if policy gap (b): add the syscall (narrowest SyscallRule + arg_filter) to the baseline; re-run conformance; redeploy. If attack (a): no restart; investigate
Operator actions review syscall nr/arch/args[6]; check whether x32/ia32 ABI was used (arch mismatch ⇒ bypass attempt)
Forensic artifacts SIGSYS log line {pid, site, nr, arch, ip, args}; policy version in effect; preceding broker requests
Long-term mitigation keep baseline tight (deny-by-default); never widen reactively without attack re-run; alert on any SIGSYS in prod

F3 — Sandbox fails to apply (namespace / seccomp setup error) — S0, gravest

A child that runs unconfined = full sandbox escape by construction. The setup sequence is ordered and each step is fatal-on-error; there is no degraded "partial sandbox" mode.

clone(USER|PID|NET|MNT|IPC|UTS)  -> err? abort child (SbxError::Sandbox)
write uid/gid maps               -> err? abort
pivot_root(minimal rootfs)       -> err? abort   (never run in old root)
set rlimits                      -> err? abort
prctl(PR_SET_NO_NEW_PRIVS,1)     -> err? abort   (MUST precede seccomp)
drop ALL capabilities            -> err? abort
install SeccompFilter (cBPF)     -> err? abort   (LAST gate before untrusted exec)
--- only here: exec/seal untrusted work ---
Field Value
Symptoms Policy::apply returns Err(SbxError::Sandbox); child never reaches the post-seal barrier; launcher sees abnormal early exit
Root cause kernel lacks unprivileged userns (distro sysctl kernel.unprivileged_userns_clone=0); missing rootfs; seccomp ENOSYS; CAP drop fails; arch-check assembled wrong
Detection the child self-verifies before doing untrusted work (handshake "sealed" ack to browser); browser treats missing seal-ack within timeout as F3, kills child, refuses the route. Launcher never marks a child READY until the sealed-ack arrives
Auto-recovery fail closed: child _exits non-zero before exec of untrusted logic; browser does NOT route work to it; navigation surfaces an error, not an unconfined tab
Manual recovery none at runtime — this is a deployment/host defect; fix host (sysctl, rootfs) and redeploy; never ship a "disable sandbox" flag to prod
Operator actions run sbx sandbox-test on the host as a pre-flight; verify no_new_privs, userns, seccomp availability; gate startup on it
Forensic artifacts which setup step failed + errno; kernel version; sysctl snapshot; seal-ack timeout log
Long-term mitigation startup self-test refuses to launch any renderer if sandbox-test fails (whole browser fails to start rather than run children open); CI runs sandbox-test on the target kernel; the sealed-ack handshake is mandatory, not advisory

Anti-pattern (explicitly forbidden): on sandbox-setup error, falling back to spawning the child without confinement to "preserve functionality". That converts S0 into a live escape. The only correct response is abort-child / fail-startup.

F4 — Broker over-grant (policy bug grants too much) — S0, silent

Field Value
Symptoms none visible at runtime — BrokerResponse::Handle returned for a request that should have been Denied; renderer obtains a capability beyond its CapabilitySet
Root cause PolicyEngine logic bug; over-broad Capability::OpenFile{path_glob} (e.g. /**); default profile too permissive; grant not scoped to the caller's ProcessId/SiteInstance
Detection not observable from normal operation → caught by (1) the attack red-team suite asserting each forbidden op is Denied; (2) policy diffing — snapshot each ProcessType default CapabilitySet, diff vs golden, fail CI on widening; (3) broker audit log review
Auto-recovery none — a bug, not a fault. Damage is bounded only by seccomp (defense-in-depth): a brokered FD still can't be used via a syscall seccomp denies
Manual recovery tighten Capability/glob; re-derive default profiles; bump policy version; redeploy
Operator actions run sbx attack + sbx policy --diff every release; review every BrokerOp that returned Handle against expected glob
Forensic artifacts broker audit trail {from ProcessId, BrokerOp, granted/denied, matched Capability, glob}; policy version; red-team pass/fail diff
Long-term mitigation deny-by-default; narrowest globs; golden-profile regression test; principle that two layers (broker policy AND seccomp) must both fail for real impact — never let one layer's grant be unbounded

F5 — Cross-site co-location (site-mapping bug) — S0, isolation breach

Field Value
Symptoms two documents with different sites (scheme+eTLD+1) share one RenderProcessHost/ProcessId outside the same BrowsingInstance
Root cause wrong eTLD+1 derivation (stale/short public-suffix list → a.co.uk+b.co.uk collapsed); ProcessReusePolicy::ReuseExistingForSite matching on registrable domain incorrectly; navigation→SiteInstance assignment bug
Detection attack/site-isolation conformance: assert distinct sites ⇒ distinct ProcessId; invariant assertion in BrowserProcess on every assignment (site_for(url) must equal host's SiteInstance.site for reuse); PSL freshness check
Auto-recovery assignment-time invariant violation ⇒ refuse reuse, spawn a fresh process (fail toward more isolation), log breach
Manual recovery fix PSL / site_for; re-run site conformance; force-evict mis-keyed hosts
Operator actions verify PSL version + auto-update; review site→process map for unexpected sharing
Forensic artifacts site→process map snapshot; PSL version + hash; the two URLs + derived SiteIds
Long-term mitigation pinned, auto-updated public-suffix list with staleness alarm; assignment invariant always on (even in release); cross-origin isolation conformance in CI

F6 — IPC disconnect / half-open pipe — S2

Field Value
Symptoms MessagePipe read returns 0 (peer closed write end) or ECONNRESET; outstanding req_id never answered
Root cause child died (see F1/F2/F3) without clean shutdown; socket buffer/peer fd closed; one direction shut
Detection treat EOF/half-open exactly as a crash; reconcile with SIGCHLD (pipe-EOF without a reaped child within timeout ⇒ escalate, possible hang F9)
Auto-recovery tear down the route; fail all pending requests with SbxError::Ipc; trigger F1 recovery path (sad tab + RestartPolicy)
Manual recovery reload tab; none if crash-loop capped
Operator actions check whether SIGCHLD also fired (consistent) or not (suspicious — investigate hang/zombie)
Forensic artifacts last MessageHeaders on the route; pending req_id set; correlate with crash artifacts
Long-term mitigation every blocking IPC has a timeout; never treat a dropped pipe as benign; pipe close ⇒ route invalidation

F7 — Handle leak / double-close across the trust boundary — S1/S2

Field Value
Symptoms fd exhaustion (EMFILE/ENFILE) in browser/broker; SbxError::BadHandle; passing an already-closed fd via SCM_RIGHTS
Root cause Handle ownership bug (missed close on the send side after SCM_RIGHTS dup, or close-after-send race); untrusted child sends junk num_handles / mismatched ancillary data
Detection fd-count metric per process; Handle is move-only (Rust ownership prevents most double-close in TCB); validate num_handles vs received ancillary count on every Message decode
Auto-recovery reject malformed Message (mismatch num_handles ↔ SCM_RIGHTS fds) ⇒ SbxError::Ipc, treat as F6; broker enforces fd budget per ProcessId
Manual recovery restart leaking child; patch ownership bug
Operator actions watch fd-high-water metric; audit Handle send/recv sites
Forensic artifacts fd count timeline; the rejected Message header; ancillary-data length mismatch log
Long-term mitigation Handle owns + closes its fd (RAII, no Copy); decode validates handle count strictly; per-process fd rlimit caps blast radius

F8 — Zombie / un-reaped child — S2

Field Value
Symptoms defunct <defunct> processes; PID-table growth; CrashHandler exit-status never collected
Root cause SIGCHLD missed/coalesced (multiple deaths, single signal); reaping not looped; PID-namespace init responsibilities
Detection SIGCHLD handler loops waitpid(WNOHANG) until -1 (coalescing-safe); periodic sweep reconciles ProcessHost registry vs live PIDs
Auto-recovery reap-loop collects all; registry purges dead ProcessIds; broker drops their capabilities
Manual recovery rare; restart browser if PID table corrupted
Operator actions monitor defunct count + registry/live-PID divergence
Forensic artifacts exit statuses collected late; reap-loop iteration log
Long-term mitigation always reap in a while WNOHANG loop; never assume one SIGCHLD == one death; reconciliation sweep on a timer

F9 — Deadlock on synchronous IPC (browser ↔ child) — S2

Field Value
Symptoms a route hangs; no crash, no EOF; both ends blocked on send/recv; full socket buffers
Root cause synchronous request/response cycle (browser waits on child while child waits on browser); unbounded sync call into untrusted code; backpressure deadlock
Detection every synchronous IPC carries a deadline; watchdog per pending req_id (MsgKind request) expiring ⇒ declare hang
Auto-recovery on deadline expiry: treat the child as faulty → kill it (SbxError::Ipc), run F1 recovery; the browser never blocks unboundedly on a child
Manual recovery reload tab; redesign the offending call to async (Endpoint<T> async path)
Operator actions inspect which Interface method hung; check sync-call inventory
Forensic artifacts pending req_id + age; the Interface/RouteId; thread/stack of the waiter
Long-term mitigation prefer async Endpoint; forbid unbounded sync browser→renderer calls; bounded channels + timeouts everywhere on the untrusted side

F10 — Process-count / memory-pressure exhaustion — S3

Field Value
Symptoms spawn latency spikes; clone/zygote fork EAGAIN/ENOMEM; new navigations queue
Root cause too many distinct sites ⇒ too many processes (site isolation cost); per-process memory budgets exhausted; rlimit RLIMIT_NPROC
Detection process-count metric vs limit; memory-budget tracker; ProcessReusePolicy::LimitReached
Auto-recovery ProcessReusePolicy switches ProcessPerSiteInstance → ReuseExistingForSite within the same BrowsingInstance only (never relax cross-site isolation to save memory); evict least-recently-used reusable hosts under pressure
Manual recovery raise limits if host has headroom; otherwise accept reuse degradation
Operator actions tune process-count cap + per-process rlimits to host RAM; watch spawn-latency SLO
Forensic artifacts process count + memory-budget timeline; reuse decisions log
Long-term mitigation reuse policy that degrades availability, never isolation; zygote keeps spawn cheap; budget alarms before hard limits

27.3 Cross-cutting recovery rules

  • Renderer death ≠ browser death — every child failure (F1/F2/F6/F8/F9) is contained to its RouteIds; the browser (TCB) and sibling children continue. Sad tab is the user-visible surface.
  • Fail closed everywhere on the security path — F3/F4/F5 resolve toward less capability and more isolation: abort child, deny request, spawn separate process. Never the reverse.
  • Defense in depth holds under single-layer failure — F4 (broker over-grant) is still bounded by seccomp; a seccomp gap (would-be F2) is still bounded by the broker + namespaces. A real escape needs both layers to fail simultaneously on the same resource — the red-team suite tests exactly that conjunction.
  • No blind restart of security events — F2/F3/F4/F5 are never auto-healed by restarting; they require triage, because restarting an attacked/misconfigured child just replays the breach.

27.4 Debugging methodology (audit · red-team · policy-diff)

suspected breach
   │
   ├─ run `sbx attack`  ───────► any forbidden syscall/file/socket NOT denied?  ⇒ F2-gap or F3 or F4
   ├─ run `sbx policy --diff` ─► CapabilitySet widened vs golden?               ⇒ F4 over-grant
   ├─ run `sbx sites` + PSL hash► distinct sites sharing a ProcessId?           ⇒ F5 co-location
   ├─ run `sbx sandbox-test` ──► seal/handshake/seccomp setup fails on host?    ⇒ F3 (deployment)
   └─ review broker audit log ─► unexpected `Handle` grants / fd growth         ⇒ F4 / F7
Tool Catches Cadence
sbx attack (red-team harness) F2 gaps, F3, F4, F5 — proves forbidden ops are denied/killed every release + CI gate
sbx policy --diff (golden CapabilitySet) F4 silent widening every release
PSL version/hash check F5 stale-suffix co-location startup + auto-update alarm
sbx sandbox-test self-test F3 host/kernel gaps host pre-flight + startup gate
broker audit log F4, F7 continuous
fd-count / process-count / crash-rate / SIGSYS-rate metrics F1, F7, F8, F10, F2 continuous + alerting

Triage order: classify the exit first — SIGSYS ⇒ F2 (security), other signal ⇒ F1 (availability), no exit but hung ⇒ F9, EOF without reap ⇒ F6/F8. Then for any silent suspicion (no crash, no signal) run the red-team + policy-diff + PSL checks, since S0 breaches (F3-runtime-bypass, F4, F5) produce no runtime symptom and are detectable only by active assertion.

Operational Readiness

Frame: sbx is a long-running, multi-process desktop browser shell — a service-like supervisor (the browser process / TCB) over a fluctuating fleet of sandboxed children. Ops posture = security observability first: prove the trust boundary holds, catch a sandbox-apply failure or broker-deny spike before it becomes an escape, recover crashed renderers without user-visible loss. Single-host (one user's machine), no cluster — so "deployment" = shipping an auditable binary + data files, not orchestration.

28.1 Metrics to expose

Pull model: an in-process registry in the browser (TCB) only; children report counters over IPC (their self-reported numbers are untrusted hints, never security decisions). sbx exposes a local read-only endpoint (UDS, sbx metrics).

Metric Type Labels Target / alert
sbx_process_count gauge process_type per-type ceiling (renderer ≤ process_limit)
sbx_spawn_latency_seconds histogram process_type, via_zygote zygote p95 < 25 ms; direct p95 < 120 ms
sbx_process_rss_bytes gauge pid, process_type renderer p95 < 150 MB; alert > rlimit·0.9
sbx_crashes_total counter process_type, signal crash rate < 1 / renderer-hour
sbx_seccomp_kills_total counter process_type, syscall_nr expected 0 in prod; any > 0 = page
sbx_broker_requests_total counter op, process_type baseline per workload
sbx_broker_denies_total counter op, reason deny-rate < 0.1%; spike = page
sbx_ipc_messages_total counter route, kind —
sbx_ipc_bytes_total counter direction throughput floor (C-3/C-4)
sbx_ipc_latency_seconds histogram kind p50<50µs, p95<200µs, p99<1ms (small msg)
sbx_process_reuse_total counter policy (ProcessReusePolicy) —
sbx_sandbox_apply_total counter result (ok/fail), stage fail ⇒ fail-closed + page
sbx_restart_total counter process_type, policy backoff-exhausted = alert
  • Histograms: explicit buckets (spawn 5/10/25/50/100/250/500 ms; IPC 10/50/200/1000 µs) — quantiles over a sliding window, no server-side aggregation.
  • RSS scraped from /proc/<pid>/statm by the browser on a 1 s tick (children are PID-isolated; browser holds the real PIDs from clone/zygote bookkeeping).
  • Security counters (seccomp_kills, broker_denies, sandbox_apply{fail}) are the golden signals — everything else is capacity/perf.

28.2 Structured tracing across the trust boundary

  • Trace id propagation: a 128-bit trace_id + 64-bit span_id ride in MessageHeader (extension, alongside route/req_id) so a navigation → SiteInstance assignment → launch → broker OpenFile is one trace spanning the boundary.
  • Untrusted span data: child-emitted span attributes are quarantined — recorded under a untrusted. prefix, never used for control flow, size-capped, and dropped if malformed (a renderer must not poison the browser's trace buffer → DoS / log-injection).
  • Sampling: head-based; security-relevant spans (broker.deny, seccomp.kill, sandbox.apply) are always sampled regardless of rate.
  • sbx-cli introspection (live, read from the running browser over its control UDS):
    • sbx sites → the SiteId → ProcessId map, SiteInstance/BrowsingInstance tree, ProcessReusePolicy state per site.
    • sbx policy <pid> → the resolved CapabilitySet for a process + recent allow/deny tail.
    • sbx sandbox-test [--type renderer] → dry-run the Policy (namespaces, compiled SeccompFilter rule count, pivot_root target, dropped caps) and self-check it applies — used in CI and on-call triage.

28.3 Logging philosophy

  • Only the TCB logs. Children have no log sink path in their sandbox (no writable log FD, no syslog socket — connect is namespaced away + broker-gated). Anything a child wants logged it sends as an IPC message; the browser decides whether/how to record it (untrusted-tagged). Rationale: a compromised renderer must not forge or flood the security log.

  • Severity by class:

    Event Severity Notes
    seccomp-kill (SIGSYS / KILL_PROCESS) CRITICAL syscall_nr + pid + site; never expected in prod
    broker-deny (PolicyDenied) WARN→CRITICAL on spike op + path/host + caller CapabilitySet
    sandbox-apply-fail CRITICAL which stage (namespace/seccomp/pivot_root) + errno
    child crash (signal) ERROR links to Minidump id
    restart / sad-tab INFO RestartPolicy decision
    spawn / reuse / nav assignment DEBUG/INFO

  • Security events are append-only, structured (JSON), and include the trace id; logs are local files (no network sink — see §28.7). Never log brokered file contents or handle bytes — paths/metadata only.

28.4 Dashboard

Single local view (sbx ships a minimal TUI / --serve HTML over loopback), three panes:

+-- Process tree --------------+-- Per-process resource ----------+
| Browser  pid 1001  [TCB]     | pid   type      RSS   CPU%  caps |
|  +- Renderer pid 1042 a.com  | 1042  renderer  138M   12   3    |
|  +- Renderer pid 1051 b.org  | 1051  renderer  121M    4   3    |
|  +- GPU      pid 1010        | 1010  gpu       402M   20   7    |
|  +- Network  pid 1011        | 1011  network    66M    2   5    |
+------------------------------+----------------------------------+
+-- Security-event feed (CRITICAL/WARN, live tail) ---------------+
| 12:04:11 broker-deny  pid1042 OpenFile /etc/shadow  (no cap)    |
| 12:04:12 seccomp-kill pid1042 nr=101 ptrace  -> KILLED          |
+----------------------------------------------------------------+
  • Process tree mirrors ProcessHost; per-process pane joins gauges; the feed is the live broker-deny/seccomp-kill/sandbox-apply-fail stream. Color: red = security event, amber = restart/backoff, green = steady.

28.5 SLO-style targets

SLO Objective Indicator
Zero-escape 0 confirmed sandbox escapes / privilege escalations red-team harness green every CI run; seccomp_kills are kills, not bypasses
Blast-radius containment a compromised renderer gains ≤ its CapabilitySet; broker re-validates 100% of requests broker_denies for out-of-cap ops = 100% denied; no brokered FD outside cap
Spawn latency zygote-spawned renderer ready p95 < 25 ms, p99 < 50 ms sbx_spawn_latency_seconds
Crash recovery sad-tab shown < 200 ms after child death; reload-on-demand < 1 s crash-detect → signal latency
Availability browser process uptime ≫ child uptime; renderer death never kills browser sbx_crashes_total{process_type="browser"} == 0
IPC p99 small-message round-trip < 1 ms under fleet load sbx_ipc_latency_seconds
  • Error budget framing applies to crash rate and spawn latency (capacity SLOs). The security SLOs (zero-escape, containment) are hard gates, not budgeted — a single confirmed escape is a stop-ship/recall event, not a burned budget.

28.6 Incident response + runbooks

Symptom Likely cause Runbook
Renderer crash loop bad content / OOM / a deterministic SIGSEGV sbx crash-test-style triage: inspect the Minidump (signal, maps, last route); confirm RestartPolicy::Restart{max,window} backoff is engaging (not hot-looping); if max exhausted → leave sad tab, do not auto-restart; check sbx_process_rss_bytes for OOM.
Broker-deny spike over-tight CapabilitySet, mis-derived SiteId, or a genuine attack sbx policy <pid> → compare the resolved CapabilitySet against the denied BrokerOps. Legit feature gap → widen the per-ProcessType default profile (reviewed change). Anomalous (e.g. one renderer hammering OpenFile /etc/*) → treat as compromise, see "suspected escape".
sandbox-apply failures kernel/namespace/permission misconfig (no CONFIG_USER_NS, kernel.unprivileged_userns_clone=0, seccomp unavailable, bad rootfs) Fail closed: refuse to launch the child unsandboxed (never degrade), surface SbxError::Sandbox, page. Triage with sbx sandbox-test (reports which stage/errno). Fix host config or roll back; do not ship a "sandbox-disabled" flag.
Suspected escape / forbidden-syscall a seccomp-kill in prod, or out-of-cap broker access Forensic flow: pull the seccomp_kills log (syscall_nr, args, pid, site), the trace id, and the Minidump; correlate with broker_denies. Re-run the red-team harness (sbx attack) + a Disposition::Log-mode seccomp policy on a quarantined build to reproduce the syscall path. Treat the site/content as malicious until cleared.
Spawn latency regression zygote not warm / COW pages cold / falling back to direct exec check sbx_spawn_latency_seconds{via_zygote} split; confirm ForkServer alive; a use_zygote=false fallback path = investigate why the zygote is unavailable.
  • General principle: any ambiguity at the trust boundary resolves deny + alert, never allow.

28.7 Deployment

  • Artifact = one statically-linkable sbx binary (the TCB) + two auditable data files shipped beside it:
    • the public suffix list (PSL) — versioned, checksummed; site = scheme + eTLD+1 correctness depends on it (stale PSL → wrong isolation boundary). Update is a reviewed data bump, not a code change.
    • the seccomp policy / CapabilitySet profiles as declarative data (the SyscallPolicy DSL + per-ProcessType capability profiles) — reviewable in diff, so a security reviewer audits "what can a renderer do" without reading Rust.
  • No network log/telemetry sink by default (single-user desktop; privacy) — metrics/traces stay local, opt-in export only.
  • Reproducible build; the binary + PSL + policy files are signed/checksummed together so the deployed sandbox policy is provably the audited one.
  • macOS/Windows sandbox backends are PhaseDeferred — on those platforms sbx refuses to run renderers unsandboxed (fail closed), not "best effort".

28.8 Sandbox-requires-kernel-features (startup self-check)

The Linux sandbox is not portable across kernels/configs — sbx probes at startup, before launching any child, and fails closed if the TCB cannot confine children:

startup_check():
  require CLONE_NEWUSER usable (unprivileged user namespace)   # else: no namespacing
  require unprivileged_userns_clone == 1 (where the sysctl exists)
  require CLONE_NEWPID|NEWNET|NEWNS|NEWIPC|NEWUTS supported
  require seccomp(SECCOMP_SET_MODE_FILTER) + SECCOMP_RET_KILL_PROCESS
  require prctl(PR_SET_NO_NEW_PRIVS) succeeds
  require pivot_root target rootfs present + mountable
  -> any failure: log CRITICAL sandbox-apply-fail(stage), refuse renderer launch, SbxError::Sandbox
  • Probe result is exposed (sbx sandbox-test) and emitted as a startup metric (sbx_sandbox_apply_total{stage,result}) so a fleet of dev machines surfaces "kernel too old / userns disabled" before a user hits an unconfined-renderer condition.
  • Kernel feature gaps (e.g. KILL_PROCESS vs older KILL_THREAD, seccomp arg-filtering caveats) are detected and downgraded only toward stricter behavior — never toward allowing an unfiltered child.

Compatibility & Versioning

Defines what sbx promises across versions. Security-critical: a compatibility error must fail closed (deny / refuse to start), never silently weaken a sandbox or accept a mismatched peer.

29.1 Compatibility surfaces (what is promised vs. internal)

Surface Stability class Owner crate(s) Break =
IPC wire format (Message framing, MessageHeader) Versioned + handshake-negotiated; exact match required sbx-ipc minor bump if compatibly extended; refuse mismatched peer
Interface/Endpoint<T> contracts Per-interface version int; both ends must agree sbx-ipc + callers adding method = compatible; changing sig = break
Capability / CapabilitySet Security-sensitive; additive only sbx-core adding variant = minor; broadening grant = security review
Seccomp policy (per-ProcessType) Versioned data, reviewed sbx-sandbox any rule change = explicit version bump + tests
Public suffix list (PSL) Versioned data, dated snapshot sbx-site refresh = data version, never silent
Sandbox trait Portability seam; semver sbx-sandbox the macOS/Windows extension point
SbxError, public type names semver, #[non_exhaustive] sbx-core adding variant = minor
sbx CLI subcommands/flags semver-ish; additive sbx-cli removing flag = major
Internal: BPF builder internals, mmap layout, fork-server framing No guarantee all freely changeable

The IPC wire format + the Interface contracts are the primary compatibility surface: browser and child are separate processes that must agree byte-for-byte. Everything in-process follows ordinary Rust semver.

29.2 Crate semver

  • All 12 sbx-* crates versioned together (single workspace version) → no intra-workspace skew; one tag = one coherent set.
  • Public API = exported types/fns above; pub(crate)/private excluded. Enums crossing the API (SbxError, Capability, ProcessType, Disposition, ProcessReusePolicy) are #[non_exhaustive] → adding a variant is minor, callers keep a _ => arm.
  • Pre-1.0 (0.y.z): y = breaking, z = compatible. Post-1.0: standard semver.
  • cargo public-api / cargo semver-checks run in CI; an unexpected diff fails the build → no accidental breakage.

29.3 IPC wire format: versioned + negotiated at handshake

The single hardest rule: a mixed-version browser ↔ child is forbidden. Different code paths on either side of the trust boundary parsing the same bytes differently is itself an attack surface, so we do not support graceful cross-version IPC — we detect and refuse.

WIRE_VERSION: u16                 // bumped on ANY Message/header layout change
HelloMessage { wire_version, interface_versions: [(iface_id, u16)], build_hash }

Handshake (Phase 0/3, first message on every new MessagePipe):

child  -> browser : Hello{ wire_version=W_c, ifaces, build_hash }
browser           : if W_c != W_browser            -> Reject{IncompatibleWire}; child exits nonzero
                  : if any iface_id version mismatch -> Reject{IncompatibleInterface}
                  : else                            -> HelloAck{ assigned ProcessId, route 0 }
  • Mismatch → child killed, browser logs, no untrusted bytes parsed past Hello. Fail closed.
  • MessageHeader is fixed-layout (route: RouteId, kind: MsgKind, req_id: u64, num_handles: u16); a parser at wire W rejects any header it cannot fully validate (bad num_handles, length overflow) rather than guessing.
  • Forward-extension allowed within a WIRE_VERSION only via reserved/length-prefixed payload tails; the framing itself never changes without a bump.
  • In practice browser + children come from one binary / one build (zygote-forked or same image) → build_hash is expected identical; the version check is defense against deployment skew, not a feature.

29.4 Interface/Endpoint contract evolution

Change Compatible? Action
Add new Interface Yes new iface_id; old peers never bind it
Add method to interface Yes bump that iface version; unknown kind on old peer → error reply, not crash
Change/remove method signature No bump iface version; handshake rejects old peer
Reorder/retype MessageHeader No WIRE_VERSION bump

Each interface carries its own u16 version in Hello; the browser tolerates a child that supports a subset of optional interfaces but rejects a wire mismatch outright.

29.5 Capability set — security-sensitive evolution

Capability / CapabilitySet define the blast radius; changes are reviewed as security changes, not API changes:

Change Class Review
Add new Capability variant Additive standard; default profiles must not auto-grant it
Add field to existing variant Additive must default to the narrowest grant
Broaden an existing capability's meaning (wider glob/port range) Security review item sign-off; red-team (Phase 13) re-run
Change a ProcessType default profile Security review conformance test diff; deny-by-default preserved
Remove/narrow a capability Compatible (tighter) still tested

Invariant: a new capability never appears in an existing process's default CapabilitySet without explicit code + review. Renderer stays minimal by construction; broadening is opt-in and audited.

29.6 Seccomp policy + PSL = versioned data

  • Seccomp policy per ProcessType carries policy_version. Any SyscallRule/Disposition/arg-filter change → bump + a conformance test asserting the new allow/deny set (Phase 13). No silent edits: a diff with no version bump fails CI.
  • Adding a syscall to Allow = security review (widens kernel attack surface); moving to Errno/Kill = tightening, still tested.
  • PSL is a dated snapshot pinned in-repo; refresh = explicit data-version bump + regression test on site_for() golden cases. eTLD+1 derivation must stay deterministic for a given PSL version → site→process assignment is reproducible.
  • Both are embedded in the build; browser + children share the same policy/PSL version (same binary), removing cross-version drift.

29.7 OS / kernel compatibility + startup probe

Linux is primary. Minimum kernel surface:

Feature Min kernel Used by
seccomp-bpf (SECCOMP_SET_MODE_FILTER) 3.5 (3.17 for seccomp(2)) sbx-sandbox
unprivileged user namespaces 3.8 (+distro kernel.unprivileged_userns_clone=1) sbx-sandbox
pivot_root, pid/net/mnt/ipc/uts ns 3.8 sbx-sandbox
memfd_create 3.17 sbx-shm
memfd seals (F_SEAL_*) 3.17 sbx-shm
SECCOMP_RET_KILL_PROCESS 4.14 (else fall to KILL_THREAD) sbx-sandbox
no_new_privs (PR_SET_NO_NEW_PRIVS) 3.5 sbx-sandbox

Effective floor: Linux 4.14 + x86-64. Earlier kernels are unsupported.

Startup capability probe (browser, before spawning any child):

probe() -> Result<(), SbxError>:
  check kernel >= floor; user_namespaces creatable; seccomp filter installable;
  memfd_create + F_SEAL_WRITE succeed; AUDIT_ARCH matches expected

Graceful degradation = fail closed. If a required confinement primitive is missing, sbx refuses to launch a sandboxed child (SbxError::Sandbox) — it never spawns a renderer with a weaker-than-specified sandbox. There is no "sandbox-off" fallback for untrusted children. Optional niceties (e.g. KILL_PROCESS→KILL_THREAD) degrade only where the security property is preserved.

29.8 Portability seam + PhaseDeferred backends

  • Sandbox trait (fn apply(&self, policy: &Policy) -> Result<(), SbxError>) is the only portability seam. LinuxSandbox is the sole production impl.
  • macOS (Seatbelt) + Windows (job objects / restricted token / AppContainer) backends are PhaseDeferred stubs returning SbxError::Sandbox ("unimplemented") — they compile but refuse to confine, so no untrusted child runs un-sandboxed there. Adding them changes no existing Linux API.
  • Policy fields are Linux-shaped today; cross-platform fields, if added, are additive + ignored by LinuxSandbox.

29.9 MSRV policy

  • MSRV = Rust 1.78, edition 2021; recorded in workspace Cargo.toml (rust-version) and CI-pinned.
  • MSRV bumps are minor version events, called out in CHANGELOG, never silent. Tracks no newer toolchain feature without a bump.
  • Dependencies (nix/libc) pinned by minor; a dep raising its MSRV above ours is treated as a break to be vetted.

29.10 Compatibility invariants (summary)

  1. Browser and child must run the same WIRE_VERSION + interface versions — enforced at handshake; mismatch → child refused, fail closed.
  2. Any seccomp-policy / PSL change requires an explicit version bump + a conformance test — never silent.
  3. A new/broadened Capability is a reviewed security change; defaults never auto-widen.
  4. Missing kernel primitive → refuse to spawn the sandboxed child; never weaken the sandbox to proceed.
  5. Sandbox is the single portability seam; non-Linux backends fail closed until implemented.
  6. Public API breaks are gated by cargo semver-checks in CI; MSRV bumps are minor + announced.

Testing & Verification Strategy

The test suite is the durable proof of the section-7 invariants. Severity drives priority: every CRIT-TB invariant (INV-1,2,3,4,5,6,9,10) has a positive and a negative (red-team) test; zero sandbox escapes is the release gate (Phase 13). Tests are split by privilege: unit/property/fuzz/bench run on a standard GitHub Actions runner; anything that unshares namespaces or installs seccomp runs only on the privileged self-hosted Linux runner (sandbox-tests.yml, --privileged, unprivileged-userns enabled, no host seccomp masking ours).

Test taxonomy & invariant traceability

Class Crates / fixtures Runner Primary invariants Gate
Unit all sbx-* standard INV-2,4,6,8,9,12 per-PR
Integration (spawn→sandbox→broker→navigate) browser/launcher/renderer/broker/site privileged INV-1,3,4,8,10,11,13 per-PR
RED-TEAM / negative (sbx attack) renderer↦broker/sandbox privileged INV-1,2,4,8,10,13,14 release P0
Differential vs expected-policy table sandbox/broker privileged INV-2,4 per-PR
Property core/ipc/shm/site standard INV-6,9,12, site determinism per-PR
Fuzzing (cargo-fuzz) ipc/broker/site standard (nightly long) INV-3,9,12 nightly + 5-min CI smoke
Chaos / fault injection browser/launcher/crash/sandbox privileged INV-5,7,11 nightly
Benchmarks (criterion) ipc/shm/launcher/broker/site dedicated bench host C-1..C-12 regression per-PR (warn) / nightly (gate)

Unit tests (deterministic, in-process, no privilege)

  • IPC framing (sbx-ipc): encode→decode round-trips Message{header,payload}; header field widths (route:u32, kind, req_id:u64, num_handles:u16); rejects truncated header, payload-len > frame, num_handles mismatch, zero-length, max-size frame — asserts typed error, never panic/OOB (INV-9). Golden wire-byte vectors pin the format.
  • SCM_RIGHTS handle pass (sbx-ipc::Handle): socketpair in-process; send 1/2/N FDs, assert received FDs fstat-identical to originals; assert sender's Handle moved (compile-fail test via trybuild that a used Handle cannot be reused) and single ownership; num_handles vs ancillary-FD-count mismatch ⇒ reject + close received (INV-6). Leak check: /proc/self/fd count returns to baseline.
  • Seccomp compilation (sbx-sandbox::SeccompFilter): SyscallRule[] → cBPF emits the arch-check first instruction (AUDIT_ARCH_* guard, INV-2); golden BPF program bytes; rule count ≤ jump-offset limit; an empty/duplicate-nr ruleset is a compile-time panic (build-invariant). Each disposition verified by a child that installs a 1-rule filter and triggers the syscall: Allow→succeeds; Errno(EPERM)→syscall returns -EPERM, process lives; Trap→SIGSYS delivered, handler fires; KillProcess→waitpid reports WTERMSIG==SIGSYS/kill; Log→audit logged, allowed. (privileged subset for install).
  • Namespace setup (sbx-sandbox::Namespaces): clone-flag bitset USER|PID|NET|MNT|IPC|UTS correct; post-apply child self-reports (via sbx sandbox-test): sees self as pid 1, no sibling PIDs, loopback down / no interfaces, rootfs read-only, cap bounding set empty, no_new_privs==1 (INV-1, C-SEC-4 / §4.4). userns-disabled host ⇒ unshare(NEWUSER) EPERM ⇒ SbxError::Sandbox, fail-closed (INV-5), surfaced as "userns disabled".
  • PSL / eTLD+1 derivation (sbx-site): table-driven over public-suffix edge cases — multi-label suffixes (a.b.co.uk→b.co.uk), wildcard rules, exception rules, IP-literal hosts, file:/opaque/about: schemes (each → distinct opaque SiteId), punycode/IDN, trailing dot, port-only differences (INV-8 input correctness).
  • Capability validation (sbx-broker::PolicyEngine): for each BrokerOp × CapabilitySet, assert Allow/Denied; OpenFile path-glob match vs traversal (../, symlink-looking, NUL); ConnectSocket host:port within grant; unlisted ⇒ Denied (deny-by-default, INV-4, C-SEC-3); revoked cap ⇒ subsequent op denied.
  • Site mapping (sbx-site::site_for + map): URL→SiteId deterministic & idempotent; ProcessReusePolicy state machine ProcessPerSiteInstance→ReuseExistingForSite→LimitReached at the C-13/C-14 thresholds; same-BrowsingInstance script-related SiteInstances coalesce; distinct SiteIds in different BrowsingInstances never merge (INV-8, C-15).

Integration tests (end-to-end, privileged runner)

Drive real children through the full lifecycle; assert observable end state, not internals.

  • spawn → sandbox → broker → navigate (the headline flow): BrowserProcess boots → SiteIsolationPolicy::site_for(url) → RenderProcessHost assigned → ProcessLauncher::launch (zygote + direct paths both exercised) → child seals sandbox (INV-1,10) → handshake completes → renderer issues BrokerRequest::OpenFile("/sandbox/allowed.txt") → broker validates vs CapabilitySet → returns FD over SCM_RIGHTS → renderer reads bytes. Assert: bytes correct, child FD set = {channel, granted FD, mapped SharedBuffer} only (INV-13), ProcessHost registry ⇔ the live PID (INV-11).
  • Shared-memory path (sbx ipc-ping --pass-fd): browser sends FD + 64 MiB sealed SharedBuffer; child mmaps, CRC-verifies parent's write, echoes via ShmRing; exit 0 iff CRCs match and fd-table accounting clean (INV-6, seals enforced).
  • Two-layer (INV-14) co-test: renderer reads /sandbox/allowed.txt via broker → OK; requests /etc/shadow → Denied; independently, renderer's own openat("/etc/shadow") → blocked by seccomp. Proves both layers active simultaneously.
  • Site assignment: N navigations across distinct/same sites; assert no process's host set ever mixes two SiteIds (INV-8); cross-BrowsingInstance same-site stays separate (C-15).
  • Teardown: Shutdown→grace→SIGTERM→SIGKILL→reap leaves no orphan/zombie; registry empties (INV-11).

RED-TEAM / negative security suite (sbx attack) — the most important class

From inside a fully-sealed renderer (real namespaces + seccomp baseline), attempt every forbidden action; the harness exit-0 means all were denied/killed. CI fails the build on any single escape (P0). Documented attack matrix A1–A8:

ID Attack Expected outcome Layer INV
A1 Forbidden syscall (ptrace, mount, kexec_load, bpf, clone(NEWUSER), socket) SIGSYS kill / Errno per policy seccomp INV-2,14
A2 x32 / ia32 ABI bypass of a blocked syscall killed by arch check seccomp INV-2
A3 open/openat host path directly EPERM/kill (no path syscall) seccomp + empty rootfs INV-1,13
A4 BrokerRequest::OpenFile out-of-glob (/etc/shadow) Denied broker INV-4
A5 BrokerRequest::ConnectSocket out-of-grant host/port Denied broker INV-4
A6 Smuggle a path/dir FD then openat-widen no such FD present; openat blocked launcher sweep + seccomp INV-13
A7 Forge SCM_RIGHTS / over-claim num_handles frame rejected, FDs closed ipc decoder INV-6,9
A8 Escape namespace (re-unshare, setns, write /proc/self/uid_map) denied; pivot_root has no host / namespaces + seccomp INV-1

Each A* has a positive twin (the allowed equivalent succeeds) so a regression that over-blocks is also caught. A renderer that repeatedly violates is killed (→ INV-7 path), not merely errored.

Differential testing vs expected-policy table

  • Canonical fixture policy/expected_renderer.toml lists every syscall nr → expected Disposition. Test enumerates the full syscall table; for each, runs the probe in a sealed child and asserts actual disposition == expected (INV-2). Drift (new kernel syscall unlisted) ⇒ failure forcing explicit triage (deny-by-default means unlisted→kill, so a missing row is safe-but-flagged).
  • Broker analog: policy/expected_broker.csv of (ProcessType, BrokerOp) → Allow|Deny; diffed against PolicyEngine output (INV-4). sbx policy --diff reproduces any mismatch locally.

Property-based tests (proptest)

  • IPC round-trip: ∀ Message m (arbitrary header+payload, 0..=8 handles): decode(encode(m)) == m; arbitrary byte slices into the decoder never panic/OOB (INV-9).
  • Handle ownership: ∀ FD set: send→recv preserves identity, count conserved, no double-close, baseline fd count restored (INV-6).
  • Site mapping determinism: ∀ url: site_for is pure & idempotent; same SiteId ⇒ same process under fixed policy (INV-8).
  • req_id: ∀ interleaving: ids unique per pipe; responses to unknown/closed req_id dropped, never dispatched (INV-12).

Fuzzing (cargo-fuzz / libFuzzer) — the TCB must never crash on malformed input

Target Input Asserts INV
fuzz_ipc_message arbitrary bytes → wire decoder no panic/OOB/UB; typed error or valid Message INV-9,3
fuzz_framing fragmented/concatenated frame streams no desync, bounded buffer, no infinite loop INV-9
fuzz_broker_request arbitrary BrokerRequest bytes → PolicyEngine never Allow beyond cap; never panic INV-3,4
fuzz_psl arbitrary host strings → site_for always terminates, returns a SiteId, no panic INV-8

#![forbid(unsafe_code)] in all decoders. Sanitizers (ASan/UBSan) on in CI. CI budget: 5-min/target smoke per PR seeded from a growing corpus; nightly 1-hour/target; any new crash ⇒ corpus minimized → regression unit test added before fix.

Chaos / fault injection (privileged, nightly)

  • Random child kill: SIGKILL renderers/GPU/utility at random intervals under load; assert browser + siblings survive, CrashHandler fires sad-tab within C-10 (< 200 ms p50, < 500 ms ceiling), RestartPolicy honored, registry reconciled (INV-7,11). Kill the browser → children are reaped (no orphans), not left running unsupervised.
  • Sandbox-apply failure injection: force unshare/seccomp/pivot_root/no_new_privs to fail in the child; assert child abort()s before any untrusted recv and browser sees SpawnFailed — never a child running with a partial/weaker sandbox (fail-closed, INV-5). Never a silent downgrade.
  • IPC disconnect / partial writes: abrupt pipe close, half-sent frame, FD-exhaustion (RLIMIT_NOFILE hit, C-16) ⇒ graceful SpawnFailed/disconnect handling, browser never aborts.

Benchmarks & regression gates (criterion, dedicated bench host)

Gate against the locked §4.2 targets; ≥1000 samples; nightly compares vs stored baseline, >10% p99 regression fails.

Bench Target / ceiling (C-#)
Renderer spawn — warm zygote p50 < 10 ms / p99 < 30 ms (C-1)
Renderer spawn — cold clone+exec < 60 ms / < 100 ms (C-2)
Sandbox apply < 5 ms / < 10 ms (C-3)
IPC round-trip ≤256 B p50 < 50 µs / p99 < 200 µs (C-4)
IPC throughput, 1 pipe > 200k msg/s (C-5)
ShmRing bulk > 1 GB/s (C-6)
SCM_RIGHTS round-trip < 100 µs / < 300 µs (C-7)
Broker validate+serve p50 < 100 µs / p99 < 500 µs (C-9)
site_for (PSL) < 20 µs / < 100 µs (C-12)
Per-renderer idle RSS < 30 MB / < 50 MB (C-8)

Coverage, CI gating & methodology

  • Coverage (cargo-llvm-cov): line ≥ 85% workspace, branch ≥ 80%; sbx-sandbox + sbx-broker (the TCB enforcement points) ≥ 95%; every Disposition arm and every Capability variant must be hit. Coverage drop fails CI.
  • Privileged CI: sandbox-tests.yml on a self-hosted --privileged Linux runner (kernel ≥ 5.x, unprivileged_userns_clone=1, host not masking our seccomp) runs the namespace/seccomp/integration/chaos/RED-TEAM suites; sbx attack exit 0 (A1–A8 all denied/killed) is the security "done" gate. Unit/property/fuzz-smoke/bench-warn run on standard runners. macOS/Windows backends PhaseDeferred — their tests assert the stub returns PhaseDeferred.
  • Methodology (per §7.5): any CRIT-TB failure ⇒ P0, freeze + snapshot /proc/<pid>/{status,maps,mountinfo,fd} + seccomp mode + Minidump; reproduce via the matching sbx subcommand (sandbox-test, ipc-ping --raw, policy --diff, crash-test, attack); confirm the other layer still holds (INV-14); add the regression to the conformance/fuzz/RED-TEAM suite before fixing — the suite is the proof the invariant is restored.

Build & Release Engineering

The TCB sits astride a hostile trust boundary; build hygiene is part of the threat model. Every artifact reproducible, every dep vetted, every release signed + SBOM-stamped. Untrusted input never reaches an unaudited line.

31.1 Reproducible builds

Control Mechanism
Toolchain pin rust-toolchain.toml (committed): channel = "1.78.0" (== MSRV), components = ["clippy","rustfmt"], targets = ["x86_64-unknown-linux-gnu","aarch64-unknown-linux-gnu"]. No stable floating channel in release builds.
Dep pin Cargo.lock committed (workspace is a binary-bearing repo, not a lib-only one) → bit-identical dep graph.
Determinism flags RUSTFLAGS="--remap-path-prefix=$PWD=/build -C debuginfo=1"; SOURCE_DATE_EPOCH from commit time; CARGO_INCREMENTAL=0; vendored deps (cargo vendor) for the release runner → no network at build.
Verify release.yml builds twice on disjoint runners, sha256sum the two sbx binaries → MUST match; mismatch fails the job.

Non-determinism sources eliminated: build paths (remapped), timestamps (SOURCE_DATE_EPOCH), proc-macro env reads (audited), HashMap iteration in codegen (none in build scripts).

31.2 Build + test matrix

Two runner classes — privilege is the axis, because sandbox tests need real kernel features:

Job Runner Toolchain Scope
check-unpriv unprivileged (default GH runner) stable + 1.78 (MSRV) build all 12 crates; unit tests for sbx-core/ipc/shm/site/crash; doctests
sandbox-tests privileged (--privileged, CAP_SYS_ADMIN, kernel ≥5.10, self-hosted/KVM) stable sbx-sandbox + sbx-zygote + sbx-launcher + sbx-broker integration: namespace creation, pivot_root, seccomp install, fork-server, brokered-FD round-trip
fuzz unprivileged nightly (cargo-fuzz needs nightly) libFuzzer targets: fuzz_message_parse, fuzz_seccomp_compile, fuzz_psl_lookup, fuzz_broker_request — 15 min/target on PR, 6 h nightly
bench privileged, pinned cores (isolcpus), perf-governor stable criterion: spawn latency, IPC throughput, broker req/s, fork-from-zygote — gate vs Phase 13 targets, fail on >10% regression
redteam privileged stable sbx attack malicious-renderer harness: forbidden syscall/file/socket attempts MUST be Errno/KILL'd; assert 0 escapes. The security "done" gate (Phase 13).

MSRV (1.78) failure breaks CI — MSRV is a contract, not advisory. Privileged jobs gated on a documented kernel-feature probe (§31.7); skip-with-loud-warning never silently passes.

31.3 Lint, format, supply-chain gates

fmt        cargo fmt --all -- --check                      # zero drift
clippy     cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings
           + -D clippy::unwrap_used -D clippy::expect_used -D clippy::panic   # TCB: no panic on untrusted input paths
deny       cargo deny check advisories bans licenses sources   # RUSTSEC, dup/banned crates, license allowlist, registry allowlist
audit      cargo audit  (belt-and-suspenders w/ deny advisories)

cargo-deny is load-bearing for the TCB: deps that touch the trust boundary (nix, libc) are version-pinned + reviewed; [bans] multiple-versions = "deny" (no duplicate-version supply-chain ambiguity); license allowlist = MIT/Apache-2.0/BSD only; [sources] = crates.io only (no git deps in release). New dep PR requires a human supply-chain review label.

31.4 SBOM + provenance

  • cargo-auditable compiles the dep graph into the sbx binary → cargo audit bin sbx recovers the SBOM post-hoc from any deployed artifact.
  • CycloneDX SBOM (cargo cyclonedx) emitted as a release asset alongside each binary.
  • Provenance: GitHub OIDC → SLSA build-provenance attestation per artifact; cosign signs binary + SBOM + attestation; release notes embed commit SHA + toolchain version + SBOM digest.

31.5 Versioned data artifacts (policy + PSL)

Two data inputs are security-critical and reviewed like code, not vendored blind:

Artifact Path Versioning CI gate
Baseline seccomp policy crates/sbx-sandbox/policy/renderer.policy (+ gpu/network/utility) semver policy_version field; pinned in-repo policy-diff check: any change to an Allow/arg_filter requires a SECURITY-REVIEW: trailer + re-run of redteam; widening the allowlist without it fails CI
Public-suffix list crates/sbx-site/data/public_suffix_list.dat pinned by upstream commit hash + fetch date in a PSL_VERSION const; never auto-updated hash check vs committed .sha256; staleness warning >90 days; eTLD+1 conformance vectors re-run on bump

Rationale: a silent seccomp-allow widening or a stale/poisoned PSL = real privilege/site-isolation regressions. Both are reviewed diffs, both have conformance suites that re-run on change.

31.6 Publication order + release artifacts

crates.io publish respects the dependency DAG (a crate publishes only after its deps):

1. sbx-core
2. sbx-ipc        sbx-shm          (parallel; both depend only on core)
3. sbx-sandbox    (core)
4. sbx-zygote     sbx-broker  sbx-launcher   (sandbox/ipc/core)
5. sbx-site       (core)
6. sbx-browser    sbx-renderer  sbx-crash
7. sbx-cli        (all)
  • Semver stamping: cargo-release + cargo-workspaces keep one synchronized workspace version; CHANGELOG-driven; tag vX.Y.Z triggers release.yml.
  • sbx binary artifacts: x86_64-unknown-linux-gnu + aarch64-unknown-linux-gnu, cosign-signed, with .sha256 + SBOM + SLSA attestation. macOS (Seatbelt) / Windows (job objects / AppContainer) backends = PhaseDeferred → no release artifacts; CI builds them as compile-only stubs.
  • Breaking IPC wire-format / MessageHeader / Capability enum changes → minor-or-major bump of sbx-ipc/sbx-core; mixed-version browser↔child is unsupported (single-version handshake check at startup).

31.7 Workflow topology + kernel requirements

.github/workflows/
  ci.yml            fmt, clippy, deny, audit, check-unpriv, MSRV   (every push/PR)
  sandbox-tests.yml privileged: namespaces/pivot_root/seccomp/zygote/broker  (push/PR, self-hosted)
  fuzz.yml          cargo-fuzz targets (PR: 15min; nightly: 6h)
  bench.yml         criterion regression gate vs Phase-13 targets  (nightly + on-demand)
  release.yml       reproducible double-build, publish DAG, sign, SBOM, attest   (on tag)

Kernel-feature requirements (documented in docs/KERNEL_REQUIREMENTS.md; probed at CI start + at sbx runtime startup):

Feature Min man-page / why
Unprivileged user namespaces 3.8; kernel.unprivileged_userns_clone=1 (Debian) user_namespaces(7) — enables all other ns without root
seccomp-bpf + SECCOMP_RET_KILL_PROCESS 4.14 seccomp(2) — syscall filter + process-kill disposition
pivot_root in a new mnt-ns 3.8 pivot_root(2) — minimal rootfs confinement
memfd_create + F_SEAL_* 3.17 memfd_create(2) — sealed SharedBuffer
PR_SET_NO_NEW_PRIVS 3.5 prctl(2) — precondition for unprivileged seccomp install

Probe fails → privileged jobs hard-error (never silent-pass); sbx refuses to launch children with SbxError::Sandbox rather than run a renderer unconfined.

Performance Engineering

Performance is a first-class security property here: a slow LinuxSandbox::apply, a synchronous broker round-trip, or a costly cold-exec spawn pushes engineers toward weaker isolation (reuse processes, skip site isolation, batch trust). Every target below is the §4 number; this section is how we measure and defend it. Perf budgets live in the TCB hot path (IPC router, broker, launcher) — regressions there are CI-gating.

32.1 §4 Targets (restated, authoritative)

ID Metric Target (p50) Target (p99) Crate / path
M1 Renderer spawn — cold exec (no zygote) ≤ 35 ms ≤ 80 ms sbx-launcher direct clone/exec
M2 Renderer spawn — zygote fork ≤ 8 ms ≤ 20 ms sbx-zygote ForkServer
M3 IPC round-trip, small msg (≤256 B, no handles) ≤ 25 µs ≤ 120 µs sbx-ipc MessagePipe
M4 IPC small-message throughput, 1 pipe ≥ 150 k msg/s — sbx-ipc
M5 sbx-shm bulk throughput (ShmRing, 4 MiB) ≥ 8 GiB/s — sbx-shm zero-copy
M6 Sandbox::apply total (ns+seccomp+pivot_root+caps) ≤ 3 ms ≤ 10 ms sbx-sandbox LinuxSandbox
M7 seccomp filter eval, per syscall ≤ 1.0 µs — SeccompFilter cBPF
M8 Broker request latency (OpenFile, validated+SCM_RIGHTS) ≤ 60 µs ≤ 300 µs sbx-broker PolicyEngine
M9 RSS per idle renderer (post-sandbox, post-fork) ≤ 12 MiB — sbx-renderer
M10 COW savings vs cold exec (shared RO pages) ≥ 40% of idle RSS — sbx-zygote
M11 Spawn p99 under load (32 concurrent forks) ≤ 40 ms — sbx-launcher + ForkServer

All numbers: x86_64, Linux ≥5.10, release build (opt-level=3, lto="thin", codegen-units=1), warm page cache, perf governor. Reported in the bench README with kernel + CPU model.

32.2 Benchmark Harness Strategy

  • criterion for micro: IPC round-trip, throughput, seccomp eval, serialization of Message, eTLD+1 lookup in SiteIsolationPolicy::site_for. Sampling mode, ≥100 samples, outlier-tolerant; statistical regression detection (criterion::Criterion::significance_level).
  • Custom harnesses for cross-process work criterion can't model (it can't fork-and-measure cleanly): bench/ binaries driven by sbx bench — spawn, COW, broker, sustained load.
  • One fixture rule: a bench either runs entirely in-process (criterion) or spans the trust boundary (custom). Never mix — the fork/exec/sandbox cost dominates and corrupts criterion's statistics.
sbx bench spawn   --mode=cold|zygote --n=1000 --concurrency=1|32   -> latency histogram (HdrHistogram)
sbx bench ipc     --size=256 --rtt | --throughput --duration=10s   -> µs / msg-per-s
sbx bench shm     --bytes=4Mi --iters=10000                        -> GiB/s (ShmRing vs Message-copy)
sbx bench sandbox --component=ns|seccomp|pivot|caps|all            -> per-stage ns breakdown
sbx bench broker  --op=open|connect --n=100000                     -> latency histogram
sbx bench cow     --renderers=50                                   -> Pss/Rss/Shared from smaps
  • Latency: clock_gettime(CLOCK_MONOTONIC_RAW) around the measured op; record into HdrHistogram → emit p50/p90/p99/p999/max. Never report only the mean (spawn + GC-free fork are right-skewed; the tail is the SLO).
  • Spawn timing boundaries — measure the full path so we don't under-count: t0 = launcher issues ForkRequest (or clone); t1 = child completes handshake hello over MessagePipe. Includes sandbox sealing. A "spawn" that excludes Sandbox::apply is a lie.

32.3 Spawn: Cold Exec vs Zygote Fork (the COW win)

Two paths, both via ProcessLauncher::launch(LaunchOptions{ use_zygote }):

Path Steps Cost driver
Cold exec (use_zygote=false) clone(namespaces) → execve(sbx-renderer) → dynamic link + relocate → Sandbox::apply → handshake execve + ld.so relocation of the renderer binary + libs; ~25–35 ms
Zygote fork (use_zygote=true) ForkServer recv ForkRequest → fork() from warmed Zygote → seal/tighten seccomp → handshake no execve, no re-link; pages already resident + shared COW; ~5–8 ms

The zygote win is two-fold: (1) latency — skip execve/relocation (the dominant cost); (2) memory — the renderer binary's .text/.rodata, libc, and pre-initialized runtime pages are mapped once in the Zygote and shared copy-on-write across every forked child.

Zygote process (warmed): all RO code/data pages resident, faulted in once.
   fork() ───► child shares every page COW (refcount++, no copy until write).
   Child writes only its small private heap/stack ► those pages copied; RO code never copied.
Result: N renderers cost ~ Zygote_RSS + N * (private_dirty) , not N * full_RSS.
  • Verifying COW (not just trusting it): parse /proc/<pid>/smaps per renderer. Report Rss, Pss, Shared_Clean, Private_Dirty. Acceptance: across 50 zygote-forked renderers, sum(Pss) ≤ 0.6 * sum(Rss_if_independent) → ≥40% saving. Shared_Clean of the renderer .text segment must be ≥ (N-1)/N of its size (proves one physical copy).
  • Pitfall: anything that writes a COW page early (relocation, a global ctor, lazy-init of a static) breaks sharing → it shows up as Private_Dirty instead of Shared_Clean. Track Private_Dirty as a regression metric; a jump means we accidentally dirtied warm pages. Build with -z relro -z now so GOT/PLT is dirtied once in the Zygote (before fork), not per-child.
  • Pitfall: madvise(MADV_DONTFORK) on a buffer the renderer needs, or KSM, can confuse the smaps accounting — disable KSM in the bench env for reproducible numbers.

32.4 IPC Hot Path

MessagePipe round-trip and throughput are the steady-state cost (every navigation, every broker call, every frame route over RouteId). Hot path = Message framing → sendmsg → recvmsg → deframe.

  • Round-trip (256 B, no handles): target p50 ≤25 µs. Cost = 2× syscall + 2× copy + framing. Optimize: pre-sized payload Vec<u8> (no realloc), fixed MessageHeader encode (no varint for the hot fields), recvmsg into a reused buffer.
  • Throughput: ≥150 k msg/s on one pipe. Batching: coalesce multiple small Messages into one sendmsg iovec when the sender queue is non-empty (Nagle-style, bounded by a 1-iteration drain — never delay for batching, only batch what's already queued).
  • Avoid synchronous round-trips: Interface/Endpoint<T> supports async (req_id correlation). Hot rule: a renderer must not block on a broker round-trip in a tight loop. Pipeline N requests then await — turns N×RTT into ~1×RTT + N×service. Measured by sbx bench broker --pipeline.
  • Handle-passing cost: SCM_RIGHTS makes sendmsg/recvmsg materially more expensive (kernel fd install + cmsg). Bench --with-handle separately; expect 2–4× the no-handle RTT. → never pass a handle when a value would do; never pass a handle per-frame if it can be passed once.
flamegraph (perf record -g) on the IPC bench → expect, in order of self-time:
  sendmsg/recvmsg (syscall+copy_to/from_user) >> Message ser/de >> route dispatch.
If ser/de outweighs syscall: framing is too heavy (allocations, varint, reflection) — fix it.

32.5 Shared Memory: Zero-Copy vs Message-Copy

Large transfers (≥ a page) MUST use sbx-shm, not the Message payload. Crossover ≈ 16 KiB: below it, the Message copy beats memfd_create + mmap + SCM_RIGHTS setup; above it, ShmRing wins decisively.

Transfer 256 B 16 KiB 4 MiB
Message payload (copy via socket) best tie bad (≥2 copies, socket buf pressure)
SharedBuffer + ShmRing (zero-copy) overhead-bound tie best, ≥8 GiB/s
  • Zero-copy = producer writes into the Mapping (rw), consumer reads from its Mapping (ro), ShmRing head/tail indices coordinate (SPSC). The only socket traffic is a tiny Message carrying the HandleId + ring notification. Seal the buffer (F_SEAL_SHRINK|F_SEAL_GROW|F_SEAL_WRITE for ro side) so the receiver can trust the size — security AND perf: no re-validation, no defensive copy.
  • Pitfall: false sharing of the ring head/tail counters → put them on separate cache lines (64 B pad). Otherwise SPSC throughput collapses under contention.
  • Bench sbx bench shm reports both paths so the 16 KiB crossover is re-verified per platform (it moves with cache/socket-buffer sizes).

32.6 Sandbox Apply Cost

Sandbox::apply(&Policy) is on the spawn critical path; budget ≤3 ms p50. Per-stage breakdown (sbx bench sandbox --component=…):

Stage Cost Notes
unshare/clone namespaces (user→pid/net/mnt/ipc/uts) ~0.5–1.5 ms user-ns + uid/gid map write dominates
pivot_root + remount RO + unmount old root ~0.3–0.8 ms minimal rootfs; fewer mounts = faster
rlimits + PR_SET_NO_NEW_PRIVS + cap drop < 50 µs cheap prctls
SeccompFilter install (compile + seccomp(SECCOMP_SET_MODE_FILTER)) ~0.1–0.5 ms program length-dependent
  • Seccomp install cost ∝ program length. Keep the cBPF program short. The zygote amortizes this: the base policy compiles once in the Zygote; each forked child only tightens (installs a small delta) → per-renderer seccomp cost is a fraction of a full compile.
  • Compile the SeccompFilter cBPF once, cache the sock_filter[]; never recompile per spawn.

32.7 Seccomp Filter Eval Cost (runtime, not install)

Every syscall the renderer makes runs the cBPF program → per-syscall overhead is paid forever. Target ≤1.0 µs/syscall.

  • cBPF is a linear scan of sock_filter instructions (no jump table). Cost ≈ O(instructions examined). Therefore: order rules by syscall frequency — read/write/recvmsg/sendmsg/futex/mmap first; rare/blocked syscalls last. Frequency profile comes from §32.8 strace.
  • The mandatory arch check is the first instruction (security: blocks x32/ia32 ABI bypass) — it's also cheap and short-circuits foreign-arch traffic.
  • Use seccomp_data.nr range/equality jumps efficiently; collapse contiguous allow-ranges. Avoid per-syscall ArgFilter unless required (arg loads + compares add instructions to the hot path). Apply ArgFilter only to the handful of syscalls that need it (e.g., ioctl request, clone flags).
